While the Rest of Hell's Missing, Alastor's Pissing (Hazbin Hotel Fanfiction) - Page 2

Author: secretomoact

*000*2026-03-16 12:44

The hotel was peaceful for once. No bickering from Husk, no explosions from Angel, no frantic scribbling from Charlie as she drafted yet another optimistic plan. Just an uneasy stillness broken only by the hum of neon outside and the distant rumble of Hell’s constant, low-grade chaos.

Alastor stood in the lobby with his hands clasped politely behind his back. His grin was in place, but there was a tension behind it that hadn’t been there earlier that morning. A stiffness. A subtle bounce to his heel each time he shifted his weight. If anyone had looked closely, they may have noticed the slightest flicker of discomfort ghosting over his usually unreadable expression.

For the past couple days, ever since that detestable meeting, Alastor had been having an even more difficult time ‘excusing himself’ than usual. Just the idea of someone spotting him wandering off, thinking that MAYBE he had to relieve himself had become much, much more nerve-racking than before.

Vox had come… So close to uncovering Alastor’s little… Urinary hesitancy issue. And, he knew Vox would never, EVER allow him to live it down.

Keeping this problem a secret from his nemesis had, infuriatingly, become one of Alastor’s top priorities. It was frustrating to have to expend any of his mental energy on this of all things, but he’d made plans. Lists of preventative measures to ensure Vox would never come that close to discovering it again.

First, he would of course always relieve himself before going anywhere he knew Vox would be present. This would make it highly unlikely that his problem would rear its ugly head in front of him again. And, it would be easy for Alastor to remember; He so rarely left his room without using the facilities first anyway, eventually he hoped to get the number of times he forgot cut down to zero.

Second, just to be safe, he would ensure he knew where the nearest private restroom was. This way, if he did happen to end up… Full, he’d know precisely where to go to have the best chance of success. No wandering around while his static and shadow fought to give away his predicament. No relying on someone like Rosie to assist him. Nothing unusual to draw attention to him and make Vox start pondering.

Third, in the highly unlikely— But nevertheless terrible— Scenario that he found himself utterly desperate in front of Vox, with no private facilities within a reasonable distance, and unable to shake off the infuriating demon’s presence, Alastor would…

He’d…

Well… THAT plan was still a work in progress. All he knew was that he would STILL not allow Vox to uncover anything. He would KEEP this hidden. Somehow.

Oh, but what were the odds that he’d EVER find himself at Vox’s mercy like that, anyway? And for long enough for his bladder to get too full to handle?

The final thing he had decided was that NO ONE else in Hell could ever find out now. The more sinners knew, the more likely it was that someone would let something slip to Vox. For example, he knew that Angel was around the Vees often enough, and his mouth was larger than his brain. He may not even say anything intentionally, but with Angel’s tendency to babble, the news could reach Vox’s airwaves by accident.

He was anxious ENOUGH having to worry about Angel mentioning it, he couldn’t deal with having even more potential information leakers.

Fuck, wasn’t there a better term for that? One that DIDN’T mention ‘leaks’? Alastor’s thighs pressed together, glancing around the lobby to make certain no one noticed.

A cheerful clap echoed through the room. “Okay, team!” Charlie called, bouncing in place with the eager enthusiasm of someone who had consumed far too much coffee in one sitting.

Just… Not in the same way Alastor had.

“Today’s rehabilitation activity is going to be a trust exercise!” Charlie announced.

Angel Dust groaned dramatically from the couch. “Princess, last time you said ‘trust exercise,’ Husk fell off a ladder.”

“It was Al’s fault for letting go!” Husk shot back without looking up from his cards.

“It was called a trust FALL,” Alastor said.

Charlie pressed her palms together with forced patience. “No one will get hurt this time, I promise. Vaggie and I planned it carefully.”

Vaggie stood beside her, arms crossed but not entirely hostile. “We decided that everyone is going to pair up. One person will be blindfolded, and their partner will guide them through the hotel without letting them trip, fall, or run into anything.”

A collective groan rippled through the room.

Charlie beamed undeterred. “And I already assigned pairs! Angel with Husk. Niffty with Vaggie. And…” She paused with the dramatic timing of someone who had clearly rehearsed this. “…Alastor with me!”

Alastor’s smile froze. Of course, he’d be expected to participate. Internally, he cursed whatever part of his physiology necessitated mundane annoyances. He had been planning to excuse himself at the earliest opportunity— As soon as he could do so discreetly, of course. But now, with Charlie calling his name and everyone turning towards him expectantly, that plan evaporated like mist.

He offered a crisp chuckle. “Well! Isn’t that delightful?”

Charlie smiled brightly. “I thought it might be nice for us to partner up. You know… Build trust.”

“A charming idea,” he replied, though the bounce in his step grew more pronounced.

Vaggie handed out blindfolds. “Charlie, you’re up first. Alastor, you guide.”

Charlie tied the cloth over her eyes with determination and an air of unshakable confidence. “Ready!”

Alastor cleared his throat— A small, strained sound unusual for someone always so terrifyingly composed. “Very well, my dear. Do stay close. I wouldn’t want you to… Wander.”

He placed one hand lightly on Charlie’s shoulder. His other hovered awkwardly in front of him, as if unsure what to do. His composure remained pristine, but beneath it, his nerves felt like a swarm of bees trapped in a jar. He shifted his weight again— Too subtly for most onlookers, but not unnoticed by Husk, who squinted over his cards.

“You look stiff,” Husk grumbled.

Angel Dust draped himself over Husk, “Thank you for noticing, daddy.”

Husk groaned and shoved him aside.

Alastor’s smile sharpened. “I’m simply… Enthused.”

“Uh-huh.”

Charlie, oblivious, beamed. “Okay! Lead the way!”

Alastor inhaled through his nose and set the two of them in motion. The first few steps were easy, careful and calculated. But each jolt of movement reminded him of his internal predicament. He kept his posture immaculate, however there was an undeniable urgency in the way he guided Charlie, speeding up when he should have slowed, hesitating at points where he should have moved smoothly.

Charlie stumbled slightly. “Whoops! Sorry! You okay?”

“Never better,” he answered, voice a tad tighter than usual. “Just… focused.”

She giggled. “You sound kinda tense!”

“Tense? Why, dear girl, I haven’t been tense since before the Great Depression!”

“Uh-huh,” Angel snorted from across the room.

They approached the first minor obstacle; A stack of boxes Niffty had “temporarily” piled near the hallway.

Alastor’s heel tapped rapidly as he considered how to maneuver around them without jostling himself— Or Charlie— Too much. He swallowed an uncharacteristic flash of panic.

“Alright now, step to your left— No, your OTHER left—” He paused, briefly squeezing his legs together in a motion so small it barely qualified as movement. “…Excellent. Mind the boxes.”

Charlie tip-toed cheerfully around the obstacles with his guidance. “Wow! You’re good at this!” she said.

“Hah! Naturally.” Behind the smile, though, his thoughts were considerably less poised; ‘Oh dear. This is becoming a situation.’

He could have handled a loan shark, a rampaging sinner, or even a debate between overlords far more easily than this very simple— Yet horribly mistimed— Trust exercise. And the worst part? There was no dignified escape. Not with Charlie blindfolded and holding his arm with such innocent reliance. Not with everyone watching, already starting to realize there was something unusual about him today.

He forced his radio-static hum to remain calm and even. ‘Just a little longer. Just maintain control until—‘

“Okay!” Charlie chirped. “Now guide me up the stairs!”

Alastor froze. The stairs? The stairs?! He forced a grin that felt stretched even to him. “Why of course, my dear.”

He took the first step very slowly, very carefully, and with all of the dignity that a demon of his status could muster while facing the worst trust exercise of his very long afterlife.

The first stair creaked beneath Alastor’s shoe, its wooden board giving the familiar, grouchy complaint. Charlie obediently followed the guiding pressure of his hand on her arm. She lifted her foot, found the step, and balanced herself confidently.

Alastor, however, was less fortunate.

The moment he raised his leg to climb, a sharp reminder of his predicament jolted through his abdomen. His grin twitched but never faltered—His poker face was legendary, after all. But this? This was not the battlefield he wished to wage war upon.

‘This is absurd,’ he scolded himself internally. ‘I’ve faced Hell’s worst monstrosities without blinking, yet today I am bested by stairs and my own internal plumbing?’

He attempted a casual posture. Unfortunately, a casual posture was incompatible with a very real need not to move abruptly. He lowered his center of gravity just slightly— Very slightly— Hoping no one noticed.

Naturally, Angel Dust noticed. He lounged across the banister with exaggerated interest, propping his chin in his hand. “Aww, what’s the matter, Radio Daddy? Not as spry as ya used to be?”

Alastor didn’t look back. “My dear boy,” he said smoothly, “I am as spry as ever.” He then took the second stair. It felt like scaling a mountain; A mountain that had been booby-trapped by his own anatomy.

Charlie stepped up after him, nearly tripping. Alastor steadied her with an audible inhale—not of panic, definitely not panic, just of… Of focus!

“Careful now,” he said through a tight grin. “One foot directly in front of the other.”

“Are you okay?” Charlie asked. “You sound… Different.”

“I assure you,” he said, voice velvet-smooth despite the tension building behind his eyes, “I am simply being thorough.”

He took another step.

His foot tapped rapidly before lifting, as though negotiating with the stair. He braced, shifted his weight, then very carefully elevated his leg. His bladder felt like it was being split in half and his opening flared with the movement. He froze halfway through the step, breath hitching.

Niffty ran up. “Ooooh, you look funny, Mr. Alastor!”

He grinned at her through clenched teeth. “Do I? I’m only trying to concentrate, dear Niffty.”

She giggled and zipped off.

Charlie continued climbing, blissfully unaware of the silent crisis at her side. “You’re being really gentle today,” she said brightly. “It’s nice!”

“Yes, well,” Alastor managed, “I find gentleness is appropriate in some situations…”

The next stair felt taller than the others— Almost maliciously so. He approached it as though confronting an enemy. His knee bent. His other leg remained firmly planted, unwilling to concede ground.

‘If I lift too quickly— No, too slowly— For the love of all infernal realms, focus!’ He inhaled, counted softly. “One… two…”

Charlie took the step early. “Three!” she announced cheerfully.

Her sudden movement tugged his arm. His balance jolted, his breath caught, and he planted both feet firmly, shoulders stiffening as he recovered. His bladder’s contents shook and rippled, like a lake’s surface directly after a rock had been skipped across it.

“Charlie, my dear,” he said in a voice an octave higher than intended, “Perhaps we should synchronize our stepping a tad more!”

“Oh! Sorry!” she laughed. “Okay, okay! You call it out!”

He cleared his throat, lowering his pitch back to its usual smooth hum. “Ahem. Quite alright. Now then…” He tried again, one hand briefly pressing to his hip in what he prayed looked like dignified contemplation instead of dire need. “On three. One… Two… Three.”

They advanced. Slowly, excruciatingly, painstakingly.

Each stair was its own trial. Each elevation a battle between pride and anatomy. His posture remained perfect— Impeccably so— Because Alastor would sooner have combusted than let his discomfort show fully.

But the slight tremor in his step? That, he could not hide.

By the time they reached the middle of the staircase, he felt like he had lived ten separate lifetimes since the first step.

Vaggie, observing from the bottom, raised a brow. “What’s wrong with HIM? He usually walks like he’s gliding.”

Husk muttered, “Guy looks drunk.”

Angel cackled loud enough to echo.

Alastor closed his eyes briefly. “Just a momentary… Inconvenience,” he said through his smile. “The stairs and I are… Negotiating.”

Charlie perked up. “You’re doing great, Alastor! Okay; Next one!”

Alastor hesitated before climbing again. He could see the landing at the top like a salvation— And also like a mischievous challenge daring him to maintain his façade until then.

“One step at a time,” he murmured to himself. “Quite literally.”

Charlie, blindfolded but enthusiastic, lifted her foot.

He braced himself, and the climb continued.

Alastor began to suspect the staircase had somehow lengthened itself just to fuck with him. Of course, he knew logically that this was impossible. He was the only one capable of placing such an enchantment on them, and unless his desperation had completely revoked his ability to control his magic, he hadn’t done it.

Nevertheless, the sentiment stood. Every step felt taller than the last. Every step tugged at his patience, his composure, and, increasingly, his self-control. Every step made him wish, more than anything, that an ‘intermission’ were an option.

Charlie, meanwhile, was humming. Cheerfully wandering forwards entirely at the mercy of his guiding arm.

He guided her upwards with all the grace and precision he could muster. His movements were slow, meticulous, and ever so slightly rigid— But still dignified. Always dignified. His reputation allowed nothing less.

“One… Two… Three,” Charlie echoed as they stepped again.

Alastor swallowed tightly. “Yes… Well done.”

Only three stairs remained. Only three, and salvation— Glorious, merciful, stationary salvation— Waited at the top.

His knee lifted cautiously. His weight shifted. Each micro-movement was calculated like a chess master planning five moves ahead in a match where the opponent was gravity itself.

Angel Dust, leaning over the railing, whispered dramatically to Husk, “I swear he’s gonna snap like a glowstick.”

Alastor pretended not to hear him.

Step one.

His foot touched down on the wood plank. He let out a breath he hadn’t realized he was holding. His bladder’s walls tightened as his lungs expanded. One eyelid quivered.

Charlie followed suit, trusting him completely. “Perfect,” she said. “We’re doing so well!”

“Yes,” Alastor answered, voice a shade tight. “We are certainly… Ascending.”

Step two.

This time, Charlie stumbled a little as she reached for the next stair. Alastor caught her swiftly, hands gentle yet tense. The movement sent a jolt through his bladder, but he held firm, steadying her without so much as a wobble.

“You okay?” she asked.

“Marvelous,” he said quickly— Too quickly— But his smile didn’t break.

He did not, however, release her shoulder immediately. He needed a second. Just one second.

At the bottom of the stairs, Vaggie watched him with her arms folded. “What is WRONG with him?”

Husk gruffed, “I already said, he looks drunk.”

Alastor ignored both of them with professional expertise.

Step three. The final one.

Alastor took a breath, steadying himself the way one might before pulling a sword from a stone.

“One more, Charlie,” he said. “Up we go.”

She lifted her foot.

He lifted his.

They stepped, and at last, they reached the landing.

Alastor straightened the moment his foot touched the upper floor. It was subtle, refined, but unmistakably relieved. His posture regained that effortless, supernatural quality; His shoulders loosened; His grin smoothed into its usual polished sharpness. It was nearly over…

Charlie pulled off her blindfold with a victorious flourish. “We did it!”

“Yes indeed!” he exclaimed, perhaps a bit too loudly. “We conquered the treacherous ascent!”

Charlie beamed. “You were so careful with me! I really appreciate it. I feel like I can trust you with anything.”

Alastor’s laugh was bright and filled with static. “But of course, my dear! Your safety is my utmost priority.”

Charlie handed him the blindfold. “Okay! Your turn!”

Alastor froze. The landing might as well have opened beneath him. “My… Turn?”

“Yep!” Charlie said. “Now I guide you DOWN the stairs!”

The stairs. Again. With a blindfold on. Alastor’s smile remained perfectly plastered on— But, there was an unmistakable panic behind it. Only a flicker, only a crack, but a crack nonetheless. His eye twitched.

“W-well!” he said, straightening as if his spine were being pulled by puppet strings. “It appears I shall simply have to… Rise to the occasion!”

Charlie giggled. “No—DESCEND to it!”

He laughed politely. Internally? He wanted to shout.

Charlie stepped beside him, tying the blindfold around his head with careful, eager hands.“Ready?” she asked.

He inhaled sharply. “Ready,” he lied.

The blindfold came down over Alastor’s eyes like a curtain closing on a doomed performance. The world went black, no sense of depth, no sense of footing, only the warm pressure of Charlie’s hands tying the knot behind his head and his own growing sense of internal urgency.

He stood perfectly still, back stiff, fingers flexing once in a rare, unguarded motion. Losing sight did nothing to help his situation; In fact, it turned discomfort into something much closer to danger.

“Okay!” Charlie said brightly, clapping once. “I’ll guide you down, just like you guided me up!”

“Splendid,” he said, voice tight as a rubber band pulled to snapping point.

Angel whistled low from the railing. “Ooooh, he’s sweatin’! I didn’t think he COULD sweat!”

“I’m not sweating,” Alastor replied

“Uh-huh,” Angel snorted.

Charlie squeezed his arm reassuringly. “Don’t worry! I know it’s scary not being able to see, but I’m right here. We’ll go slow!”

Alastor nodded, even though the motion wasn’t entirely voluntary— He had to keep moving or risk worsening his own self-inflicted predicament. His bladder was pleading with him, demanding that he squirm around— That he do SOMETHING. The longer he held still, the more waves battered its walls.

“Very well,” he said. “Lead the way.”

Charlie placed one hand on his forearm and the other at the center of his back, guiding him gently towards the top stair.

Alastor’s foot hovered uncertainly over the edge. Without vision, depth perception had abandoned him completely. His heel bobbed, searching for the first drop, and every second he lingered felt like balancing on a chasm’s edge with a very pressing reason NOT to misstep.

“Okay, first step!” Charlie said cheerfully. “Just follow my voice. You can do it!”

Alastor inhaled, trying to maintain dignity. “One… two… three—” He stepped. His foot found the stair— But it was far lower than he had expected. The sudden drop jolted his abdomen, sending a tight, involuntary shiver up his spine. His free hand twitched, gripping the railing harder than he’d intended.

Charlie gasped softly. “Oh gosh! Sorry! It’s hard when you can’t see, huh?”

“A gentle surprise,” he replied, voice strained. “Nothing more.”

He felt her pat his arm sympathetically. “Don’t worry, Alastor. You’re doing amazing.”

‘Amazing,’ he thought bleakly. ‘If I survive this with my dignity intact, it shall be a miracle fit for broadcast.’

They reached the next step.

“Okay,” Charlie said, moving slowly and carefully, “Bring your foot down like this…”

Alastor tried. He REALLY tried. But, his attempt at caution resulted in an awkward, stilted lowering of his leg that felt like it took an entire century. His posture remained upright, but only because if he bent anything too fast or too far he would risk—

No. Unacceptable. That possibility was unthinkable.

His foot finally found the next stair. Another small, uncontrollable tremor ran through him.

Charlie squeezed his arm again. “You’re shaking! It’s okay, no reason to be nervous!”

“Nervous?” Alastor let out a brittle laugh. “My dear, I fear nothing.”

Except, apparently, stairs, blindfolds and biology. Particularly when they came packaged as a set.

“You don’t have to pretend with me!” Charlie said warmly. “Everyone gets anxious sometimes.”

“Mm,” he replied, “How reassuring.”

At the bottom of the stairs, Husk muttered to Vaggie, “He’s walkin’ like someone stapled his knees together.”

Vaggie squinted. “Something’s up with him. He’s never this… Fidgety.”

Angel beamed. “He’s gonna break! This is the best day ever.”

Alastor took another step. This time, the angle forced his knee to lift higher than he would have liked. His breath caught sharply.

Charlie immediately reacted. “A— Alastor? Are you okay? Do you want to slow down?”

He forced a chuckle. “Slower? I fear at too slow a pace I may fossilize.”

“Well… Then tell me what you DO need!”

“That,” he said carefully, “…Is a complicated question.”

Charlie blinked, confused but sympathetic. “Is it because you can’t see? It’s okay! We’ll take tiny steps.”

Tiny steps. The worst possible idea, considering the rush he was in to finish this. But, he couldn’t tell her that. Because the moment he said anything remotely close to ‘I need a break,’ he risked more demons discovering his ‘condition’, he risked one of them somehow spilling the news to Vox.

So he just nodded. “Lead on.”

Charlie guided his hand to the banister. “Here—This might help you feel steadier.”

He gripped it like a lifeline, though he kept his smile serene.

One step.

Then another.

Then another, each more agonizingly slow than the last. His shoes squeaked faintly on the wood, broadcasting his tension in quiet little bursts that he desperately wished weren’t audible.

Charlie leaned close, whispering comfortingly, “Don’t worry. I’ve got you.”

Angel leaned closer to Husk, whispering far less comfortingly, “He’s gonna lose it.”

Step.

Step.

Step.

Alastor’s entire body was a study in controlled crisis, but his voice remained the same calm, charming, smooth baritone. “Almost there…?” he murmured.

“Just three more steps!” Charlie announced proudly.

He nearly groaned. Nearly. But he caught himself at the last second, turning the sound into a polite throat-clear. “Wonderful,” he said instead.

Charlie continued downwards, innocently making assumptions out loud. “I know darkness can be scary! But once we’re at the bottom, you can take the blindfold off and everything will be fine!”

He nodded stiffly. If only it were truly that simple. Alastor had faced eldritch horrors, rival overlords, and violent sinners with a smile so confident it bordered on smug. But these three remaining steps— These unassuming slabs of creaking wood— Were rapidly becoming his greatest nemesis.

Blindfolded. Disoriented. Trying to retain every ounce of composure, and absolutely terrified of tripping in a way that would make him look ridiculous. In a way that might cause him to fall. A fall that could cause him to…

No. Nope. No way would THAT happen! Not with witnesses! That was… That was the POSITIVE side of his whole stage-fright conundrum! He couldn’t… Fully lose control.

It just… It FELT like it…

Charlie held his arm gently and reassuringly.“Okay, Alastor, you’re almost there! Just three more steps and you’ll be fine!”

He managed a strangled-sounding laugh.“Marvelous… marvelous…”

Charlie smiled. “You’re doing so well! I had no idea you got this nervous about… You know… Not seeing where you’re going.”

He didn’t bother denying it. At this point, letting her assume he was just bothered by being visually disoriented was far better than admitting the truth; That he needed to urinate so badly he wouldn’t have been able to see straight even with the blindfold OFF.

“Let’s take them one at a time,” Charlie said.

“Yes, that would be… prudent.”

Charlie guided his hand to the railing. “Just ease your foot down—Slowly.”

He attempted exactly that. But without vision, his brain supplied wildly inaccurate guesses about the stair’s distance. His foot hovered, searching. He bent his knee too far, corrected, hovered again, and then—finally— THUNK.

He hit the stair a bit harder than intended.

Charlie winced. “Oof — You’re really tense!”

“Tense? Nonsense! I am… Relaxed.” His voice cracked on the last syllable.

Angel Dust choked on laughter so loud that Husk slapped him.

“Okay,” Charlie said soothingly, “Lean into me just a bit. I won’t let you fall.”

Alastor did lean— Rigidly, like a board that refused to bend because bending was undignified. Because bending would put more pressure onto his midsection, which couldn’t handle much more strain. He felt foolish, unsteady, and bizarrely vulnerable.

Charlie mistook the trembling in his hand for fear.“It’s okay,” she said, patting him. “Being blindfolded makes your imagination run wild, right? Happens to everyone!”

“Indeed…” he muttered, foot searching the air again.

He dipped too far this time. Charlie caught his elbow, steadying him before he pitched forward.

Alastor, face unseen behind the blindfold, felt his composure wobble like a poorly stacked tower. “Nearly there,” he said with a tight grin. “This is… Invigorating.”

Charlie beamed. “Good job!”

The final step was the worst one of all.

Charlie guided him forwards. “This is it! Last one!”

Alastor drew in a slow breath. Then another. Then one more.

To Charlie, he sounded like someone preparing to leap off a cliff. “You’ve got this,” she promised. “I won’t let anything bad happen to you.”

Alastor nodded stiffly. “I trust you, my dear.” His foot hovered. And hovered. And hovered.

“You can put it down now!” Charlie encouraged.

“Yes, well— Ah— Just calculating the distance—”

“It’s right there! Promise!”

“Right. Yes. Of course.” He finally lowered his foot with agonizing caution… And missed the step entirely.

Charlie caught him under the arm just before he stumbled forwards.

Angel Dust’s scream-laugh echoed through the entire hotel. Husk snorted so violently he dropped his cards. Niffty ran past cheering, “You’re doing great, Mister Alastor!”

Alastor steadied himself, posture snapping back into perfect form the moment his foot found the actual stair.

“There,” Charlie said brightly. “Safe and sound!”

He exhaled shakily. “Marvelous. Positively… exhilarating.” And then he stepped off the final stair. The moment both feet were on level ground, his whole spine untensed in one fluid, relieved shudder. Shadows around him flickered ever so faintly with released stress.

Charlie, still holding his arm, softened. “See? You were brave the whole time.”

Alastor laughed weakly— YES, weakly, something he would deny until the end of days. “My dear, bravery has many forms.”

Charlie guided him a few steps away from the staircase and reached up to untie the blindfold.“Okay, Alastor,” she said softly, “You’re safe now. You can open your eyes.”

He didn’t move at first. There was a stiffness in his shoulders— An unusual rigidity for someone who typically glided like a phantom. When the blindfold finally slid off, his smile flickered, wavering ever so slightly before snapping back into place.

But the moment his eyes adjusted and he fully processed that the ordeal was over, a wave of relief washed over his features. It was brief—Blink-and-miss-it brief— But Charlie saw it.

“Thank you, my dear,” Alastor said, clearing his throat. “A most invigorating exercise, to be sure.” He tried to stand casually, as he always did— But the composed stance faltered again. His posture wavered, his hands twitched at his sides, and he shifted his weight from one foot to the other as though he couldn’t settle.

Charlie’s smile softened. She dismissed the others with a glance— An unspoken ‘Give him some space.’ To her surprise, they obeyed. Even Angel, for once, slunk off without a joke.

Then she turned back to Alastor. “Hey,” she said gently. “You okay?”

He froze. His grin stayed, but his eyes flicked away, unable to meet hers. “Of course. Nothing of concern.”

She stepped closer—Slowly, to avoid startling him. “Alastor,” she said quietly, “It’s not… It wasn’t just the blindfold, was it? Do you also need to… Um… Go to the bathroom?”

His eyes darted to her, startled.

Charlie smiled reassuringly. “It’s okay. You don’t have to pretend around me.”

Alastor’s mouth opened slightly— Just slightly. His static hummed. For a second, his façade cracked, not with humiliation but with something far rarer for him; Relief.

“You noticed,” he said quietly.

“Of course I noticed,” she replied. “You hide it well, but I look out for everyone here. Even the ones who pretend they don’t need it. ESPECIALLY them.”

“This exercise was… More demanding than anticipated,” he admitted carefully. “And I, um… Yes. You are correct… Nature calls. And, unfortunately, even the Radio Demon must answer.”

“That’s fine,” Charlie said. “Do you need… Er, I mean, do you WANT me to help you get settled? Can’t go breaking our deal now, can I?”

A silence passed.

“Yes,” Alastor whispered. “I believe that would be… Appreciated.”

She offered her arm again, and his smile softened into something almost human.

Charlie didn’t say anything else at first. She simply placed a gentle hand on Alastor’s sleeve—light enough that he could pull away if he wished, but firm enough to show she truly meant to guide him— Then walked with him through the hallway and out of view of the lobby.

Alastor followed her without comment.

That alone spoke volumes.

The Radio Demon rarely allowed anyone to lead him anywhere. His usual gait carried a kind of intense dominion— Effortless, smooth, unshakeable. But right now, his steps were noticeably smaller, more careful. Not timid, no, but very tense. Measured. Self-conscious in a way no one but Charlie had ever witnessed.

She didn’t push him.

She didn’t ask questions.

She simply walked beside him until they reached a seldom-used restroom on the hotel’s second floor. She opened the door quietly. “Here,” she said softly. “Nice and private.”

Alastor hesitated at the threshold. His silhouette was rigid—Almost statuesque—but his eyes flicked inside the room, scanning for any sign of life or noise.

Then he stepped in.

Charlie gently closed the door behind them. The outside world grew muffled, like someone had turned down the ambient chaos of Hell.

She approached slowly. “You can relax. No one will bother you here.”

Alastor’s hands, normally clasped neatly behind his back or poised at his cane, hovered uncertainly at his sides.

Then she brightened. “What if I play some music to help you go? You know— Vintage stuff? Something comforting so you’re not focused on everything else!”

Alastor hesitated. “Music… Might help.”

Charlie instantly fumbled for her phone.

Alastor eyed it suspiciously. “If you play anything recorded after 1934, I will almost certainly loathe it.”

“No worries!” Charlie scrolled. “I have a whole playlist called ‘Alastor Friendly (No Dubstep).’” She pressed play and a warm, crackling swing melody drifted through the room.

Alastor’s shoulders relaxed, just barely. “Hmm,” he murmured. “Acceptable.”

Charlie cheered internally. Progress. Real progress.

The old swing melody echoed warmly off the walls. Charlie stood statue-still, as if even wriggling a toe might psychically disrupt the delicate equilibrium of Alastor’s nerves as he entered a stall.

From behind the door, she heard… Nothing.

The music swelled into a cheerful trumpet solo.

Still nothing.

She frowned. Maybe he needed a little more distance from her. She tiptoed backwards three careful steps. Then—

From behind the door came the faintest, tiniest sound.

A powerful rush of liquid, followed by a blissful, quietly triumphant sigh.

He’d done it. His desperate state slowly relaxed, transforming itself into sheer, total relief. His spine lost its tension, eyelids fluttering. His fountain was loud, piercing his ear drums, but the music helped to muffle it just enough for his shoulders to go limp in contentment. His whole body felt numb and floaty by the time his stream finally started to slow down.

Charlie clapped and clapped outside the stall. “Yes, Alastor! That’s great! You’re doing it! We’re making progress!”

The music continued to play as his faucet ran for another moment, bladder wringing itself out the rest of the way. This was followed by the rustle of fabric and Alastor humming a soft, jaunty little tune that she’d never heard him hum before.

He actually sounded… Happy.

‘Mission: Success.’

A moment later, the stall door creaked open. Alastor stepped out with the casual composure of someone who had absolutely not just been psyching himself up for a basic biological act all day. He dusted off his suit, adjusted his bowtie, smoothed his hair… Then smiled his usual homicidal grin.

“Well!” he declared. “That was… Refreshing.”

Charlie continued clapping her hands delightfully. “You did great!”

“I am aware,” Alastor said, preening slightly. “Your music selection was adequate. And the distance you provided was immensely helpful.”

“See? Teamwork!” Charlie cheered.

“Yes, yes.” He waved a hand dismissively, but he couldn’t quite hide the pleased twitch at the corner of his mouth. “Let us speak of this no longer!”

“Oh, of course! Totally secret! One hundred percent confidential!” She leaned in and whispered, “But I AM very proud of you. You’re acing ALL your trust exercises today!”

Alastor made a noise somewhere between a laugh and a malfunctioning radio. “Let us pretend I didn’t hear that.”

Charlie gave him a sunny salute. “Your secret’s safe with me, Alastor.”

He began striding elegantly down the hall, cane tapping in a jaunty rhythm. “Splendid! Now, if you’ll excuse me, I have mischief to attend to. I feel remarkably invigorated!”

Charlie giggled. “Glad I could help!”

*001*2026-03-23 13:23

It was late at night and the streets of Pentagram City buzzed with the same chaotic rhythm they always did; Neon signs flickering in and out, demonic laughter echoing down alleyways, and the distant boom of something exploding that probably shouldn’t have exploded. Alastor walked through it all as if this cacophony were a well-rehearsed orchestra playing only for him. His cane tapped crisply against the cracked sidewalk, each step measured, jaunty, and perfectly self-assured.

Well... almost perfectly.

Trailing beside him, Angel Dust flicked ash from a cigarette and adjusted the fur trim of his coat. “So lemme get this straight,” Angel said, leaning in with a sly grin. “You drag me out to the city’s grimiest, stickiest corner, in the middle of the night, just to shop for spices you ‘simply must acquire’? What, ya runnin’ a cooking show now?”

Alastor gave a light, old-timey laugh. “My dear boy, one must maintain taste even in Hell. And, you were the only one still awake!”

His smile twitched a little. He had not been entirely truthful. Yes, he HAD selected Angel Dust purely because HE was the only one who hadn’t yet gone to bed, but his REASON for wanting to wander so late at night went far beyond a desire for spices.

Sleeping had become rather difficult. He’d begun to dread it, in fact.

His recent… Fixation on the possibility of Vox uncovering his ‘urinary hesitancy’ situation had brought with it a slew of uncomfortable, humiliating dreams. Night after night, his mind would play him scenarios in which he accidentally gave his problem away to his nemesis. And, every time, Vox was delighted. Every time, Vox went straight to his studio for an ‘emergency broadcast’. Every time, he told all of Hell that Alastor’s weak point wasn’t a flaw in his magic, wasn’t a specific spot he found too painful to be hit on, no… Alastor’s weak point was his bladder, and his inability to empty it if a single soul was within hearing-range.

His reputation was destroyed immediately. No one really feared him anymore. Demons snickered when he walked by, rather than cowering.

When he woke up, he knew a lot of his dream was nonsensical. His ‘shortcoming’ being public knowledge wouldn’t cause anyone to think his powers suddenly didn’t exist. They’d still have plenty of reason to be terrified of him. They’d be aware that openly laughing at his embarrassment would mean untold levels of pain. They’d know what he could still do.

But, they WOULD also know about the one thing he couldn’t…

And that was a bit more than Alastor could tolerate.

No, Vox could never know. He could never, ever know. He’d tell everyone…

Last night’s dream had been the worst one yet, by far.

Everything else had been the same; Vox figured out Alastor’s situation and broadcasted it to the entirety of Hell. But, this time, instead of waking up once the laughter began, Alastor found himself transported to a foggy, neon-streaked version of Hell, all twisting streets and buzzing signs. Vox was there at the end of the road.

Vox, lounging atop a glowing neon cube, smirked at him with that infuriatingly smug expression. “Hello, Radio Demon,” he said, voice crackling through static. “Ready for me to cure you?”

Alastor straightened up, teeth bared in a grin that, even in his dream, felt forced. “What? Cure me? What do—” He froze mid-gesture, watching Vox pull out a little remote and mash a button.

The next thing Alastor knew, there was a strange, uncomfortable warmth spreading down his legs.

“Huh— No,” he whispered to himself, his usually impeccable composure crumbling. The warmth spread faster. “This… Cannot be happening!”

Vox’s smirk widened. “Oh? Is the mighty Radio Demon… Having an accident?” The cube beneath him flickered ominously, almost as if laughing along.

Alastor tried to turn away, tried to dash behind a conveniently placed building—but the heat had betrayed him. He looked down in horror. His suit was soaked.

A scream caught in his throat, though even that sounded eerily like a laugh. “Impossible! I am… unflappable! Unstoppable! I—”

But Vox wasn’t listening. He was now twirling a giant remote, clicking buttons with gleeful malice. Each click seemed to amplify the embarrassment, a spotlight highlighting Alastor’s predicament for an audience of giggling demons.

Alastor tried to speak, to retort, to summon his usual charm— But, all that came out was a squeaky, high-pitched whine. His nightmare self looked utterly ridiculous. Worse yet, he could feel the dream version of himself squirming, legs weak from sheer humiliation.

Somewhere deep in the back of his mind, he realized the absurdity of it all, and a chuckle bubbled up. A horrified, wheezing chuckle that quickly spiraled into full-blown laughter.

Vox tilted his head, amused, and the neon cube beneath him pulsed like a mocking heartbeat. “Look at you,” he said. “All of Hell watching as you drench yourself…”

Alastor’s laughter only grew, tinged with horror and hilarity. Just as the sound threatened to overwhelm him, he jerked awake, tangled in the sheets of his cozy, bed. Heart racing, face flushed, bladder about to explode. He dashed from the bed to his bathroom. He realized… He had narrowly avoided total disaster. Had he remained asleep only a couple minutes more…

Well. He didn’t wish to dwell on that!

Or, on the dream itself.

And he certainly wasn’t eager for it to give an encore performance, so pushing back sleep for a few extra hours was the solution. Having someone along for company would also stave off the drowsiness longer.

“Besides,” his smile sharpened at Angel Dust. “You were in desperate need of fresh air.”

Angel snorted. “Sweetheart, the only thing fresh around here is you, and even that’s debatable.”

They continued their walk, the banter bouncing easily between them. To any onlooker, it looked like the Radio Demon was in his usual, unshakable form. His posture tall, his grin fixed, his aura humming with warm, vintage static. But Angel, perceptive in ways few realized, noticed something off.

Alastor’s stride had stiffened. His eyes darted, subtly— Too subtly for most to pick up on— Towards the nearby storefronts. The grip on his cane tightened just a fraction.

Angel raised a brow. “Hey, uh… You good? You look like someone stuck ya with a bad reception signal.”

“Perfectly fine,” Alastor replied smoothly. “Never better.”

He lied smoothly, too. That was the problem.

Truth was, the irritation in his middle had been growing for several blocks now, though he refused to acknowledge it out loud. A distinctly mortal need— Which he thoroughly despised— Had been nagging him.

Hell, with all its depravity, still had public restrooms. Terrible ones. Loud ones. Crowded, unsanitary, bustling with demons who had zero respect for privacy. It was one of the only environments that genuinely unnerved him. And the last environment he wanted to be in when he was trying to FORGET about the stupid dreams he’d been having.

‘Humiliating,’ he thought, jaw tightening. ‘Absolutely humiliating that something so trivial persists in this body.’

Angel chattered on for another block before stopping abruptly. “Okay, okay, hold up.” He turned and planted his many hands on his hips. “You’re fidgety. You don’t DO fidgety. What’s the deal?”

Alastor’s smile froze a bit too long. “Angel Dust, I assure you, there is nothing wrong.”

Angel made a face. “Bullshit. You keep glancing at every building like they’re auditioning for somethin’. And you haven’t insulted me in at least ten minutes— That’s basically a cry for help.”

The Radio Demon sighed through his teeth, static flickering faintly around him. He took a breath— Two breaths— Before speaking quietly enough that only Angel could hear. “I…” He coughed delicately into one hand, gaze shifting away. “Need to… Attend to a private matter.”

Angel blinked. “A—Oh. Ohhhhhh. You gotta take a huge leak.”

Alastor’s entire expression remained fixed in its smile, but the air around him pulsed with offended energy. “I would NEVER phrase it in such crude terms.”

Angel tried, to his credit, very hard not to laugh. “Okay, okay. Sorry.“

“However.” Alastor glanced nervously— Yes, genuinely nervously— At the neon restroom sign behind a nearby club. Demons filtered in and out of it with all the grace of drunk rhinoceroses. “I cannot.”

“Yeah, I know, babe. You got stage fright. But, I can he—“

“It is not ‘stage fright.’ I am not some amateur performer unable to deliver his lines!” Alastor hissed, too quietly for anyone but Angel to hear. “I simply require… Solitude.”

And Hell, by design, offered none.

Angel looked at him— Really looked at him— For a moment. Then all of the sarcasm slipped from his voice. “Hey. It’s okay. Seriously. Nothin’ to be embarrassed about.”

Alastor stiffened again. “I am NOT embarrassed.”

“Yes, you are,” Angel said gently. “And that’s fine. Everybody’s got something. I keep saying I can help ya! All you gotta do is let me!”

The Radio Demon shifted, uncomfortable in ways far beyond just the physical pressure of his jolting bladder. His fingers drummed restlessly on his cane. “I would rather wait,” he said. “Until we return to the hotel.”

Angel glanced up the long stretch of the city, then back at Alastor’s too-rigid posture. “…You’re really gonna make it that far?”

Silence fell. Alastor’s thighs pressed together, tensing in an effort to apply a bit of pressure to the passageway his bladder was begging to be allowed to use. “O—Of course.”

Angel exhaled, then slung an arm around Alastor’s shoulder. “Al. C’mon. I know a place. Quiet, clean, nobody goes there because the bartender picks fights with anyone who looks at him funny.”

Alastor frowned. “Angel, really, I—”

“Nope.” Angel marched forward, dragging the Radio Demon with more strength than his lanky frame suggested he ought to possess. “I’m helpin’. Don’t argue.”

Alastor allowed himself to be pulled, though his pride writhed like a trapped beast. “This is highly unnecessary.”

“Yeah, yeah,” Angel said with a wink. “So’s your ego, but we let you keep that too.”

Despite himself, Alastor let out a dry chuckle.

Angel’s “quiet place” turned out to be a dim little bar squeezed between a pawn shop and a boarded-up theater. From the outside, it looked abandoned— Until an irritable voice yelled from inside, “WE’RE NOT OPEN, YOU LEECHES!”

Angel pushed the door anyway. “Relax, Lou. It’s me.”

A glass was thrown in his general direction.

Angel caught it with one hand and set it politely on the nearest table. “See? He likes me.”

Alastor stepped in behind him, shoulders drawn rigidly tight. The bar was empty, save for the bartender brandishing a bat with nails hammered through it. The atmosphere was quiet— Eerily so. It wasn’t exactly comforting, but compared to the chaos outside, it was at least isolated.

Angel clapped Alastor on the back— Way, way too hard to be appreciated by a demon nursing a full bladder. Particularly not by one who abhorred being touched to begin with. Alastor stumbled in both surprise and agitation. His liquids sloshed with the motion, colliding remorselessly against his bladder’s overtaxed walls. “Angel—“

“Alright, babe, the restroom’s right there,” Angel pointed at a door half-hidden behind a stack of crates. “It’s private enough, yeah?”

Alastor’s eyes narrowed. He had his own definition of the phrase “private enough”, and he wasn’t positive if that room would qualify. But, the pressure in his midsection had reached a point where arguing was becoming less of a choice and more of a precarious gamble.

“Yes,” he said hopefully. “This will suffice.” He stepped towards the door.

And Angel, unfortunately, stepped right beside him.

Alastor froze. “…Angel Dust. What are you doing?”

“Helpin’,” Angel said, leaning against the wall. “In case you need moral support.”

“I do not.”

Angel raised both sets of hands. “Okay, okay, but like— Some folks with shy bladders need a distraction. Or reassurance.”

Alastor’s eye twitched. “Your presence is neither distracting nor reassuring.”

Angel waved dismissively. “C’mon, you’re overthinkin’ it. I can talk you through it! Just pretend we’re in a buddy-cop movie or something. Or a radio-play, I guess. Hey, were there buddy-cop radio-plays, or—“

“Angel.”

But Angel was already in motion— Leaning near the door, poking at the handle, peeking inside before Alastor could stop him. “It’s clean-ish! No one’s in there! I mean, someone— Who definitely WASN’T me— Drew this whole menagerie of very detailed dicks on the wall, but I’ll stand out here and guard the door!”

Alastor’s face remained smiling, but the radio static around him began to climb in pitch, the way it did right before he strangled someone politely. “Angel,” he said slowly. “I merely require to be left alone.”

“Sure, sure! Alone! I’ll just be right here. Outside. Totally silent. Absolutely silent.” Angel stood stiff as a soldier, then added far too loudly, “See? Not makin’ a sound.”

Alastor shut his eyes. “You just made a sound.”

“Okay, NOW I’m quiet.”

“You’re still talking.”

“Oh! Right. Right. Okay, now.”

Silence.

Then Angel loudly inhaled, loudly exhaled, and announced; “See?!”

Alastor hadn’t even made it into the restroom. He simply stood there, rigid, shoulders bristling. “You talked again.”

Angel blinked. “…Oh.”

Alastor turned away from the door, cane tapping sharply. “This was a disastrous idea.”

Angel jogged after him. “Wait— Hey, no, no, no, c’mon! I can be better, I swear! I’ll shut up! I’ll stand across the room! I’ll sing a lullaby if that helps—”

“Nothing about you singing would assist anything,” Alastor said, voice thinning at the edges with barely-contained mortification. “Least of all THIS.”

Angel winced. “Okay, ow, rude, but fair.”

“And if you so much as whisper something again, I may have to—”

The bartender shouted from behind the counter, “IF YOU TWO DON’T USE THE RESTROOM OR LEAVE, I’M CHARGING YOU RENT!”

Angel held up a finger. “Hear that? You’re stressin’ out poor Lou!”

“You are the one stressing him out!”

Angel opened his mouth. Closed it. Then, in the rarest of moments, he looked genuinely apologetic. “…Look,” Angel said, softer now. “I’m tryin’. I really am. I just… I dunno how to do the gentle thing without screwin’ it up.”

Alastor’s breath faltered— Not in annoyance, but in something closer to surprise.

Angel rubbed the back of his neck. “If you want me to wait outside. Or the other side of the block. Or send me to another dimension, if that’s somethin’ you can do. Just say the word.”

Alastor hesitated. He WANTED to snap. To reassert dominance. To push Angel away before the mortification collapsed into vulnerability. But Angel wasn’t mocking him. He wasn’t pitying him. He was just awkwardly, clumsily trying to care.

“…Very well,” Alastor said finally, voice softening just a fraction. “Stand far from the door. No talking. No knocking. No commentary of any kind.”

Angel saluted with all four hands. “You got it. Zip-lipped. Mute. I’ll be your sexy lil’ ghost.”

“Angel.”

“Right! Shutting up!”

Angel darted several steps back, far enough to give Alastor space, but still within sight in case anything went wrong. For once, his mouth stopped running.

Alastor turned again towards the restroom. He took a deep breath, and tried, once more, to open the door.

The restroom door creaked faintly on its hinges as Alastor slipped inside, closing it and turning the lock. He leaned back against it for a moment— Eyes shut, breath drawn tight through his nose— Listening for any sound of Angel.

…Silence.

Miraculously, Angel seemed to be honoring the agreement. No tapping heels, no humming, no muffled commentary.

Good. That was good.

Alastor straightened, adjusting his coat and cane, even though neither actually needed adjusting. But the ritual was grounding. Familiarity was power. And power was the one thing he felt alarmingly short on at the moment.

The restroom was small, dimly lit, and smelled faintly of bleach trying to overpower something infinitely less pleasant. A cracked mirror reflected his own smile back at him; Far too stiff, far too glassy.

“Well,” he murmured to himself, “this is hardly the grand stage of one’s dreams.”

He approached the stall. Everything in him felt coiled, tense, hyper-aware. The rustle of his coat. The faint hum of a flickering light. The distant, muffled thud of Lou smacking something violently with that nail-studded bat of his.

Alastor shut the stall door, locked it, unzipped himself… And froze.

The anticipation hit him first— A sharp dread that squeezed his chest before moving down to wrap a brutal fist around his bladder. It wasn’t a matter of not needing to go; the urgency was very real and very, VERY intense. But urgency didn’t matter. Not when the environment wasn’t right. Not when his body locked up with the precision of a trap snapping shut.

He exhaled slowly. Then again.

‘This is fine,’ he told himself. ‘Merely a moment’s adjustment. A minor inconvenience. You are in control.’

But the longer he stood there, the more he tried to force out a stream, the more his body rebelled.

A ripple of frustration cut through him, gouging aggressively through his bladder. “… Ridiculous.”

He rested a hand lightly on the stall wall, fingers tightening as he made another attempt.

Nothing.

Not even the faintest willingness from his muscles to cooperate.

The static around him trembled— Quiet to most, but loud inside his own skull— Announcing his growing irritation. His embarrassment. His vulnerability.

‘Pathetic,’ he thought with a bitter edge. ‘A demon of your stature, undone by something so… Small.’

His shoulders locked.

Nothing.

A spike of frustration shot through him, quick and sharp. He clenched his teeth, the edges of his smile distorting.

He pressed his hand over his face, the gesture uncharacteristically weary.

Minutes passed. Slow, painful minutes in which nothing changed except his mounting irritation, the intensity of the pressure in his bladder, and the humiliating awareness of his own limitation.

A deep breath entered his lungs, filling him with enough composure to rebuild his façade brick by brick. He unlocked the stall and stepped out, adjusting his bowtie with a sharp tug, as if tidying himself could erase the failed attempt from the record entirely.

When he opened the restroom door, Angel jumped a little— Not because he’d been snooping, but because he’d been trying so hard not to intrude that he’d stood stiffly in place like a broken animatronic frozen mid-performance.

Angel’s eyes flicked to Alastor’s face immediately.“…Did it work?” he asked softly.

Alastor brushed past him, heading towards the door with brisk, clipped steps. “This was a lapse in judgment. I should not have attempted this outside the hotel.”

“Hey, wait—Al— Hold up—” Angel hurried after him, heels clacking a little too loudly.

Alastor didn’t stop walking. “There is nothing more to be done. I will manage until we return.”

“But that’s—what, an hour’s walk?!” Angel protested. A lie… It was more like two. “You’re already— Al, seriously, you’re gonna hurt yourself!”

Alastor paused at the threshold of the bar, spine ramrod straight. When he turned his head, the smile he gave Angel was polite… And exhausted. “I am aware of my situation, dear boy. But I cannot— CANNOT— Perform under these conditions.”

Angel stared at him, expression softening with a kind of gentle worry Alastor rarely saw directed at him. “…Okay. Then we’ll get you back as fast as possible. No stops. No jokes. No noise.”

Alastor gave him a look. “You? No noise?”

Angel’s lips curved in a tiny, sheepish grin. “I can try.”

Alastor exhaled a quiet laugh despite himself. The humiliation still burned under his skin, but it eased, just a little, knowing Angel wasn’t judging him. Wasn’t laughing at him. Wasn’t leaving.

“Very well,” Alastor said. “Let us return.”

Angel nodded, stepping close, but not too close. Careful now. Thoughtful.

And together, they stepped back into the chaotic streets of Pentagram City.

Alastor with his pride dented but intact.

Angel with his mouth miraculously, mercifully shut.

For the moment.

The city greeted them with its usual brutality; Blaring neon, screaming vendors, and the distant roar of something very large fighting something even larger. Under normal circumstances, Alastor would have navigated through it with jaunty ease.

But not now.

Every step felt too measured, too stiff, too carefully controlled. His cane tapped rhythmically, but the rhythm was tight, clipped at the edges. His posture— Always impeccable— Was now rigid to the point of strain. And Angel Dust, walking half a step behind, could see it. Alastor wasn’t gliding. He wasn’t sauntering. He was bracing himself before each step, aware that it was going to hurt him.

Angel chewed his lip, the silence between them stretching thin. He tried— He really did— To stay quiet. But, Angel Dust had never handled silence well, especially the uncomfortable kind. “You, uh… holdin’ up okay?” he asked softly.

Alastor didn’t look at him. “Marvelously.” Static crackled faintly at the edges of his words.

Angel winced. “Yeah, that’s the ‘I’m lying through my teeth’ voice. Just sayin’.”

Alastor’s smile twitched in warning. “Angel,” he said through that pleasant radio-static hum, “if you wish to remain a living creature, you will not—” He suddenly sucked in a sharp breath— Small, but unmistakable.

Angel froze mid-step.

Alastor had stopped walking entirely, one hand gripping his cane so tightly the wood creaked. His knees were drawn together, rubbing with a frantic sort of energy, his posture was still stiff but no longer elegant. His breath caught once, then steadied only by force of will.

Angel’s brows knit. “Al…”

“Keep walking.” Alastor’s voice was strained, stretched thin like an overloaded wire. “I simply need… A moment to collect myself.”

Angel didn’t move. “Do you… wanna sit down? Lean on something?”

“No.”

“You sure?”

“Yes.”

“…You really don’t look sure.”

Alastor’s eye twitched so sharply static burst forth. “Angel Dust.”

“Okay, okay! Shutting up again!” Angel took half a step back, giving him space.

Alastor inhaled slowly, recalibrating himself piece by excruciating piece. His expression was still smiling, but sweat beaded faintly at his temple—Something Angel had NEVER seen on him before.

After a long few seconds, Alastor managed to force his posture upright again. “Let us continue,” he said tightly. “The sooner we return, the better.”

Angel nodded, falling into step beside him again, but now he watched Alastor with the kind of close attention normally reserved for landmines.

The Radio Demon’s composure degraded steadily as they moved through the city. His usual swagger was gone; every few steps, he paused, breath catching, body tensing like he was fighting gravity itself.

Angel kept his voice down. “You don’t gotta pretend you’re fine, y’know.”

“I am managing,” Alastor hissed without venom, but with unmistakable strain.

“Barely,” Angel murmured.

Alastor didn’t argue. That alone was terrifying.

A group of demons stumbled out of a club ahead of them, laughing loudly and blocking the sidewalk. Normally, Alastor would simply shove through them with a charm-filled grin or teleport past. Now… he couldn’t afford sudden movements. Couldn’t risk anything jarring

Angel acted before he could think— Stepping in front of Alastor, all four arms snapping out. “HEY! Move it! Emergency here!”

One of the demons scoffed. “What kinda emergency—”

Angel shot him a glare so sharp the demon actually stepped back. “Buddy, if I say move, you move.”

The sidewalk cleared instantly.

Alastor didn’t comment, but his jaw clenched slightly.

They crossed the street, the hotel still blocks away. Alastor’s steps slowed more and more until he was almost shuffling. A soft, barely audible gasp escaped him when he stumbled over a crack in the sidewalk.

Angel’s concern spiked. This wasn’t just discomfort anymore. This was pain. “Hey,” he said gently. “Talk to me.”

“There is nothing,” Alastor began, “to discuss.”Another step. His knees buckled a fraction too far.

Angel reached out instinctively, not grabbing, just steadying, hovering a hand near Alastor’s back.

Alastor didn’t brush him off.

That alone told Angel everything.

“Al…” Angel’s voice dropped to a whisper. “We’re not gonna make it all the way at this pace. You’re hurting too bad.”

Alastor stared straight ahead, breathing shallowly, radio-static so faint it pulsed like a heartbeat. “…I am aware.”

“Then let me help.”

A long, painful silence. Angel expected resistance. Expected mockery. Expected Alastor to shove him away and claim he needed no one.

Instead, Alastor’s fingers tightened on his cane, knuckles pale. His voice, when it came, was barely a whisper. “…I do not know how.”

Angel blinked. The admission hit him harder than any insult Alastor could have ever delivered.“Okay,” he said softly. “Then we’ll figure it out together. One step at a time. I’m right here.”

Alastor shut his eyes for a moment. “…Very well.”

Angel stepped closer—Careful, gentle, ready to support him however he would allow.

And so, the Radio Demon continued the long walk home… No longer pretending the strain wasn’t real. No longer hiding how badly he needed help. And no longer walking alone.

They moved slowly, carefully, across the cracked sidewalk. Angel stayed close but not hovering, matching Alastor’s pace, watching his posture tighten with every step.

Alastor was unraveling in subtle, deeply un-Alastor ways— His breath hitching, shoulders tensed like wire, steps shortening until each one looked like a calculated risk.

Angel had never seen him this fragile. And by the look on Alastor’s face, he hated it more than anything.

“Almost there,” Angel murmured, even though they both knew they weren’t. “You’re doin’ good.”

Alastor’s hand twitched around his cane. “Flattery will not accelerate the process.”

“I ain’t flatterin’. I’m observin’.”

Alastor made a strange noise— Half a laugh, half a strained sound he immediately tried to swallow back.

Another step. Then another.

Then— He stopped. Sudden, absolute. A tremor ran through him so sharply that Angel froze, eyes widening.

“Al…?”

Alastor’s breath shook once— Just once— But, it was enough. “…This is untenable,” he whispered. “I cannot… I cannot maintain composure like this.”

Angel stepped closer, gently and carefully. “Hey. Hey, it’s okay. Talk to me.”

Alastor shut his eyes. His smile stayed in place, but it was brittle, cracking at the edges. “This situation,” he said slowly, “Is typically… Mitigated… With… A specific person’s assistance.”

Angel blinked. “Assistance?”

Alastor hesitated, the air around him thickening with embarrassment he could no longer mask. His radio-static softened into something low and tremulous. “…Yes.”

“From who?”

Another long pause. Then; “…Charlie. We have… A deal.”

Angel’s eyebrows shot up. “Charlie helps you?”

Alastor didn’t answer immediately. When he did, the words were rough with humiliation. “She… Has a calming effect,” he said tightly. “Enough to… To… Ease the physiological response that prevents me from—” He cut himself off, jaw clenching. “It is NOT ideal. I despise relying on it. But she is capable of helping me… Relax.”

Angel nodded slowly, not judging, just listening. “And it works?”

“…Yes,” Alastor admitted, voice barely audible. “Typically. She provides quiet reassurance. Stability. A grounding presence I find… Uniquely tolerable.” He swallowed, the motion sharp and uncomfortable.

Angel remained silent, expression softening with a mix of understanding and sympathy.

Alastor finally forced himself to look at him— Eyes narrowed more with shame than irritation. “This would be considerably easier,” he said in a low voice, “If she were able to arrive quickly and… Do what she normally does.”

“So you want me to call her?” Angel offered gently.

Alastor flinched. “No. Absolutely not.”

“Why not?”

“Because,” Alastor snapped quietly, “It is the middle of the night. You would be waking her up to deal with… THIS. I am already humiliated enough.“

Angel shook his head. “If this is somethin’ she’s helped you with before, she ain’t gonna show up pissed.”

Alastor sharply grabbed Angel’s wrist— Not enough to hurt, just enough to halt the conversation. His voice dropped to a trembling whisper; “Angel… I cannot walk much farther.”

Angel looked into his eyes.

Behind the smile—behind the static—Alastor was terrified. Not just of the pain. Not just of the need. But of the vulnerability. The veneer of his grin had never looked so thin. It wasn’t that he was losing control; Alastor NEVER lost control. It was that he was using every ounce of strength he had just to maintain it.

Angel held Alastor’s gaze for a long, quiet moment. The Radio Demon was trembling—minutely, but undeniably.

Angel had reached his limit. He couldn't just walk and hope. Alastor couldn’t keep going like this.

So Angel did what he HAD to. He slipped one hand into his jacket pocket and pulled out his phone.

Alastor’s eyes widened. “Angel Dust—”

Angel held up a hand. “Nope. I’m not askin’ permission. I’m tellin’ you. You need her.”

Alastor’s breath hitched sharply, panic flickering across his face— Not anger, not fury, but pure fear of exposure. “Do not,” he hissed through clenched teeth, “Call her. This is already— This situation is—Angel, I forbid—”

“You don't get to forbid this,” Angel said, voice quiet but steady. “Not when you’re in pain. Not when you’re scared.”

Alastor froze. That word—‘Scared’—shouldn’t have fit him. It should have ricocheted off him like a bullet off armor. Instead… It stuck. His mouth opened— Then shut again, as though any argument dissolved on his tongue.

Angel took a breath and tapped Charlie’s contact before he could back down.

The phone rang.

Twice.

Three times.

Four.

Then—

“Angel?” Charlie’s voice was groggy with sleep. “It’s the middle of the— Are you okay?”

Angel exhaled shakily. “Uh… Yeah. There’s been a— Uh— Situation.”

Alastor squeezed his eyes shut, face tightening with mortification.

Charlie’s tone immediately shifted from exhausted to concerned. “What kind of situation?”

Angel stepped slightly away from Alastor so the conversation wouldn’t feel too invasive, but stayed close enough to keep watch. “It’s Al,” Angel said softly. “The two of us went out. He’s… not doin’ too great.”

Charlie inhaled sharply. “Is he hurt?!”

“No,” Angel said quickly. “No, not hurt. Just… Uh… Y’know that thing you help him with?”

A brief pause on the line.

Then Charlie’s voice softened in instant understanding. “Oh. Oh. Oh no. Is it really bad?”

Angel looked over his shoulder. Alastor was bouncing on his toes, gripping his cane, chest rising and falling too quickly. His eyes were glassy, pupils contracted, posture taut with a misery that he couldn’t hide.

Angel’s voice dipped. “Yeah. He can’t walk much more. He’s really strugglin’.”

Charlie didn’t hesitate. “Where are you?”

Angel rattled off the nearest landmark.

Charlie gasped. “You’re that far out? Oh goodness— Angel, that’s at least a twenty-minute run from here if I sprint.”

Alastor winced as if the words were physical blows.

Charlie heard it— Her voice grew urgent. “Okay, okay. Don’t panic. I’m coming right now. I’ll take the side alleys— Those are faster. Just keep him calm, okay?”

Angel nodded, even though she couldn’t see. “Got it.”

“I’m hurrying,” Charlie added. “I’m on my way.”

Angel’s gaze drifted back to Alastor, who was standing rigidly, listening, mortified beyond belief.“…You want me to tell him that?” Angel murmured into the phone.

“Yes,” Charlie insisted. “He needs to know it’s almost over.”

Angel hesitated. “Alright. Just— Come fast.”

“I will,” she promised. “Don’t let him push himself.”

Angel hung up.

Silence settled for a moment.

Then he turned to Alastor.

The Radio Demon was staring ahead, smile frozen, but his eyes gave him away. He was glancing in all directions, eyelids twitching.

Angel tucked the phone away and approached him slowly—like approaching a wounded animal. “Al,” he said gently, “She’s comin’.”

Alastor inhaled sharply, a tiny sound, but one filled with devastation. “…At THIS hour?” he whispered.

Angel nodded. “She knows what’s happenin’. And she’s runnin’ to help.”

Alastor looked like the world had tilted under his feet. He pressed a trembling hand against his forehead, shoulders curled inwards in something dangerously close to shame.

Angel softened. He stepped close— Very carefully— And rested a light hand on Alastor’s arm. Not grabbing. Not guiding. Just steadying. “Al,” he murmured, “She’s helped you before. She cares. She WANTS to help.”

Alastor squeezed his eyes shut. “But not like… This. In the middle of the night. So inconvenient. So ridiculous, so—” Another tremor racked him.

This time Angel instinctively caught his elbow before he could sway. Alastor didn’t pull away.

Angel’s voice remained gentle but firm. “Listen to me. She isn’t gonna think less of you. And I sure don’t.”

Alastor swallowed hard, throat working. His voice, when it came, was the most vulnerable Angel had ever heard it: “…Please tell me she will be quick.”

Angel brushed his thumb reassuringly against Alastor’s sleeve. “She’s practically flyin’. She’ll be here any minute.”

Alastor nodded stiffly, too overwhelmed to hide the desperation anymore. It felt like every drop of water that had ever been in Hell was now contained inside of him, trapped with no possible way out. His static danced around him so loudly that his ears rang. He wondered now if he HADN’T stayed up late tonight after all, maybe this was a nightmare.

Any moment now, Vox was going to appear, he was going to begin laughing. “What’s the MATTER, Al?! Is the great Radio Demon ACTUALLY afraid of taking a piss?! Smile for the camera! Tell everyone how pitiful you are! If you want to go, you have to do it while I’m filming!”

Angel stayed close, keeping him steady as the minutes stretched on.

The sound of hurried footsteps echoed down the cracked sidewalk. Angel looked over his shoulder, squinting.

Charlie appeared in a blur of motion, determination radiating from her every step. She skidded to a halt beside Alastor with that familiar bright energy, though her eyes softened immediately when they met his.

“Alastor!” she said gently, voice firm but kind. “I’m here now. Everything’s going to be just fine!”

Alastor’s spine stiffened at the sight of her. He opened his mouth to protest, but the words caught in his throat. He couldn’t— Wouldn’t—speak. His pride, his usual composure, faltered entirely under the weight of raw, urgent desperation.

Angel gave him a small nudge with a grin that was half reassurance, half teasing: “See? Told ya she’d come. You’re in good hands.”

Alastor’s eyes flicked to Angel, a mixture of mortification and faint gratitude passing through them. Then, he turned back to Charlie. “N—Now?”

“That was part of the deal; No matter the time or the place, remember?”

He DID remember, of course. But, he’d never expected her to… Follow all of that so impeccably, especially without so much as a complaint. “I… I require assistance,” he whispered, barely audible. “To… Manage myself. Quickly.”

Charlie nodded, kneeling slightly so her voice stayed calm and even. “Of course. Let’s get you somewhere private.” She pointed to the nearest restroom— A clean little place tucked behind a small café. “Follow me, step by step. You’re not alone.”

Alastor followed stiffly, each step deliberate, each movement betraying the inner struggle Angel had seen grow over the last hour. Angel trailed behind, giving them space.

Once inside the restroom, Charlie guided him gently towards a stall. “Alright, here we are,” she said softly. “Lock the door, take a deep breath. I’m right here with you.”

Alastor’s fingers brushed against the latch before his hand froze. He looked at her, eyes wide— Not with fear, but with the vulnerability he never allowed anyone to see. “This… Exposure… Is… intolerable.”

Charlie gave him a small, reassuring smile. “I know. We’ll go slowly. Just follow my voice. That’s all I need right now. You don’t have to speak if you don’t want to.”

Alastor inhaled shakily, then exhaled. His voice was nearly gone, a whisper: “…Very well.”

Charlie began guiding him through the grounding exercise they had practiced before. “Alright, focus on your breathing,” she said. “In… And out… Nice and steady. Imagine your feet rooted to the floor, like you’re standing tall, even if everything else feels shaky.”

The muscles in Alastor’s shoulders were coiling and uncoiling. His breath was ragged, uneven.

Charlie’s voice remained calm, rhythmic, a gentle anchor. “Good,” she encouraged softly. “Now, feel the air on your face. The coolness, the warmth, whatever is there. Let it remind you you’re here, you’re present, and you’re safe. No one can bother you here.”

He trembled slightly, static humming faintly in the air around him, the usual vibrant energy that always seemed to demand attention now muted and tense.

Angel, standing quietly by the door, spoke softly as well, careful not to overwhelm. “You’re doin’ okay, Al. Just listen to her. You’ve done this before— Remember how good it feels to relax?”

Alastor’s eyes fluttered shut. His voice cracked, admitting what he rarely allowed himself to say; “…Yes… I… I remember… A little… The… Calm… It brings.”

Charlie nodded gently. “Good. Let’s use that memory. In through your nose… Out through your mouth… Steady. Feel each part of your body releasing tension as you breathe. Focus on what’s in front of you— My voice, the floor beneath your feet.”

Slowly, almost imperceptibly at first, Alastor’s rigid shoulders began to loosen. The tremor in his fingers lessened. His breathing, still shallow, began to lengthen and even out.

“You’re doing wonderfully,” Charlie whispered. “Just keep going. You’re safe here. No one else will listen. I don’t care what sounds you make.”

Alastor’s lips twitched— Not the confident, gleaming smile he always wore, but a small, fragile upturn of gratitude and relief. “…I… thank you,” he said, voice low and honest.

Angel, feeling the tension in the room lighten, allowed himself a small, relieved smile. “See? Told ya she’s the best.”

The two of them—Charlie and Angel— Stayed close, grounding him step by step. Slowly, painstakingly, Alastor’s body began to respond, tension melting away.

By the time Charlie finally said, “Alright, take a final deep breath. Open your eyes when you’re ready,” Alastor looked… Almost himself again. Still flushed from the ordeal, still trembling faintly, but calmer.

“…This… is far more tolerable,” he admitted quietly, eyes meeting Charlie’s briefly before flicking toward Angel. “Thank you… Both.”

Charlie smiled warmly. “Of course. That’s what I’m here for.”

Angel grinned, his usual energy soft for once. “Told ya. Teamwork, baby.”

Alastor exhaled slowly, a hum of static rising faintly around him. It was not as sharp or frantic as before. Nor was it as confident or commanding as usual. Just… Steady. Safe. Grounded.

For the first time in a long while, he allowed himself to simply… Be.

He inhaled slowly, counting each breath as Charlie’s soft guidance echoed in his ears.

“You’re doing wonderfully, Alastor,” she murmured. “Whenever you’re ready, you can take the next step. There’s no rush.”

Alastor’s chest tightened, then slowly released. “…I… Believe I am ready,” he whispered, voice still strained but quieter than before.

Charlie nodded, giving him a reassuring smile. “Good. You’ve got this. Just remember your breathing; Steady, in and out.”

Angel stood nearby, silently supportive, giving them space but ready to intervene if anything went wrong.

Alastor exhaled, long and deliberate, and finally allowed himself to move. The tension in his legs gradually eased as he complied with the grounding exercise: focusing on each sensation, each small movement, each sound. The tremor that had plagued his hands and shoulders ebbed, and a fragile calm replaced it.

“…It… Is… manageable,” Alastor murmured, almost to himself. His voice wavered faintly, but this time it carried relief instead of panic.

Charlie smiled gently. “See? You did it. You just needed a little support. That’s nothing to be ashamed of.”

Alastor’s eyes flicked toward her, hesitation written across his features. “…I… Am… Still not accustomed to… Assistance of this nature,” he admitted quietly. “But… I… See its… Merit.”

Angel gave him a playful, yet soft, nudge. “Not too bad, huh? You’re surviving. That’s a win.”

Alastor’s lips twitched, a hint of his usual wry smile breaking through. “…I suppose one may consider it… Survivable,” he muttered.

Charlie leaned slightly closer, her presence calm but warm. “You’re doing more than surviving. You’re allowing yourself to be vulnerable, and that takes far more strength than most realize.”

Alastor’s gaze softened slightly, the usual sparkling static of his demeanor subdued but not gone. “…I… Thank you,” he said in a low, earnest tone, almost stripped of theatrics for the first time in what felt like forever.

“You’re welcome,” Charlie replied softly. “And remember, it’s okay to ask for help when you need it. You don’t always have to be the one in control.”

Alastor exhaled slowly, his body at last giving way to the pure, genuine relief he had been craving for hours. The sound of it pattering into the bowl made his ears twitch, and he still trembled faintly, still carried the weight of embarrassment, but the pressure began to lift enough that he could think clearly again.

Alastor’s shoulders slumped, his posture relaxing for the first time since the ordeal began. He allowed himself to lean lightly on the wall of the stall as his bladder gushed its contents, a small, almost imperceptible sigh escaping him. “Ahhhh…”

Angel and Charlie exchanged a brief look— Relief and a little quiet triumph in their eyes. They had helped the Radio Demon find calm in a situation that had seemed impossible just moments ago.

Alastor allowed himself to simply exist. Not commanding, not performing, not projecting perfection. Just present, supported, and— Dare he admit it?— Grateful.

Once all of his liquid had finally been spent, his body still quivered faintly— An echo of the distress that had overtaken him just minutes ago.

When he left the stall, Charlie placed a gentle hand near his arm—Not gripping, not imposing, she knew he didn’t like being touched— Just ready to offer support. “Alright, Alastor,” she said softly. “You’ve done the hardest part. Now let’s make sure you can move safely. You must be sore…”

Alastor blinked, hesitating. “…I… Am not accustomed to being… Assisted so directly.”

“You’re doing great,” Charlie reassured him. “I’m just here to help you steady yourself.”

He sighed, a faint hiss of static accompanying the sound, and gave a small nod. “…Very well.”

Charlie guided him slowly, letting him adjust his posture gradually. Her hands hovered near his back and shoulders, ready to steady him if needed, but never touching unless he wavered. Every movement was deliberate, measured, designed to restore confidence in a body that had betrayed him.

Angel, still close by, gave a thumbs-up. “See? Easy peasy. You’re doin’ fine.”

Alastor’s lips twitched. “…I suppose I am… I’m surviving your commentary as well.”

Charlie gave him a small smile. “And now, let’s focus on your breathing again. In… And out… steady, even breaths. Keep your feet grounded. Feel the floor beneath you. You’re okay.”

Alastor followed her instructions cautiously. In… Out… In… Out… The tremor in his knees lessened. His posture gradually straightened, the stiffness in his shoulders releasing fraction by fraction. The static around him, once jagged and agitated, softened into a gentle hum that felt almost familiar.

“You’re doing beautifully,” Charlie whispered. “Take it one step at a time. There’s no rush.”

He let out a quiet, almost reluctant sigh, a sound of relief mixed with lingering tension. “…I… Had forgotten,” he admitted softly, “That such… Stability… Was attainable.”

“You’re remembering now,” Charlie said gently. “And you did it on your own, with just a little guidance. That’s important.”

Angel leaned in slightly, voice low and teasing but affectionate. “See? You’re practically normal again. Normal for you, anyways.”

Alastor’s eye twitched, not in irritation this time, but in the faintest acknowledgment of the humor. “…I am… Even able to tolerate your presence now, for the moment.”

Angel beamed. “Good enough for me, babe!”

*002*2026-03-31 01:36

Alastor prided himself on many things: his impeccable manners, his perfectly groomed attire, his unparalleled ability to reduce a room to stunned silence with nothing more than a smile. What he did not usually pride himself on was the state of his corporeal form—because, as far as he was concerned, the body was simply a vessel, and one he rarely had to think much about. Which is why he found it especially inconvenient that he woke up every morning with a very mortal, very bothersome need.

Alastor awoke in his suite just as he always did; Eyes snapping open, smile already fixed in place, radio static humming subtly beneath the air like an accompanying orchestra. His antlers caught the glow of the morning’s light.
