He stretched elegantly. Everything was going smoothly, until his body reminded him— Politely at first, then with a sharper nudge— That he needed to visit the restroom.

The Radio Demon adjusted his position as though it could fix the discomfort coiling low in his abdomen. He grimaced only internally. Outwardly, his grin remained plastered in place, if a bit more rigid than usual.

“Goodness,” he muttered to no one in particular, “Perhaps a bit too much to drink last night...” It sounded like a lie even to his own ears. Flashes of the dream he’d been having appeared in rapid succession. Another stupid Vox nightmare to add to the collection.

In this one, he’d somehow been stripped of all his magic and abilities, then captured by Vox. Vox soon discovered Alastor’s… ‘Stage-fright’, and broadcasted the news everywhere. So far, so ordinary.

But then, Vox had forced Alastor to drink until he was packed to the brim, and then informed him that the only way he was permitted to relieve himself was to do it on-camera during a live show. “Don’t worry, Al. My cameras are state of the art— Everyone will be able to see and HEAR you clearly!”

He’d needed to go… Urgently. So urgently that he couldn’t hold still no matter how hard he tried, but of course releasing his bladder under those conditions was entirely out of the question.

After such a dream, it was no wonder that he needed the facilities so terribly.

He rose from the bed with his usual flourish, cane materializing in his hand in a crackle of static. He strode towards the bathroom— Each step purposeful, and a tad quicker than normal.

But the instant he opened the door, an earsplitting, cheerful voice rang through the steam-fogged space; “Morning, Mr. Alastor!!”

Niffty, barely three feet of hyperactive chaos in a frilly dress, was standing on top of the sink, scrubbing the mirror with so much vigor that it was surprising the glass even survived.

The entire bathroom was in the middle of a deep, thorough, spring-cleaning-level assault. Towels were stacked in precarious towers. The cabinet doors were open. Several bottles of cleaning solution were lined up like soldiers.

And the toilet was… Unavailable. Utterly barricaded behind a mop bucket, a vacuum cleaner, and half a dozen boxes of disinfectant wipes.

Alastor’s smile twitched. This was not the time for one of her cleaning sprees, however admirable they normally were. He leaned slightly against the doorframe, posture casual, even though the pressure in his bladder pulsed with growing urgency.

“Niffty,” he greeted, voice smooth as ever despite the faint static crackle layered beneath, “To what do I owe the pleasure of this early-morning… Ah… Sanitation crusade?”

“Oh! I noticed things were SO dusty, and I just couldn’t stand it a second longer!” she chirped. “So I decided to take care of it for you! You’re welcome!” She beamed at him with the proud enthusiasm of a child presenting a macaroni sculpture.

Alastor swallowed. Calmly. Or, at least, he hoped it looked calm. “Ah. How very considerate of you.”He remained framed in the doorway, hands clasped behind his back in a posture of perfect composure— While internally, he was performing the emotional equivalent of pacing in small, frantic circles.

The bathroom was off-limits. Niffty was occupied. And there was absolutely no universe in which he would announce what he needed. He was the Radio Demon, for Hell’s sake. He did not… Disclose bodily inconveniences.

“How long,” he asked pleasantly, “Do you anticipate being?”

“Oh, not long!” she chirped. “Just scrubbing the tiles, reorganizing your cabinet, polishing the sink, deep-cleaning the drain, disinfecting the grout lines, sanitizing the—”

Alastor’s smile flattened microscopically. “I see.”

Niffty kept talking, blissfully oblivious. “Oh! Also, I found a dead roach under your soap dish! Isn’t that awful? But don’t worry, I—”

“I appreciate your diligence,” he interrupted, keeping his tone feather-light. “Please, take your time.”

And he meant it. He would rather spontaneously combust than admit to anyone that he badly needed to make use of the facilities. He knew the reluctance was… Maybe a little ridiculous. He had just woken up, after all. Using the restroom was one of the first things most people did each day. It was perfectly reasonable— Expected— That his bladder would be full now.

But, he wasn’t ’most people’… He was the Radio Demon.

“Okay!” Niffty hopped off the sink with a squeaky little noise, landing on the tile and immediately scooting towards a corner she deemed insufficiently spotless. “Shouldn’t take more than an hour!” she said cheerfully.

An hour.

Alastor’s smile flickered like a faulty lightbulb.“Splendid.”

He casually leaned on his cane. A microscopic tension pulled his shoulder stiff, but his smile remained steadfast.

Niffty, utterly oblivious, continued humming a chipper tune as she scrubbed with the fury of a creature whose natural predator was dust.

He backed away from the doorway, retreating with all the grace of someone not engaged in a private physical crisis.

The moment it latched, he let out a tiny, static-laced sound of frustration. The veneer of ease slipped. Not much, just enough that his shoulders stiffened and his pacing began. He crossed the room once, twice, cane tapping briskly on the floor.

“Completely manageable,” he told himself. “A trifling matter. Hardly worth note.”

His body disagreed.

He resisted the urge to dance in place, though one foot did tap— Anxiously, not rhythmically. His radio static crackled faintly, betraying his agitation.

His pacing was slow at first, then with increasing stiffness, hands clasped behind him as though deep in thought rather than mild agony, he sped up. Like moving faster would somehow cause time to pass more quickly for him.

He glanced towards the window. Opening it up and… Going was absolutely out of the question. This was his room, not a forest glade. Besides, the indignity alone—

No. He simply had to wait.

Waiting was one of his skills. He had decades of practice. Patience was a virtue that even Hell had not fully burned out of him.

He clasped his hands behind his back and attempted to stand still. The pressure sharpened again. His eye twitched.

Behind him, Niffty clattered something loudly in the sink. “Oops! Don’t worry, Mr. Alastor! Everything’s fine!”

He inhaled deeply through his nose. “Of course it is.”

Another minute passed. Then another. Alastor’s composure remained intact, but just barely. His smile didn’t falter, but his foot tapped faster, betraying what he would never say aloud. That he, Alastor the Radio Demon, Manipulator of Souls, Master of the Chaos… Really hoped Niffty would hurry up.

Alastor was, under most circumstances, the picture of collected elegance. His stillness could rival a statue’s; His patience could stretch on for ages without thinning. But, of course, patience behaves rather differently when paired with a physical need that couldn’t be put off forever.

He paced again.

Cane, step, step.

Cane, step, step.

His smile stayed firmly in place, but his gait had developed a subtle tension, barely noticeable, but present enough that if anyone who knew him well had walked in, they might have noticed.

Fortunately, no one did.

Unfortunately, Niffty was humming a cheerful cleaning tune loud enough to rattle the pipes. The sound carried through the entire suite. The clatter of scrubbing brushes, the squeak of tile polishing, the enthusiastic “Hup! Hup! HUP!” as she worked her way around the bathroom… Each sound was a reminder that the room he needed most was extremely, aggressively occupied.

Alastor inhaled deeply through his nose. “Utterly fine,” he murmured. “A minor inconvenience. Temporary.”

He tried sitting.

That lasted all of twelve seconds before he shot back to his feet, posture ramrod straight, static fizzing faintly beneath his breath.

Nope. Sitting made it worse.

Perhaps distracting himself would help.

He summoned his microphone cane to float before him and flicked it on; the red light glowed. The ever-present ambient static filled the room with a pleasant vintage buzz. He adjusted a dial, attempting to tune into one of the frequencies he maintained throughout the hotel’s common rooms.

“Good morning, listeners! A bright and cheerful dawn greets us today, full of surprises, misadventures, and—”

A sharp spike of discomfort cut through his abdomen, and his sentence ended in a barely concealed hiss of static.

He clicked the microphone off again.

“Perhaps… Something quieter.”

He walked to his bookshelf. Reading! That was dignified. Civilized. He pulled down a thick, dustless copy of Beasts of the Bayou: A Bestiary and flipped it open.

Page one.

He read the same paragraph three times and absorbed none of it.

Page two.

The static under his breath grew louder.

Page—

He snapped the book shut.

“This is ridiculous.”

Under no circumstances was he going to knock on the bathroom door and inform Niffty of the situation.

Absolutely not.

The pressure pulsed again, and his foot tapped rapidly against the carpet. “Stubbornness is a virtue,” he reassured himself.

He resumed pacing. His cane clicked against the floor in an increasingly frantic rhythm.

From the bathroom, Niffty called cheerfully, “Still at it, Mr. Alastor! There was way more grime around your faucet than I thought!”

Alastor froze mid-step.

Grime? Faucets? How in the name of Hell’s nine circles could there possibly be grime? He cleaned meticulously. He cleaned more cleanly than most cleaners cleaned!

He very nearly marched to the door to inform her of his cleaning schedule— But stopped himself. Pride and urgency wrestled in his chest, producing yet another electric crackle of static. He straightened, trying once more to appear composed. “Take all the time you need,” he called lightly, though his voice warbled ever so slightly on the final word. “I’m certainly in no rush!”

Another minute.

Another clatter.

Another pulse of discomfort.

Another circle around the suite.

He wasn’t panicking— Of course not! Alastor didn’t panic. He simply… Acknowledged that his situation was becoming more complicated than anticipated.

He adjusted his cane for the tenth time. He cleared his throat. He paced again. He debated briefly whether it would be possible to simply not need the bathroom anymore through sheer force of will.

His bladder disagreed.

Yet still, he would not speak up.

After all, he was Alastor. And Alastor endured. Even when endurance was becoming increasingly— And rapidly— Less appealing.

The sound of Niffty’s cheerful cleaning song had gotten louder, echoing through his suite. Something metallic clattered. Something else thumped. Water gushed, trickled, stopped, surged again. A scrub brush squeaked with relentless enthusiasm.

Alastor’s eye twitched in perfect time with each sound. He had retreated to the far end of the room, leaning against his bookshelf with the stiff, unnatural posture of someone attempting to appear relaxed despite being the furthest thing FROM relaxed. “How long,” he whispered to himself, “Can one possibly clean a bathroom?”

He checked the clock.

It had been five minutes since he had last checked.

It felt like five centuries.

He paced again. The cane tapped sharply: Click-click. Click-click. There was a rhythm to it, something almost frantic under the veneer of composure.

His gaze flicked towards the other half of the room where his bayou was. Just for a second. He quickly looked back away. But it only took a moment for his focus to return to it, and this time he lingered on it longer. Trees, grass… He’d… Made use of such things before.

Alastor’s ears lowered and he shook his head at himself. No, no, no! He couldn’t just… DO that! This was his room, he should avoid dirtying it. Besides, Niffty might finally come out before he was finished, see him standing there at a tree, his stance making it so obvious what exactly he was up to. “Mr. Alastor! What a bad boy you are! How am I supposed to clean a tree?!”

Alastor kept shaking his head. He wouldn’t. He couldn’t.

“Still working!” Niffty called from the bathroom. “I just found a little mold behind your shampoo bottle! Isn’t that horrible? But don’t worry—!”

Alastor stopped listening.

He was too focused on not thinking about how urgently he wanted her to be finished. A gentleman did not rush a lady performing a favor. A gentleman certainly didn’t admit discomfort over something so small and mundane.

But even his impeccable self-control was beginning to fray.

He straightened, attempting a calming breath. Static crackled anyway.

He tensed his hands behind his back and lifted his chin, adopting a posture that would have looked regal if not for the tiniest tremble in his knee. “Perfectly manageable,” he murmured. “An inconvenience, yes, but one I am more than capable of enduring.”

A splash sounded from the bathroom.

His smile went rigid.

He walked to his window, pretending to admire the view. In reality, he was very carefully not pacing. The skyline of Hell— Crooked, smoldering, always alive— Did little to soothe him.

He drummed his fingers against the window frame.

He hummed a tune.

Then: “Oops! Dropped the mop!”

The resounding crash that followed reverberated through the entire suite.

Alastor jumped. Only slightly. Barely noticeable. Maybe. He pressed fingertips to his temples, voice strained but still unnervingly cheerful.“Everything all right in there?”

“Yep!” Niffty chirped. “Just have to mop up a bit! Give me a few minutes!”

A few minutes.

He nearly groaned, but caught himself at the last millisecond. He inhaled sharply, shoulders rigid. Minutes were fine. Minutes were doable. Minutes were survivable. He began pacing again, even faster. “Compose yourself,” he muttered. “You are fear incarnate. You do not… Fidget.”

He very much fidgeted. He fidgeted a lot.

He realized this when he glanced in the mirror hanging near his bookshelf. His reflection showed him tapping his foot, shifting his weight, squirming his hips, cane clutched tightly in one hand.

He stopped pacing altogether and forced himself to stand perfectly still.

Ten seconds passed.

Fifteen.

Twenty—

He twitched violently and resumed pacing. His patience— Normally unshakable— Was wearing very, very thin. Dignity was becoming a slippery concept. Pride was battling physiology with all the fervor of a holy war, and physiology was gaining a whole lot of ground.

He paused, gripping the back of a chair. “You are fine,” he whispered. “This is fine. You have endured far worse.”

The bathroom faucet squealed, water gushing loudly. His static flared. This was becoming unbearable. He very nearly— VERY NEARLY— Took a step towards the bathroom door, ready to knock and politely inquire about her progress…

… But, he stopped himself at the last possible moment and slapped a hand dramatically over his mouth. No. Absolutely not. He would rather turn to dust.

Niffty was still humming. Still scrubbing. Still moving at the measured pace of someone under the impression that she had all the time in the world.

And Alastor simply stared at the bathroom door.

The door stared back.

His smile tightened.

If she didn’t finish soon, he feared something terrible might happen.

Like… Him admitting that he needed to ask a favor.

Soon, Alastor’s pacing had become a storm contained in a single room. His cane tapped the carpet with a frantic rhythm, each click-click a small drumbeat of growing urgency. His normally impeccable composure was starting to crack at the edges, just enough to make him vaguely aware that his “smile” now resembled a grimace stretched tight over a taut wire.

“Surely,” he muttered under his breath, “she will finish soon. Surely.”

But Niffty’s kept humming while scrubbing, occasionally clattering something metallic against the sink with gusto, and sang little improvised verses about soap and grime. Each sound struck Alastor like a bell tolling his personal agony.

He stopped mid-pace, eye twitching, hand pressed lightly to his forehead. A thought began to take shape inside his brain; A dangerous, terrifying thought. ‘Perhaps I could… Ask her to hurry?’

No. Absolutely not.

He straightened with a defiant puff of static and attempted to summon the familiar charm of his authority. “Niffty,” he said, stepping towards the door, voice cheerful but his tone a hair too sharp, “Might I inquire how… Thorough this cleaning will be?”

“Oh, Mr. Alastor!” Niffty chirped. “Just finishing the corners! Then I’ll do the mirror! And maybe reorganize your—”

Alastor froze. The words threatened to escape before he could clamp them down; ‘I need to use the facilities. Now.’ He clenched his jaw, smiled tighter, and blinked rapidly, buying time. “Yes,” he said instead, voice trembling slightly though it tried to maintain that upper-crust cheerfulness, “By all means. Take… All the time you… Require.”

Niffty, oblivious to his inner turmoil, turned back to the sink with renewed vigor, humming again. Alastor’s gaze shifted to the floor, then to the ceiling, then to the distant crimson carpet, as though hoping a miracle might appear.

‘I can endure this,’ he told himself, ‘I am a creature of patience, of unyielding composure, of infinite sophistication.’

Yet as he stepped back, another clatter from the bathroom made him wince so sharply he nearly stumbled over his cane. He gritted his teeth. “Perhaps a polite suggestion,” he muttered to himself, pacing again, “Might not be so ba— No. No! One does not— One does not admit to such mundane weaknesses! Not I. Not Alastor. Never.”

He paused mid-step, one clawed hand hovering in the air as if to gesture, as if the words might fall from his lips despite him. The tension coiled in his chest, sharp and undeniable, a tiny demon inside whispering: ‘Speak. Just ask. You’ll be free.’

And he almost… He almost did.

His jaw moved. His voice tried to form the syllables. He opened his mouth.

But the thought of Niffty looking up at him with a bright, expectant eye, smiling as though nothing could ever disturb her joy, froze him. Pride, stubbornness, and pure dignity held him in place.

He closed his mouth with a soft snap, shaking his head ever so slightly. “No,” he hissed, whispering to himself, “I will not. I shall endure.”

And so he resumed pacing. Faster now. Clicking cane against carpet, twirling it absently, glancing towards the bathroom door with every nerve in his body screaming.

Minutes passed. The pressure built. His smile remained, stretched tight but unwavering, while his eye twitched subtly, a telltale sign of his escalating inner turmoil.

“Good heavens,” he muttered under his breath. “How… Interminable… Is the pursuit of cleanliness?”

From the bathroom came another clatter. Another song. Another bright, cheerful verse. Alastor inhaled through his nose, steadied himself, and straightened. ‘I must remain dignified. I must endure. I shall not speak of… This. Not even once. Not today. Not to Niffty.’

“This is fine,” he told himself, voice pitched like a man attempting persuasion on an audience of one. “I am an ancient entity of immense power. I have endured far worse than a temporary delay.”

Another minute passed.

Then another.

Alastor stopped, pressed his gloved fingers to his temple, and exhaled sharply through his nose.

This was ridiculous.

He briefly considered using someone else’s bathroom— But, Angel’s was unspeakable, Husk’s was off-limits unless one enjoyed the scent of stale whiskey, and Vaggie’s… Well, being threatened with a spear while attempting to discretely relieve himself would be far more than anything his pride could tolerate.

So he adjusted his coat, straightened his tie (Which did not need straightening), and marched downstairs to the hotel lobby.

If he could not resolve his… Situation, he could at least distract himself.

Preferably with something that did not involve running water.

Unfortunately, the first sound he heard upon reaching the lobby was an aggressively loud glug-glug-glug from Husk refilling the bar’s ice machine.

Alastor’s eye twitched.

Just slightly.

“Morning,” Husk grumbled, not looking up.

Alastor’s smile was like brittle glass. “Good morning.”

The water finished pouring. Husk smacked the machine shut. Alastor pretended very hard that he was unaffected.

Then Charlie appeared from around the corner, cheerful as always, holding a watering can. “Alastor! Good morning! I’m about to water the lobby plants— Want to help?”

A faint pop of radio static burst around him. “…Perhaps another time, my dear.”

He excused himself before she could reply, gliding away with a little too much urgency for someone supposedly unbothered.

He just needed time. A distraction. Something— ANYTHING— That didn’t involve thinking about—

A sudden squeaky voice behind him: “Mr. Alastor! Bathroom’s gonna take a while longer, just so you know! That cockroach invited friends!”

He froze. Slowly turned. Niffty was hanging half-upside-down from the second-floor railing, waving a feather duster at him.

Every nerve in his body went rigid. “… Thank you, Niffty,” he said through a painfully tight grin.

“Just about halfway done!” she chirped before vanishing again.

Halfway.

An eternity.

Alastor inhaled deeply, electricity snapping faintly at the edges of his silhouette.

This was shaping up to be one of the worst mornings he’d had in decades.

And it was only getting started.

He stood in the hotel hallway, posture straight as a lamppost, hands clasped tightly behind his back, each one gripping the other firmly. He needed to put them— No, just WANTED to put them between his legs. But they were holding each other back. He’d never…

From the outside, he looked as composed as always. Inside, however, his thoughts were beginning to resemble static-filled screaming. “Patience,” he told himself softly. “Poise. Self-control.”

He began pacing again.

It helped for all of ten seconds.

Every now and then he paused, glanced towards his room upstairs, and exhaled like someone trying very hard not to sprint. If anyone else were watching, they’d think he was contemplating some grand, sinister plan.

Really, he was contemplating how large his bladder could reasonably be, given how incredibly narrow his abdomen was…

A rapid tap-tap-tap of heels approached from behind.

“Hi again, Alastor!” Charlie chirped, carrying a cardboard box overflowing with pamphlets. “I’m working on new brochures for our guest welcome packets! I could really use your input!”

Alastor stiffened. “Oh? My input? How delightful.”

Charlie brightened and set the box down. “Great! I have a few designs to show you. The first one is—” She reached for the top sheet, but the entire stack slid forwards and toppled out like a paper avalanche. “Oh, oops!”

Hundreds of pamphlets spilled across the floor in a sweeping cascade of glossy pages.

Alastor bent to help, instantly regretting it. His entire body seized so stiffly he looked like he was malfunctioning. He froze halfway to reaching for a pamphlet, smile stretched tight across his face.

Charlie blinked. “Are you… Okay?”

“Perfect,” he replied, voice pitched unnaturally high before he caught himself. “Simply… Admiring the spread.”

Charlie giggled. “You don’t have to pretend you like them if you don’t—”

“No, no. I adore them. Tremendously. Outstanding work.” He gathered a handful of papers in a swift, mechanical motion and deposited them back in the box with near-frantic precision. "My dear Princess," he purred, his voice smooth as velvet, "I think these are simply glorious. Positively dazzling. Why, I could gush for hours.”

He paused, flinching. His ears pressed atop his head, protesting his own poor word choice. Oh, how he'd LOVE to gush for hours! Even just ten seconds would feel so wonderful...

The pressure amplified again.

Charlie tilted her head. “You’re acting a little… Tense.”

Alastor froze. His static crackled faintly. “Tense?” he echoed with exaggerated cheer. “Me? Perish the thought! I am the very image of morning serenity!”

“Alastor,” Charlie said softly. “Remember our deal… Do you nee— WANT to use the bathroom right now?”

“I… My dear, that would be most apprecia—“

“ALASTORRRR!” Angel Dust sauntered down the stairs, stretching like a cat. “You look like you’re about to snap in half. That tie too tight or somethin’?”

Alastor’s eye twitched. “Quite the contrary. I feel splendid.”

Angel snorted. “Yeah, sure.”

“Angel,” Charlie said. “Alastor and I were discussing something. Alastor…”

But the moment was gone. Alastor abruptly waved his hand, producing a burst of static that nearly knocked over a potted plant. “HA! Ha-ha! And a fine discussion it was! But I assure you, I am quite well, ahaha—“

He trailed off mid-laugh. A sharp crease formed between his brows. Standing still wasn’t helping. Moving wasn’t helping. Nothing was helping. He was, quite genuinely, running out of composure.

Angel squinted at him. “Dude, are you sure you’re okay? You’re kinda—” he mimed vibrating “—Buzzin’ more than usual.”

Alastor cleared his throat. “I am fine. Just… a touch preoccupied.”

“Preoccupied with what?”

He hesitated. “Business,” he said smoothly. “A very urgent… Personal matter.”

Charlie nodded sympathetically.

“Uh-huh,” Angel said. “You sure LOOK like you might explode.”

“Indeed,” Alastor replied dryly. “That is… Precisely the issue.” He turned on his heel before either of them could offer assistance again, walking away with a gait that was far too brisk to be casual.

As he reached the stairwell— Trying very hard to look normal, and succeeding only in looking utterly suspicious— He froze again. Stairs… Fuck, his bladder HATED those!

And he couldn’t even focus well enough to use his magic and skip over them…

He ascended the staircase one careful step at a time, shoulders tense, breath held, entire being focused on perseverance. He could do this. He would do this. He had faced torment before. This too would pass.

He made it to the second floor with the regal bearing of someone walking towards a coronation… Or a firing squad. Hard to tell which.

He entered his room and paused, hands clasped tightly behind his back, shoulders pulled straight in a posture so stiff it was nearly creaking.

From behind the bathroom door, Niffty’s chipper humming echoed like the soundtrack to his slow demise.

Scrub-scrub-scrub.

Hummm-hum-hum-HUM!

Scrub-scrub.

Every stroke sounded like taunting.

Alastor inhaled— Slow, measured, as though he could breathe away the mounting discomfort coiling in his torso like a vengeful snake. “Composure,” he whispered to himself. “Poise. A gentleman does not… panic.”

He stood there for a dignified ten seconds.

Then began bouncing on his toes and pacing around again.

He paced with all the grace of a man trying to convince the universe that he had not reached a breaking point. He pivoted sharply at the end of the room, turning on his heel like a performer hitting stage marks. But, each step grew a little quicker, a little shorter, a little less composed.

A bead of static flickered off him. He ignored it. He passed the mirror. Saw himself. And froze.

His smile— Usually polished and unwavering— Had gone utterly brittle, strained at the corners, more grimace than grin. His eyes had that wild radio-static buzz around the edges, as though a bad signal had taken root in his skull.

He cleared his throat, smoothed his coat, straightened his tie, and forced the smile back into something resembling normalcy.

“Pull yourself together,” he muttered.

He lasted three seconds before he started frantically pacing again.

There was a resounding knock on the door.

Alastor jolted so hard he produced a burst of static that rattled all the light fixtures in the room.

“Hey, Al!” Angel Dust’s voice called. “You left your cane downstairs!”

Alastor turned so sharply he nearly tripped over his own feet. He opened the door and snatched the cane from Angel. “Thank you, my good fellow!”

Angel blinked, looking him over. “Uh, Al? You’re in your own room, why haven’t ya— Aw, jeez, your problem ain’t gettin’ worse, is it?”

“I haven’t any idea what you mean! Why haven’t I what?”

“You KNOW what, Al… I figured it’d be easy for ya to go HERE, but you’re still all—“

There was a clatter from the bathroom, followed by Niffty shouting; “HOLD STILL, ROACH!”

“Ohhh,” Angel said. “Niffty’s cleanin’ it… I can tell her I’M the one that’s gotta go so that she gets out. Will that help?”

“Nonsense!” Alastor barked, voice cracking like a damaged vinyl. “There is NOTHING to help with. I just— Ah— Never mind that! Good day!”

He tried to dismiss Angel with a crisp wave, but the motion was too fast, too fraught, and his smile quivered again.

Angel squinted “You’re sweatin’.”

“Preposterous. I do not sweat.”

Angel pointed. “Then explain that.”

Alastor looked down. His suit was a bit… Damp around his armpits. He laughed. Too loudly. “Just a minor… Power fluctuation!”

Angel stared for a moment, then shrugged. “Whatever. But if ya want me to go get Charlie, just say so.”

As Angel wandered off, Alastor sagged against the wall for exactly one second before bolting upright again, refusing to give even gravity the satisfaction of seeing him weakened.

“I stabbed ALL the roaches, now I just gotta clean up the guts!” Niffty’s shrill voice rang out from the bathroom. “Just five more minutes!”

Alastor closed his eyes. He counted backward from ten. At “three,” his left eye twitched. At “two,” a faint buzz filled the air. At “one,” he clicked his heels together and forced himself to stand perfectly still.

Five minutes. He could endure five minutes.

He was the Radio Demon; One of Hell’s most fearsome overlords. An embodiment of power and precision and— And… Oh, goodness, he needed the bathroom NOW. His sides were beginning to ache, the sharp point of pressure at his opening felt electrified, his bladder was like a massive stone of ever worsening urgency…

He shifted weight subtly. Very subtly. Barely a whisper of movement. The kind only he would notice.

He pretended that it had helped.

It had not.

His fingers tightened around his cane to the point that it creaked under the pressure.

“Almost done!” Niffty chirped behind the door, completely oblivious. “Just tackling these tiles!”

Alastor inhaled through gritted teeth. He was losing this battle. Not physically—He could hold out longer if it came to it. No, the battle he was losing was with his pride.

His breaking point approached in a series of small humiliation. First, he stepped wrong and jolted, nearly dropping his cane. Then, his smile quivered for a full second before he snapped it back in place. Finally, his legs crossed and one hand pressed itself between them for one full second before he was able to pry it back away.

Alastor stared at his bathroom door like a man negotiating with fate. “Niffty, my dear,” he called, keeping his tone light, “Might I inquire as to your progress?”

“Almost done! Just polishing the sink!”

Polishing the sink. Of course.

He closed his eyes again.

This was torture.

Actual torture.

The kind demons like him invented, but never imagined they’d have to endure.

“Done!” Niffty finally announced.

Alastor’s entire body went rigid. His eyes widened. His cane clattered to the ground. But he did not move, not yet, “How… Marvelous,” he said softly.

Niffty blinked. “You okay?”

He did not answer. Because if he spoke, he might give himself away. He just stood utterly still— So still he looked carved from marble— While Niffty swung the bathroom door wide open with an energetic flourish.

“There!” she chirped. “Spotless! Not a germ in sight! You can practically EAT off the toilet now—Oh, but probably don’t— Actually, DEFINITELY don’t—” She stopped mid-ramble. Because Alastor looked… Weird.

His shadow twitched behind him like it was trying to escape his body. His smile had become a too-smooth veneer over something fraying at the edges.

Niffty tilted her head. “…Mr. Alastor?”

No response. Just a faint crackle of static and the twitch of one gloved fingertip.

She blinked again, glanced at the bathroom, then back at him. Then her eye widened. “Oh,” she whispered.

Alastor’s eyelid twitched. He wasn’t sure which he feared more; Niffty misunderstanding, or Niffty understanding perfectly.

She stepped closer, small heels clicking across the freshly scrubbed tile. “Mr. Alastor…” she said gently, in a tone she only used when she’d broken something irreplaceable or when someone else was clearly very unwell. “Do you… Have to use the bathroom?”

Alastor’s smile flashed dangerously bright. “What an odd assumption,” he said airily, voice trembling only a little at the end. “Of course not! I was simply— Ah— Admiring your fine work, how wonderf—“

“Uh-huh.” She crossed her arms, giving him a long, knowing stare. “Silly Alastor! Why didn’t you just SAY something if you have to go so bad?!”

Alastor jerked as though he’d been stabbed. “Because—“ he sputtered, then instantly hated himself for nearly sounding flustered. He straightened, smoothing his coat. “Because, my dear Niffty, it was hardly important enough to interrupt your… Ah… Enthusiasm!”

Niffty squinted up at him. “But, you look like you’re gonna make a big, messy puddle if you don’t go RIGHT NOW.”

“I most certainly do not!”

“You’re standing like someone who got hit with a broomstick!”

“My posture is impeccable.”

“And you kept making that weird, buzzy noise!”

“That is simply my charm.”

She stared.

He stared.

His smile warbled. “… Alright,” he muttered, dropping his voice an octave, “…Perhaps I DO… Require the facilities. Slightly.“

Niffty blinked. “You mean you were doing the I-Really-Really-Gotta-Go dance this whole time but PRETENDED you weren’t?!”

Alastor’s face went completely expressionless for a moment— The kind of stillness that suggested he had briefly ascended to a plane of pure denial.“It was not a dance,” he said stiffly. “It was pacing. Thoughtful. Dignified pacing.”

She put a tiny hand on her hip. “Why didn’t you just ask me to hurry up? Or take a break? I would have! Or, why didn’t you use another bathroom? Why’d you wanna hurt yourself?!”

Alastor inhaled sharply through his teeth. A crackle of static flitted through his silhouette. “Niffty,” he said with the strained politeness of a man morally obligated to maintain dignity even while internally screaming, “There are certain matters that do not require… Broadcast.”

She blinked. Then nodded, eye sparkling as she grinned. “Awwwww! Mr. Alastor’s shy! How cute!”

Alastor made a tiny sound that may have been the last fragment of his pride disintegrating. “Niffty,” he hissed under his breath, “There is nothing… ‘Cute’ about this!”

She didn’t respond. She just stepped aside, ushering him towards the now-pristine bathroom with both arms and a small, apologetic bow. “It’s okay! Go on, then!”

He froze. Again.

She stared at him expectantly. And then— It hit her. Her eye widened. “Ohhhh! You want privacy. Right, right! Sorry! Aww, you’re really, REALLY shy!” She practically teleported three feet back, hands shooting behind her as though resisting the urge to polish something else. “I’ll just— Uh—stand over here and NOT look and NOT listen and NOT talk!” she promised.

Alastor did not waste another second. He retrieved his cane, straightened his coat, nodded once with what little dignity remained, and walked— No, glided— Into the bathroom with forced calm.

The door shut behind him.

The instant the latch clicked, Niffty heard a muffled burst of radio static that sounded suspiciously like a demon letting out the most relieved sigh in the infernal plane.

She blinked. Then giggled behind her hands. “Cuuute…”

Niffty lingered outside the closed bathroom door, wringing her hands. Despite her earlier giggles, the longer the silence stretched on, the more her expression shifted from amused to… Worried.

She rocked back and forth on her heels, peering at the door every few seconds. “Shouldn’t he be all done by now?” she whispered to herself. She leaned closer. Then closer. Then closer—

The door crackled with a warning burst of static.

Niffty jumped back with a squeak. “Okay! Okay! Not listening! Not listening!” She paced in front of the door, tiny footsteps pattering rapidly across the floor.

She’d never seen the Radio Demon look like that; So stiff and anxious he could have snapped in half. And for something so… Normal. Something demons usually didn’t even think twice about. In Hell, privacy wasn’t exactly standard.

But Alastor was different. Private. Polished. Particular. And today, he’d looked like a porcelain teacup perched on the edge of a wobbly shelf.

Niffty gnawed her lip. She crouched, stood, crouched again, then hovered by the door with anxious energy shaking her whole tiny frame. “Mr. Alastor?” she called softly. “You okay in there?”

No answer. Just faint rustling. And the whisper of static.

Her brows knit together. She pressed her hands to her cheeks. “Oh gosh… What if he got dizzy? What if he fainted? What if he overloaded his powers from trying to hold everything in and now he’s a puddle of static on the floor?!”

Her imagination immediately produced the mental image. She gasped aloud, horrified. “Oh nooooo…”

She knocked very gently. “Just checking! Not listening, I promise! Do you need… Water? No, wait, not water. Definitely not water...” She twisted one of her arms nervously. “You’ve been in there a while… That’s all.” She paused. Then she whispered, much softer; “I’m… kinda worried about you.”

Inside the bathroom, Alastor heard every word. He stood at the toilet, trying desperately to get something out of his bladder, to let the stream he’d been containing flow like a mighty river.

But, mighty rivers made NOISE. And Niffty was out there…

This was absurd. Why was he rattled? Why did a perfectly normal need make him feel like he’d been caught doing something scandalous?

He adjusted his bowtie again. It was already perfect.

Static popped faintly off his shoulders.

“…Ridiculous,” he muttered. “I am not… Fragile.”

Yet when Niffty’s small voice trembled through the door— “I’m kinda worried about you.”— His expression softened.

He looked at the door, troubled. Niffty wasn’t judging him. She wasn’t laughing. She was genuinely distressed. He sighed. Quietly.

Then he smoothed his coat and admitted; “Just a little… Blocked, dear.”

“You mean you can’t go?”

“… Yes, that’s the, ah… Issue.”

“But if you don’t go, you’ll explode. And then I’ll have to clean up all your exploded guts off the walls, and—“

“I won’t explode,” Alastor said, trying to keep his tone even despite the mortification of the words. “I’m just… Uncomfortable.”

“How do I make you comfy?”

“Just… Go wait out in the hall for now, my dear. My body shall be more… Agreeable if no one is directly outside the door.”

“Okay!” Niffty said, followed by the sound of tiny feet skittering and a door being opened and shut.

Privacy.

Solitude.

And all he’d had to do was… Ask for it. He just had to say the words…

This would have been over AGES ago if such words weren’t so HARD to say… If he’d been able to tell Niffty to pause her cleaning so he could go. If he’d been able to take Charlie up on her offer of help. If he’d been able to ask Angel to go get Charlie for him…

But, of course he hadn’t been.

He would have spontaneously combusted.

However, just like he’d assured Niffty, he didn’t explode. Not really, anyway… The eruption of liquid that started to gush out of him as soon as he’d settled himself probably could have been considered an explosion, but it was a welcome one. A pleasant one. An oh-so-relieving one. He tipped his head back. “Ahhh…”

It was okay to moan in here, in his own private restroom. He could be as loud as his body wanted him to be. He could urinate thunderously. He could sigh in satisfaction. He could relax. This was his private, personal space where he was free to let most of his walls down.

“Phew… That’s better,” he remarked to himself once he was finally, utterly drained. He took a moment to make himself presentable again before heading back to the main room and then out into the hallway.

Niffty leaped back like she’d been caught spying, hands flying behind her. “HI! I mean— Hi. I wasn’t listening! I was just— Um— Thinking! Loudly! In my head!”

Alastor straightened to his full height, and tapped his cane on the tile. His usual poise was back in place… But, his smile was gentler than before.“Niffty,” he said quietly, “I appreciate your concern. Truly.”

Her eye widened. “You… Do?”

He dipped his head in a small, respectful nod. “I apologize for alarming you. I assure you, I am quite well now. Completely refreshed.”

Niffty blinked at him, then relaxed, her little shoulders sinking with relief. “Oh, thank goodness. You looked so uncomfortable earlier I thought you might burst or something! And you weren’t saying anything and I didn’t wanna pry and I wasn’t sure if you were upset with me or just shy—”

He held up a hand, stopping her rapid-fire ramble. “I was… Not upset. Merely… Distressed. Physically.” He added, voice softer; “And you need not worry so much over me.”

Niffty smiled “I’m gonna anyway.”

Alastor froze. Static flickered around him again, but this time… Warm. Still embarrassed, but not frantic. “…Ah,” he murmured. “I see.”

“You don’t have to tell me stuff. Not if you don’t want to. But… If something’s wrong, you can say so.”

Alastor looked down at her thoughtfully. Then he placed one hand over his heart in a gesture of formal gratitude. “You are remarkably perceptive, my dear. I thank you.”

Niffty beamed.

And Alastor— Feeling oddly lighter, even if mortified— Allowed himself a small, genuine chuckle.

“Well then,” he said, voice returning to its usual theatrical cadence, “Shall we pretend none of this ever happened?”

Niffty saluted with comical seriousness. “You got it!”

But as Alastor walked past her, she whispered behind her hand: “…Still glad you’re okay, though.”

He heard it. And he didn’t even pretend that he hadn’t.

*004*2026-04-06 14:39

The afternoon in Hell was unusually bright, almost offensively so. The light shimmered against the cracked sidewalks outside the hotel, reflecting off puddles of sulfurous water and the neon signs of nearby vice dens beginning to flicker awake for the evening rush.

Alastor stepped out from the front doors with his usual buoyant stride, hands clasped behind his back, a smile sharp enough to cut glass pasted across his face.

Behind him, Husk trudged along with the enthusiasm of someone who’d been talked into leaving his bar nook under threat of severe harm.

“Tell me again,” Husk muttered, wings ruffling, “Why I had to come with you?”

“Because,” Alastor replied, radio static crackling pleasantly behind each syllable, “Charlie has requested— Quite insistently— That I partake in more ‘healthy social interactions.’ And since you were the nearest warm body, here we are!”

They walked towards an area where ruined factories had been repurposed into a maze of markets, bars, and gambling holes. “Just a stroll,” Alastor said, “Nothing more. A chance to observe the denizens of our lovely underworld and enjoy a moment of fresh— Well, relatively fresh— Air.”

Husk lit a cigarette. “Right. A walk. Fantastic.”

Despite Alastor’s ceaseless cheer, Husk noticed that the other demon’s movements were a bit... Tight. His shoulders stiff. His steps not quite the easy, drifting glide he normally displayed.

“You okay?” Husk asked, not because he cared deeply, but because Alastor being even slightly off felt like the end times.

“Of course!” Alastor chirped. “Never better!”Static popped again, but this time the tone wavered— Briefly— Just long enough for Husk to pick up on it.

They passed a row of vendors hawking everything from nightmare fruit to demon teeth charms. Alastor’s smile faltered for half a second as he glanced past the stalls towards a narrow brick alleyway leading into a small plaza.

And in that plaza sat a public restroom.

Husk blinked. “Oh. That’s what’s up? You gotta—“

Alastor stiffened. “I beg your pardon?”

“You need a leak.”

“I most certainly do not!”

But even as he insisted, he adjusted his posture just slightly— An imperceptible motion to most, but Husk had seen enough drunks pretend they were fine to recognize the move instantly.

“Dude,” Husk said flatly, smoke curling from his cigarette, “It’s fine. Just go.”

“It is not ‘fine.’ And I do not… Just ‘go.’” Alastor scoffed as if the very concept were beneath him. “Such base bodily functions are hardly—”

“A surprise,” Husk interrupted. “Even the Radio Demon has organs, y’know.”

Alastor sniffed. “Physical vessels are merely containers for the soul’s—”

“Yeah, yeah. But you gotta take care of the container sometimes.”

The Radio Demon’s jaw tightened.

They stood at the edge of the plaza. The restrooms sat across it like a taunt. A couple of demons lingered nearby— Loiterers, loud, boisterous, and absolutely impossible to ignore.

Alastor’s smile thinned. His fingers twitched.

“Oh,” Husk realized. “Right. You have… That thing. The shy-bladder thing Charlie mentioned.”

Alastor froze, the smile cracking for real. “She told you?”

“No, but I overheard her telling Niffty to stop calling it ‘cute’. Look, man, it ain’t a big deal.”

“For you.” Alastor glanced towards the cluster of demons, then immediately looked away again. “I am simply unaccustomed to… Performing under observation. It is inconvenient. And impolite. And terribly inconsiderate of the universe to place restrooms in such public spaces.”

“It’s Hell,” Husk deadpanned. “We don’t get privacy. We get screaming, blood, and torment.”

Alastor huffed. “Even so.”

Husk flicked his cigarette away, crossing his arms. “Okay. Wanna find a quieter spot?”

Alastor’s eyes darted again— Towards the building, towards the noisy demons, then down, ever so slightly, at the ground. “That would be… Preferable,” he admitted in a barely audible tone. The radio static behind the words trembled— Like a weak frequency struggling to hold.

Husk raised an eyebrow. “Wow. A crack in the mask. Mark your calendars.”

Alastor shot him a sharp, warning look— But, rather than snapping back with his usual flair, he only sighed, letting the forced grin settle into something more controlled. “Let us proceed,” he said quietly. “Quickly.”

They circled around the plaza, trying to look casual. Husk kept a small distance, making sure not to crowd him.

It didn’t help Alastor much.

Every time they turned a corner, another small group of demons seemed to appear— Laughing, drinking, shouting, taking up space with their noise and presence. Alastor stiffened each time. His breathing, though controlled, was shallow.

Husk noticed his hands twitching— Small, compulsive gestures, like he was tuning an invisible dial to calm himself. The hum of static behind him rose and fell, erratic at the edges. “You sure you’re good to go?” Husk asked, softer this time.

Alastor didn’t answer at first. His eyes were fixed ahead, his smile brittle. “Please don’t say ‘go’…”

“Sorry, man. Are you alright?”

“It is profoundly irritating,” Alastor murmured. “To be hindered by something so trivial. So… Human.”

“It’s just anxiety. Happens to plenty of demons.”

Alastor gave him a sideways glance— Surprised, maybe, or just thrown off by the casual tone.

Husk shrugged. “I’ve had it. Angel’s had it. Probably half the hotel’s got some kinda issue like that. Hell’s not built for comfort.”

Alastor exhaled, long and low. “I dislike being vulnerable,” he said at last.

“Yeah,” Husk replied, “No kidding.” But, instead of mocking him, Husk reached out and lightly swatted Alastor’s shoulder. “C’mon. We’ll find somewhere empty.”

Alastor didn’t argue, he simply followed.

The two made their way deeper into the area, weaving through narrow brick passages that smelled faintly of saltwater, rust, and a thousand unidentifiable toxins. Husk stayed half a step in front.

Alastor kept his hands clasped behind his back, posture upright, but the way his ears flicked and stilled and flicked again betrayed his nerves. The static in his wake had softened to a quiet buzz; A signal barely hanging onto frequency.

They approached a side street lined with old fish-processing warehouses. Most had collapsed or crumbled, leaving toothlike rows of pillars and scattered shadows.

Husk gestured. “What about there? Looks empty.”

Alastor peeked around the corner.

A demon couple was pressed against a wall, noisily engaged in something Alastor would have preferred not to witness. “I refuse to conduct personal business in the vicinity of… THAT.”

“Yeah, fair,” Husk muttered, turning away quickly. “Next.”

They walked on.

The next alley was quieter, but a small group of demons lingered nearby, drawing sigils on the ground with glowing chalk, giggling whenever one sparked too brightly.

Alastor stiffened again. “Absolutely not.”

Husk rubbed his temples. “Okay. Keep moving.”

After a couple more minutes, they ducked through a rusted doorway into what had once been a cold-storage room. The floor was cracked, the overhead chains swayed with an eerie clink, and frost still rimmed parts of the wall in stubborn patches.

“Creepy,” Husk said. “But private.”

Alastor stepped inside. He waited. Listened. His ears twitched as he scanned the space for movement, breath hitching ever so slightly.

Then… voices from outside; Laughing. Passing by.

Not entering, not even close— But close ENOUGH that Alastor immediately straightened, withdrawing like a spooked deer. “No. Too exposed.”

Husk stared at him. “They’re across the street.”

“Yes,” Alastor said tightly. “And should they get closer, the acoustics here would be… Unfortunate.”

Husk blinked. “You’re worried about the acoustics?”

Alastor sniffed in indignation. “I will not have my bodily noises echoing like a haunted kettle.”

“Okay,” Husk sighed, dragging a wing down his face, “We’ll keep looking, then.”

Past another pair of closed storefronts and a collapsed loading dock, the streets became quieter. The noise of the market faded to a distant murmur, replaced by the pale whirr of flickering street lamps.

Husk’s feathers settled as the air grew still. “This part of town’s dead. Good sign.”

Alastor nodded, though his smile remained tight. The tension in his shoulders hadn’t eased, but the buzzing static around him softened— Still uneasy, but no longer jagged.

They slipped into a side alley where a crooked metal staircase led up to a second-story service platform.

“Try up there,” Husk suggested.

Alastor glanced at the stairs, their rusted rungs, the precarious landing. “… Yes, perhaps.” He ascended carefully, and Husk followed until they reached the small landing above. It overlooked the alley but was shielded on three sides by old metal siding and a collapsed sign.

Alastor scanned the environment meticulously: No demons in sight. The sound from the main street was distant, only the faint hum of broken neon lights. Husk was standing below the landing, not on it His posture eased—just slightly, but enough for Husk to notice.

“This may suffice,” Alastor murmured.

Husk leaned against the railing, deliberately turning his back. “I’ll look the other way. No rush. No one’s coming.”

Alastor took a slow breath through his nose.

Then another.

He removed his cane from his arm, leaning it gently against the wall, the act feeling startlingly vulnerable for a demon who carried it like a scepter.

Husk kept silent, giving him space.

Alastor stood there for several long seconds, hands at his sides, trying to center himself. His eyes half-closed.

And then…

A faint noise drifted up from the alley; The sound of someone rummaging through a trash bin half a block away.

Alastor froze instantly.

Husk tensed. “It’s just a scavenger. They’re nowhere near us.”

“It’s ENOUGH,” Alastor whispered, voice thin. The static behind him crackled sharply, then fizzled into a faint hum of discomfort. His ears curled inward. Shoulders hunched a fraction.

Husk exhaled slowly. “Alright,” he said quietly, “We can go deeper. There’s an old shipyard past here. Real quiet. Hardly anyone wanders there, except me when I need to clear my head.”

Alastor hesitated… Then gave a single, small nod.“Lead the way.”

They walked in silence. The buildings grew more decrepit, more skeletal. Lamp posts leaned at odd angles. The sea— If one could call Hell’s black, boiling shore a “sea”— Whispered distantly, muffled by layers of smog and oily mist.

Husk could feel Alastor’s tension gradually shifting, from sharp panic to something more controlled, more hopeful, as they moved further from civilization.

“Almost there,” Husk said, jerking his chin toward a large rusted structure ahead.

Alastor looked up.

A derelict dock sat nestled between two cliffs of abandoned warehouse shells. Its massive walls shielded it from view on all sides. A lonely walkway stretched across the top, battered by time but still intact.

Alastor’s ears perked. The static dimmed. His posture finally, finally loosened.

Husk couldn’t help but smile a little. “Told ya. Nobody comes here. Too quiet, too dark, too depressing. Perfect for— Uh— Whatever you need.”

Alastor let out a slow breath, relief seeping in. “Yes… Yes, this might actually do.”

Husk stopped walking. “You want me to stand guard somewhere? Far enough not to bother you?”

Alastor looked at him, not with annoyance, but with genuine consideration. After a long moment, he nodded. “Yes. Thank you, Husk.” His voice was soft and real; A tone few got to hear.

Husk grunted, trying not to make a big deal of it. “Yeah, yeah. Just get yourself comfortable.”

With that, Husk walked ahead to the end of the dock, giving Alastor the full stretch of silence and solitude.

Alastor stood still a long moment, soaking in the emptiness. The isolation. The lack of prying eyes. Then— Slowly— He stepped toward the quietest corner of the dock, shoulders easing as the pressure in his expression finally melted into something like genuine calm.

For the first time since they’d left the hotel, he looked almost peaceful. And for the first time since they’d left the hotel, it seemed he might finally be able to try.

The quiet of the abandoned dock was absolute.

For most demons, it would have been unsettling— The kind of silence that felt carved out of the world, leaving nothing but the faint groan of distant machinery and the low simmer of Hell’s toxic sea. But for Alastor, the stillness was a sanctuary.

He stood alone in the shadowed corner of the massive structure, Husk stationed far off like an indifferent sentry. Enough distance to feel unobserved. Enough isolation to finally breathe.

Alastor inhaled deeply.

Then exhaled.

He adjusted his posture, smoothing his coat, turning slightly away from the open walkway. He waited another moment, centering his breath, letting the static in his wake dim to a low, barely audible hum. He finally unzipped his pants.

‘Now,’ he told himself.

He closed his eyes.

Tried.

And…

Nothing.

A clench of tension shot through his chest— Not pain, but a familiar freezing sensation, as though someone had placed a hand around a fundamental mechanism of his body and simply… Stopped it.

Alastor tried again.

Still nothing.

His ears twitched sharply. He glanced over his shoulder. Husk wasn’t looking. Good. Good.

Alastor shifted his weight, attempting a more relaxed stance. He inhaled slowly, trying to coax his body into cooperation.

“Come now,” he muttered under his breath, voice tight but quiet. “You are alone. You are safe. You are in control.”

But his body disagreed.

The tension held firm— Worse, even, as the minutes dragged. Pressure built uncomfortably, a dull, growing ache spreading through his abdomen. It demanded relief, yet refused to give it.

He tried again.

Then again.

Still… Nothing.

His composure began to crack.

Static flickered around him, faint and choppy, as though his internal frequency struggled to stay stable. His breathing remained controlled, but shallow. His posture— Normally pristine— Wavered with frustration.

He pressed one hand briefly against the wall for support, tapping his fingers rhythmically, compulsively, as though the familiar motion might ground him.

‘Calm. Steady. No intrusion. No eyes. No ears…’

But even from this distance, even with Husk turned away, the thought of someone out there, someone merely existing within possible earshot, was enough to lock his bladder shut.

He hissed softly in irritation. “This is absurd.”

A wave of discomfort surged, sharper, more insistent. He squirmed— Not dramatically, but enough that the gesture was unmistakable for someone usually so perfectly poised.

He tried once more— Pushing down hard on his lower muscles.

The pressure intensified.

His entire body resisted.

A low growl of static throbbed behind his teeth.

‘I cannot hold this much longer… and yet I cannot…’

He shut his eyes and forced himself to breathe through the tension. His foot tapped once. Then twice. One leg curled around the other like a constricting vine.

Desperation, intense and acutely humiliating, wound tight inside him.

He let out a trembling sigh. “…Dammit.” The curse was whispered, but the admission of struggle left his throat tight. He straightened slowly, closing his eyes in defeat.

A moment later, he heard footsteps approaching the edge of the dock— The soft, steady cadence of Husk’s feet. Not close. Not intrusive. But, near enough to check on him.

“You good?” Husk called lightly, not turning his head. “You ain’t makin’ any sound over there.”

Alastor didn’t answer at first. His pride strained against the truth, but the desperation had become too sharp, too urgent to disguise. “…No,” Alastor finally said, voice quiet and tight. “I am… Not succeeding.”

Husk didn’t turn, he just flicked an ear. “That bad?”

A long silence.

“Yes,” Alastor murmured. The single syllable quivered with tension. “It’s… Becoming rather pressing.”

Husk hummed sympathetically. “Yeah. Been there.”

Alastor stared down at the ground, jaw clenched, one hand hovering near his midsection without quite making contact. He shifted again, discomfort now written plainly in every line of his posture. “I was certain… CERTAIN that this distance would suffice.”

“It’ll happen,” Husk said calmly. “Sometimes takes a while.”

“I do not HAVE a ‘while’ left,” Alastor hissed softly, almost frantic. “If I wait any longer, I—” His voice caught. His entire body tensed sharply as another surge of urgency hit him, forcing him to straighten and bite back whimpers of pain. “This is becoming… Quite unbearable,” he whispered.

Husk finally turned his head halfway, still keeping his eyes respectfully averted. “Hey. You’re not gonna explode. Just breathe. We’ll figure somethin’ out.”

Alastor swallowed hard, breath trembling. He was pacing in place now—small, constrained steps—like a caged animal fighting a battle on two fronts: physical desperation and psychological paralysis. “I do not understand,” he muttered. “I have endured far worse torments than this. Why should— Why DOES this feel so insurmountable?”

Husk shrugged. “Brain’s weird. Anxiety’s weirder. Doesn’t matter if you’re the Radio Demon or a nobody. Sometimes the body just… Locks up.”

Alastor let out a strained, humorless laugh. “Locks up. Yes. That is precisely the problem.” He braced one hand on the railing, head bowed slightly, breathing shallow and rapid.

The desperation was becoming serious.

Husk took a slow step closer, but still kept distance. “Look, if this place ain’t doin’ it, we can move deeper. There’s a lower platform under the dock. Wanna try there?”

Alastor’s voice cracked faintly. “Lower?”

“Yeah.”

Another wave hit Alastor. He stiffened, holding himself absolutely still as he endured it, fingers trembling minutely. “…Yes,” he whispered. “I think—I need more privacy. Before I— Before something will give.”

Husk nodded gently. “Alright. C’mon. Lean on me if you need.”

Alastor shot him a sharp look— Refusal on instinct— But, it softened almost instantly, replaced by another flinch of discomfort.

He exhaled shakily. “I… May accept the offer.”

Husk nodded again. “No problem. Just take it slow.”

Alastor stepped towards him carefully— Very carefully— And Husk guided him to the hidden stairs leading down into even deeper shadow.

The urgency was now unmistakable.

And Alastor was running out of patience.

The metal staircase groaned under their weight as they descended to the lower platform; A rusted balcony tucked directly beneath the main dock, hidden from the world by layers of broken plating and decades of neglect.

Down here, the silence deepened into something heavy. The air was cooler, dense with old salt and iron. The only light came from cracks overhead where the red glow of Hell filtered through in broken strips.

Alastor gripped the railing as he stepped off the last rung.

Husk stayed a few paces behind him, close enough to catch him if his balance faltered, but far enough to preserve space.

The lower platform was empty. Still. Perfectly secluded.

Alastor took a slow, trembling breath. “This should… Suffice,” he whispered, though even he didn’t sound convinced.

He positioned himself in the darkest corner, back turned fully to Husk.

His shoulders rose with another breath.

Then fell.

He waited. Focused.

Tried again.

The silence around him was absolute.

And yet… Nothing. WORSE than nothing. The moment he attempted, his body recoiled violently, tightening with a painful, instinctive resistance that made him wince.

Husk heard the sharp hitch in his breath. “Still stuck?”

Alastor didn’t answer immediately. He squeezed his eyes shut, gripping the railing as another wave of urgency pulsed through him. His knees bent slightly with the effort of controlling it. His ears were rigid, coiled like a spring. Finally, through clenched teeth, he muttered; “Yes.”

Husk frowned. “How bad is it?”

Alastor trembled— Barely, but unmistakably. “Quite… dire,” he admitted. “I can scarcely… Maintain composure.” The strain in his voice cracked, the pressure unmistakable. He shifted his stance, but the movement only made the urgency spike.

He stiffened sharply, breath catching in a pained gasp. Static flickered like a broken signal. He pressed his palm against the wall to steady himself, head lowering as he swallowed back a sound dangerously close to panic.

“I—I don’t understand,” he whispered, voice tight. “Why can’t I— Why won’t my body—” Another surge hit him mid-sentence, cutting him off. He straightened abruptly, gripping the wall so hard his claws scraped it.

Husk stepped closer on instinct. “Easy. Easy. Don’t keel over on me.”

Alastor didn’t respond. He was breathing harder now— Quick and shallow. Panic crept at the edges of his composure like ink bleeding onto paper. His whole frame was trembling with the effort of holding himself together.

“I cannot—” he choked out, shaking his head. “This pressure—It’s—” His voice faltered again, cracking under the strain.

Husk softened his tone. “You’re not gonna hurt yourself, are you?”

“No,” Alastor hissed, though the certainty wasn’t entirely convincing. “But I cannot wait much longer. And yet… it will not start.” He tried again, desperate, leaning subtly forward as his body pleaded for release.

Still nothing.

The tension only tightened brutally, cruelly, locking him in place. Alastor let out a low, helpless sound— Half growl, half groan of frustration— And slammed his fist lightly against the wall.

“This is torture,” he whispered through clenched teeth. “Actual torture. In a place built for it— And yet, somehow, this is the worst of it.”

Husk hesitated. Then, cautiously spoke. “Hey. Look at me.”

Alastor didn’t move.

Another spasm of urgency wracked him; he doubled over a bit, every muscle coiled, trembling. His legs wrapped tightly around one another in a desperate attempt to lessen the pain.

“Alastor,” Husk repeated, firmer this time. “Look at me.”

Slowly, agonizingly. Alastor turned his head. “I… Cannot bear this much longer,” he confessed, voice barely audible. “I truly cannot.”

Husk took a careful step forwards, lowering his voice. “Okay. Then we gotta calm you down.”

Alastor shook his head sharply. “There is no calming through this, Husk— This is a breaking point. If this continues—” Another wave hit him, violent enough that his knees buckled a bit. He gasped, whipping his hand from the wall to his abdomen as instinct overtook dignity. His whole body shook.

Husk reached out without thinking. “Alastor— Hey— Steady—”

“Do NOT touch me,” Alastor snapped, not with anger but with sheer overwhelmed panic. His voice cracked again, thick with desperation. “I can’t— I can’t— If anyone is near me, I—”

“Okay, okay,” Husk backed off instantly, hands raised. “No touching. Promise.”

Alastor squeezed his eyes shut, pressing himself back against the wall as though trying to root himself there, trembling uncontrollably.

He was past anxiety now. This was raw physiological panic layered over unbearable pressure— His body screaming for relief, his mind locking it away behind iron walls.

He spoke again, voice shaking violently; “Husk… I truly do not know… How much longer I can withstand this…”

Husk swallowed hard. This was no longer just awkward. This was serious; Alastor, the ever-collected Radio Demon, was seconds away from breaking entirely.

Alastor clung to the wall as another wave of pressure hit him with brutal force. His breath caught, his knees bent, his whole frame stiffened like a struck wire. For a moment, he couldn’t speak at all. Not from pride, but from sheer, overwhelming desperation.

Husk waited, hands still raised in a placating gesture, keeping distance, watching Alastor’s trembling form with growing concern.

Finally, when the surge eased enough for him to inhale, Alastor whispered, voice hoarse: “This is… Untenable.”

Husk nodded slowly. “Yeah. You’re at your limit. I can see that.”

Alastor pressed his forehead to the wall, breathing through clenched teeth. His shoulders shook with tiny spasms— Controlled, but just barely. He drew a ragged breath. “There is…” He swallowed hard. “There is one thing that can… Occasionally help me overcome this particular impediment.”

Husk blinked. “Oh yeah? What?”

Alastor hesitated. His pride battled with the urgency, with the humiliation, with the panic crushing him from the inside. His legs curled tighter around each other, and his claws scraped weakly at the wall. Finally, painfully, he whispered;“Charlie.”

Husk’s wings flicked in surprise. “Charlie?”

Alastor squeezed his eyes shut. “Yes.”

“How’s she supposed to help?“

Alastor almost laughed— ALMOST— But, the pressure inside him was too intense, too dominating. The sound that escaped was half-snarl, half-gasp. “She knows how to… Calm things.”

“So she relaxes you.”

“Yes!” Alastor snapped, then flinched as the outburst jolted his already fraying nerves. “Yes,” he repeated, softer. “She relaxes me.“ He pressed his palm against his sternum, as if holding something inside. “She helps me feel… Unthreatened. And that—” His breath shuddered.“That sometimes allows the mechanism to… release.”

Husk whistled low. “Huh.”

Another surge hit Alastor suddenly— Even harder than the last. His knees buckled fully this time, and he dropped into a crouch, gripping the wall with one hand, trembling violently as he still fought so hard to let go and urinate like his body was begging for. “Ah—!” A sharp, pained gasp escaped him before he could smother it.

Husk’s eyes widened. “Alastor—”

“Do not come closer,” Alastor choked, squeezing his eyes shut as he rode out the wave of extreme need. “Just listen.”

Husk stepped back again, giving him room.

Alastor’s voice shook with terror, humiliation, and unbearable urgency. “Husk… I require her. I truly do. If she were here, I could, perhaps… Finally—”He broke off, teeth gritted, breath quivering. His entire body was trembling so violently that static crackled uncontrolled around him. “Please.” The word fractured in his throat—raw, desperate, stripped of pride.

Husk felt something twist in his chest. Hearing Alastor beg— Even just with a single word— Felt unreal. “You want me to call her?”

Alastor hesitated. His face was twisted with panic, shame, and the unbearable pressure inside him. He hated needing help. He hated admitting it even more. But he could feel the breaking point approaching like a cliff’s edge beneath his feet. “Yes,” he whispered finally. “Call her. Please.”

Husk nodded, already reaching for his phone.“Alright. Hang on. I’ll get her here fast.”

Alastor pressed his forehead back to the wall, squeezing his eyes shut as another trembling surge wracked him. “Hurry,” he whispered. “I cannot… Endure this much longer…”

Husk fumbled with the phone tapping it twice until Charlie’s bright, earnest voice answered. “Hello? Husk? Is everything okay?”

Husk glanced towards Alastor, still crouched near the wall, shaking with effort, breath tight and shallow. “Uh… No. We need you. Now.”

The concern in Charlie’s tone sharpened instantly. “What happened? Are you hurt?”

“It’s not me,” Husk said. “It’s Alastor. He’s… Struggling. Physically...”

Another tiny, muffled sound escaped Alastor; A hitched breath that wasn’t pain, exactly, but close enough. Husk winced. Charlie must have heard it.

“Oh— Oh dear. Is it his… Um… Private issue?”

“Yeah.”

“I’m on my way.”

The phone went dark.

Husk exhaled in relief. “She’s coming, Alastor. Just hang in there.”

Alastor didn’t speak right away; He couldn’t. He was shaking too hard, one hand gripping the wall so tightly that paint was flaking off beneath his claws. His legs were wound so tight that they trembled like a drawn bowstring.

“Hurry…” he whispered, voice barely there.

It took less than three minutes.

Light footsteps approached from above, then down the metal stairs. Charlie appeared at the edge of the lower platform, her eyes wide with concern the moment they landed on him. “Oh, Alastor…” Her voice was so soft. So warm. Alastor’s shoulders twitched at the sound— As if some part of him wanted to relax but couldn’t yet find the way.

Charlie immediately held out her hands in a slow, unobtrusive gesture, signaling that she wouldn’t touch him without permission. “Can I come closer?” she asked gently.

Alastor’s breath shook. He managed the tiniest nod.

She stepped closer, but stopped several feet away, giving him plenty of space.

Husk stepped back even farther, letting her take the lead.

Charlie lowered herself to a kneel so she’d be at his eye level, but still respectfully distant. Her voice softened into that calm, careful tone she used for frightened residents at the hotel. “Alastor… You’re gonna be alright. Okay? I’m here. I’m right here.”

He let out a strained, barely-there gasp as another wave hit him. He bent lower, trembling hard.

Charlie inhaled deeply, slow and steady, demonstrating deliberately so he could follow if he wanted. “Can you breathe with me?” she whispered.

Alastor clenched his jaw. “I—I am trying—”

“I know,” she said gently. “You’re doing really well. Let’s make it a little easier.” Her voice stayed smooth, soft, rhythmic— Something grounding in and of itself.

“Okay. Follow my voice if you can. Just listen.”

Alastor forced his eyes open enough to watch her.

She gave him a warm, steady smile, not pitying, just reassuring.

“Let’s do the four-step grounding exercise,” Charlie said softly. “You remember this one, right?”

He swallowed hard, then barely nodded.

“Good. Just focus on me. Nothing else.” She held up one finger. “First… Look at five things around you. Just five. They don’t need to be important. Just notice them.”

Alastor’s eyes flickered weakly, searching his surroundings through the haze of desperation.

Charlie guided him gently. “That broken beam… yes, good. The rust on the railing. Husk’s tail over there. The glow from the cracks above. My shoes— Yes. Perfect.”

Alastor’s trembling eased by a fraction.

“Now touch four things. You don’t have to move much— Just what’s within reach.”

His claws scraped lightly against the wall.

“Good. That’s one.”

He touched the railing.

“Two.”

His coat sleeve.

“Three. One more.”

He tugged lightly at the hem of his vest, grounding himself in the familiar texture.

“Four,” Charlie said warmly. “You’re doing amazing.”

Another spasm of pressure hit Alastor. He squeezed one eye shut, but the panic didn’t spike as violently this time. The exercise was helping.

“Alright,” Charlie continued softly. “Three sounds.”

Alastor inhaled shakily, listening. “The sea,” he whispered. The dark waves in the distance churned softly beneath the platform, the sloshing causing his bladder to cramp.

“Yes,” Charlie encouraged. “That’s one.”

“The… Humming of the neon signs above us…”

“Two.”

He paused, listening again.

“…And my heartbeat,” he murmured faintly, eyes flicking toward her.

Charlie smiled gently. “Three. Perfect.” She placed a hand on her chest to demonstrate slow breathing. “Now two big, deep breaths with me. Just two.”

Alastor hesitated— Another spasm hit his middle— But, he followed, breath trembling.

“In… Two… Three…”

“Out… Two… Three…”

Again.

“In… Two… Three…”

“Out… Two… Three…”

His trembling eased further— Still desperate, still urgent, but no longer at the edge of panic.

Charlie watched him carefully. “You’re doing it, Alastor. You’re calming your body. That’s the hardest part.”

Alastor sagged slightly, panting softly. “I still—cannot— Begin,” he managed. “The pressure is… Unbearable.”

“I know,” Charlie said gently. “But now that the fear is quiet, the body can try. You’re doing everything right.” She tilted her head, voice soft as could be. “Would it help if I talked? Just little things. To keep you steady?”

Alastor’s breath shook. “Yes,” he whispered, barely audible. “Please.”

Charlie smiled— Gentle, encouraging and steady.“Okay,” she said. “Then I’m staying right here.”

She began speaking in calm, soothing tones; Soft stories, gentle reassurances, quiet memories of the hotel, her voice a lifeline in the silence.

Alastor closed his eyes, trembling hard—but now he was trembling with relief, with the last threads of panic dissolving.

He felt the locked mechanism inside him loosen slightly. Not fully, not enough for him to actually start going. But, enough that hope flickered in his chest.

Charlie’s voice flowed gently through the still air, soft as a lullaby, steady as a heartbeat. Every word she spoke smoothed another edge of Alastor’s fraying nerves. “…And then, Vaggie walked in on Angel trying to teach Niffty to play cards,” Charlie said with a warm laugh. “And they both were cheating in completely different ways, so…”

Alastor let out a thin, wavering breath. Then another. Bit by bit, his shaking eased. The panic had finally loosened its claws.

His body wasn’t fighting him anymore.

It was simply waiting.

Charlie noticed the shift immediately. She softened her voice even more. “You’re gonna be alright,” she whispered again. “Nothing’s going to startle you. We’re right here. You don’t have to rush. Just let it happen on its own.”

Alastor’s hand pressed weakly to the wall, his head bowing as he exhaled. It was a different kind of tension now— Still huge, still urgent, but no longer locked by terror.

He swallowed hard.

Charlie smiled, utterly gentle. “Don’t push. Don’t force it. Just breathe and let your body do what it needs.”

He closed his eyes. And finally, FINALLY, the internal barrier gave way.

His posture changed in an instant— Shoulders slumping, knees going weak with sheer relief. His breath hitched— Not in pain this time, but in pleasure, in overwhelming unburdening after excruciating torment.

A soft, shuddering exhale left him, long and slow, like the first breath after surfacing from deep underwater. “Ahhhhhh…”

Charlie’s eyes flooded with warmth. “There you go… That’s it. You’re doing so well.”

Alastor braced a hand on the wall to steady himself as the last of the panic dissolved into profound relief. His whole body trembled, but now with the aftershocks of finally, finally letting go.

Husk— Still respectfully turned away— Let out a low sigh of his own. “Thank HELL. Thought you were gonna pass out.”

Alastor couldn’t even muster a retort. He just breathed— Deep, shuddering breaths— Each one loosening the iron grip that had held him captive.

When at last it was over, he sagged forwards, utterly spent, one hand pressed over his eyes as he caught his breath. His muscles unwinding from hours of tension.

Charlie didn’t move until he did. Only when Alastor slowly pushed himself away from the wall, wiping at the corner of one eye with the back of his glove, did she speak. “How do you feel?” she whispered.

Alastor swallowed, voice hoarse, thin, but steady. “…Refreshed.” Then, after a moment, he added; “And, terribly embarrassed.”

Charlie shook her head. “You don’t need to be. You asked for help. That’s one of the bravest things anyone can do.”

Husk finally turned around, giving him a once-over. “You look like you fought a war.”

“…I rather feel as though I have,” Alastor muttered, trying to straighten his coat with his shaky hands.

Charlie smiled kindly. “You didn’t fight it alone. And that’s good.”

Alastor lowered his gaze, flustered— Truly flustered. For once, the ever-imposing Radio Demon looked simply… Human. “…Thank you,” he murmured. “Both of you.”

Charlie’s expression softened even more. “Always.”

When Alastor finally managed to stand all the way upright again, his legs nearly buckled under him. The tension of the past few hours had drained him so thoroughly that his limbs felt like rubber.

Charlie noticed instantly. “Easy— Easy,” she murmured, stepping closer with open hands. “Can I support you?”

Alastor hesitated— His pride reflexively stiffening his posture— But, another tremor ran through his knees, and the last of his resistance melted. “Yes… I suppose… That would be advisable,” he said, voice faint and brittle around the edges.

Charlie slipped a hand gently beneath his elbow, guiding rather than holding. Husk came to his other side, offering a wing for balance.

“Don’t get used to this,” Husk grumbled, though the softness in his tone ruined any attempt at gruffness.

Alastor attempted a dry chuckle. It came out as more of a weak breath. “I assure you,” he muttered, “I have no desire to repeat this ordeal.”

Together, they began the slow walk back up the rusted staircase. Alastor relied on them more than he would ever admit aloud— His posture slumped just a fraction, his steps small and cautious. The relief had left him shaky, unsteady, like he’d shed both physical and emotional weight all at once.

Charlie walked slightly ahead once they reached flat ground, her pace slow and carefully measured to match his.

Only when they reached a quiet stretch of the dock did she speak again. “You did amazing back there,” she said softly. “I know how difficult that was.”

Alastor’s shoulders tensed. “Difficult is… An understatement.”

Husk snorted. “Damn straight.”

Charlie shot him a look, but Alastor didn’t seem bothered. He was staring ahead, expression tight. A shadow passed over his features.

“Alastor?” Charlie asked gently. “What’s wrong?”

He didn’t answer at first. His claws tapped restlessly against his cane, his gaze growing distant, troubled. Finally—Quietly, as though the words were being dragged out of him—He murmured; “This is the SECOND time I’ve needed someone to… Call you to come assist me.”

“And I don’t mind!” Charlie assured. “All part of our deal! I want to help you!”

“True, but… I am concerned my… ‘Situation’ is worsening.”

Charlie’s brows knit with concern. “Worsening? Oh no, Alastor! You’re getting BETTER. I’ve seen it! You’re more open to asking for help, for one. And—“
