***

Charlie had been excited about a lot of things in her life— Redeeming sinners, grand musical numbers, aggressively believing in people— But, THIS ranked surprisingly high; A mall trip. A real, honest-to-hell shopping mall. With escalators. And pretzels. And capitalism distilled into fluorescent lighting.

What made it unprecedented wasn’t the mall itself, it was her companions.

“Okay!” Charlie clapped her hands together, beaming as the massive glass doors of Pentagram Galleria slid open with a cheerful ding. “Here we are! I thought it’d be fun to get out of the hotel and, you know, do something… Normal?”

Lucifer stood beside her in a tailored white suit that looked aggressively out of place among the gaggle of demons wearing ripped jeans and chains. He stared at the mall interior with narrowed, crimson eyes. “…Normal,” Lucifer echoed flatly. “Charlie, my darling, this place smells like debt and broken dreams.”

Alastor, standing next to him, looked thrilled. The Radio Demon adjusted his monocle, his ever-present grin stretching just a fraction wider as the automatic doors closed behind them. The lights reflected sharply off his antlers as he surveyed the mall like a man stepping into a personal playground.

“My, my!” he chimed, his voice crackling with faint radio static. “Such a vibrant little circus of consumption! Bright lights, endless noise, and desperate souls clinging to material joy!”

Charlie laughed nervously. “See? You’re both gonna love it! I just thought it’d be nice to… Bond. Together. Like a family. And Alastor.”

Lucifer shot her a sideways look. “He is NOT family.”

Alastor placed a gloved hand over his chest in mock offense. “Why, Luci, that cuts deep! And here I thought we’d grown so close.”

“You threatened to rearrange my internal organs into a jazz band just this morning.”

“Semantics!”

Charlie immediately stepped between them, arms outstretched. “Okay! Rule number one of the mall: No threatening each other. Rule number two: No murdering shoppers.”

Alastor scoffed. “I’m insulted you’d even think I’d stoop so low.”

Charlie gave him the look. The one that had somehow managed to make overlords pause. “…Please?” she added softly.

Lucifer sighed, running a hand through his golden hair. “Fine. For you. But, if anyone asks me to sign up for a credit card, I’m burning the place down.”

“Deal!” Charlie chirped.

They began walking, and immediately the mall reacted.

Demons stared. Whispered. Stopped mid-conversation. A pair of imps dropped their drinks. Someone gasped, “Is that—?”

Lucifer noticed, of course. His posture straightened instinctively. “They’re staring.”

Charlie smiled at him. “They always do.”

Alastor leaned closer to Charlie, his voice lowering conspiratorially. “You know, dear, I do believe your father’s presence alone has raised the collective blood pressure of this establishment. How delicious.”

Their first stop— Charlie's insistence— Was a clothing store. “Just browsing!” she said brightly, already grabbing a hanger. “I thought it’d be fun to try some new looks. Maybe something casual!”

Alastor stared at a rack of distressed jeans like it had personally offended him. “Why are they already ruined?”

“Fashion, Alastor.”

“And why,” he continued, holding up a shirt with aggressive slashes, “Does this one look like it lost a knife fight?”

Lucifer, meanwhile, had wandered to the accessories section. He picked up a pair of oversized sunglasses and perched them on his nose. “Well?” he asked, turning to them. “Incognito.”

Charlie giggled. “You look like a celebrity trying really hard not to be noticed.”

As Charlie disappeared into a fitting room, Lucifer leaned against a display table, arms crossed. “Alastor,” he muttered, “I don’t understand why she wants us here. This is pointless.”

Alastor hummed. “On the contrary, I find it rather illuminating. She’s sharing her world. Extending an olive branch.”

Lucifer glanced at him sharply. “Don’t pretend you care.”

“Oh, I don’t,” Alastor replied cheerfully.

Charlie popped back out, wearing an oversized hoodie with a cartoon demon stitched on the front. “What do you think?”

Alastor paused. “…It’s acceptable,” he said, which from him was practically a standing ovation.

Charlie beamed.

***

Charlie practically dragged them to the arcade.

The sounds hit first; Electronic jingles overlapping in chaotic harmony, synthesized victory fanfares screaming for attention, the clatter of plastic tokens, the thud of frustrated fists against malfunctioning cabinets. Lights pulsed in every color imaginable, reflecting off polished floors and glass screens like a migraine given physical form.

Alastor stopped dead at the entrance. “…Absolutely not.”

“Oh come on!” Charlie turned around, walking backwards with her hands clasped behind her. “It’s fun! Games, prizes, friendly competition!”

Alastor’s smile hadn’t vanished— But, it had tightened, like a painting stretched too far over a warped frame. His eyes flicked over the machines, the screens, the humming cables. His gaze lingered on a racing game cabinet like it might lunge at him. “How very soulless.”

Charlie frowned, then brightened again with determination. “Okay! We’ll start small. Something simple. No pressure.” She scanned the arcade, eyes landing on a row of crane machines along the far wall— Glass boxes filled with plush toys, blinking invitingly. One in particular caught her eye. It was mainly filled with pastel-colored ducks wearing tiny crowns and bowties.

“Oooh! That one!” she said. “Those are adorable!”

Alastor followed her gaze and stiffened.

The crane machine whirred softly, mechanical arm dangling uselessly above the plush pile. Its screen flashed instructions in bold, cheerful font.

INSERT TOKEN.

MOVE JOYSTICK.

PRESS BUTTON.

He stared at it like it was written in a dead language.

“…It appears to be a box,” he said slowly, “Designed to mock you.”

Charlie laughed. “It’s a crane game! You try to grab a stuffed animal with the claw.”

Alastor raised an eyebrow. “And people PAY for this?”

“Yes!”

“…Why.”

“You should try! Just once.”

Alastor lifted his hands slightly, as though the machine might bite. “My dear, I prefer entertainment with INTENT. This thing runs on— What— Electricity? Algorithms? Chance?”

“Not chance,” Charlie said gently. “Skill.”

Lucifer mumbled; “That’s propaganda.”

Charlie leaned closer to Alastor, lowering her voice like she was sharing a secret. “You don’t have to like it. Just… Give it a shot. For me?”

There it was. That earnest, hopeful look. The same one she used when convincing demons to attend group therapy or sing about their feelings.

Alastor hesitated. Then, with an exaggerated sigh, he straightened his coat and stepped forward. “Very well! I shall humor this delightful exercise in futility.”

He took the joystick between two fingers like it was distasteful.

The machine chimed.

The claw began to move.

Alastor’s eyes sharpened.

The background noise of the arcade seemed to fade as his focus narrowed. He leaned slightly forward, head tilting, gaze tracking the claw’s path with unnerving precision. His smile was real now, predatory and delighted.

“…Oh,” he murmured. “I see.”

Charlie blinked. “You do?”

“Of course!” His fingers adjusted minutely on the joystick. “The timing. The weight distribution. The illusion of randomness— It’s merely a performance! A cheap parlor trick!” He pressed the button.

The claw descended.

It closed.

And lifted, firmly gripping a plush duck by its tiny crown.

Charlie gasped. “Alastor!”

The machine dinged triumphantly as the duck dropped into the prize chute.

Alastor laughed, not his usual controlled chuckle, but a sharp, delighted bark of genuine amusement. “Ha! Marvelous! Did you see that, Luci? It practically begged to be taken!”

Lucifer stared at the duck, then at the machine. “… Do it again.”

“Oh, gladly.”

Five minutes later, a small crowd had gathered.

Alastor didn’t miss once.

He analyzed each plush pile like a battlefield, calculating angles, tension, claw strength. Duck after duck fell into the chute. Bears. Cats. An evil looking frog with glittery eyes. Charlie was clapping openly now, bouncing on her heels.

“You’re amazing!” she said. “You’re like… Scary good.”

“I do pride myself on mastery,” Alastor replied, dropping another token in. “Even in the most… Unexpected mediums.”

Lucifer watched in silence. He didn’t comment. Didn’t sneer. Just observed— The way Alastor moved with confidence now, how his discomfort with the technology had vanished the moment he understood it. How he adapted.

Eventually, Alastor stopped.

The machine sat emptied of its most desirable prizes, blinking uselessly.

Alastor reached into the chute one final time and retrieved a small plush duck— Pale yellow, button eyes, a tiny red bowtie slightly crooked. He turned it over in his hands. Then, without ceremony, he walked over to Lucifer and held it out. “For you.”

Lucifer froze. “… What.”

Alastor smiled, easy and casual. “A token of victory! Consider it a souvenir of our delightful outing.”

Charlie’s eyes widened.

Lucifer stared at the duck like it might explode.“You… Won this,” he said slowly.

“Indeed.”

“…And you’re giving it to ME.”

“Well, I doubt it suits my aesthetic,” Alastor replied lightly. “And you seem… To enjoy ducks.”

Lucifer hesitated just a fraction of a second before taking it.

The plush duck was absurdly soft. Something twisted quietly in his chest. He tucked it under his arm, expression carefully neutral.

Charlie noticed anyway. She smiled to herself. ‘Awww, look at them…’

Alastor, just for a moment, glanced over, his grin softer, satisfied in a way that had nothing to do with winning.

And then, the lights flickered. Not the playful arcade kind. The sharp, intrusive kind.

A low electronic hum rolled through the air, crawling up the walls, vibrating the screens. Several machines glitched at once, their displays warping into static before snapping back.

Alastor’s ears twitched. “…Oh,” he said pleasantly. “This just became interesting.”

A nearby screen flared to life, colors oversaturated, resolution painfully crisp. A familiar, smug face filled it— Blue-tinted, angular, eyes glowing with artificial light. “WELL WELL WELL!” Vox’s voice boomed through the arcade speakers, overriding the cheerful music.

Lucifer sighed, pinching the bridge of his nose. “Why is he everywhere?”

The screen fizzed, then Vox himself stepped out from behind a row of cabinets, coat sharp, screen-face gleaming, antennae flickering with excitement. He took in the scene in one sweeping glance.

Princess Charlie.

Lucifer.

God damned stupid fucking piece of shit Alastor.

And then—

He stopped.

His head turned slowly toward a crane machine near the far corner.

Inside it sat a plush shark. Oversized head, tiny fins, stitched-on jagged grin, neon-blue fabric with pixel-patterned scales. A perfect, tacky, corporate nightmare.

Vox’s screen flickered. “…Is that a limited-edition SharkNet promo plush?”

Alastor followed his gaze, grin widening immediately. “Ah. So THAT’S what caught your eye.”

Vox was already moving. He planted himself in front of the machine, staring at the shark like it was destiny. “I need it.”

Charlie blinked. “Oh! You like sharks?”

Vox didn’t look away. “I LOVE sharks.“

Alastor folded his arms. “It’s a stuffed toy.”

“Yes,” Vox snapped, jamming a token into the slot, “and it’s MINE.”

The machine chimed.

Vox grabbed the joystick with confidence that absolutely was not earned.

The claw lurched.

Overshot.

It missed the shark entirely and grabbed a sad, unrelated octopus by one tentacle— Then dropped it immediately.

The machine let out a pitiful buzz.

Alastor laughed; A full, delighted, static-laced laugh that cut clean through the arcade noise.“Oh, this is just precious.”

Vox’s screen flashed red. “Shut up.”

Charlie tried to be encouraging. “You were really close! Just, um— Maybe adjust a little?”

Vox tried again.

Too far left.

Then too far right.

Then directly over the shark— Only for the claw to close too early and come back up empty.

Lucifer watched impassively. “… You’re terrible at this.”

Vox growled. “This machine is rigged.”

Alastor sauntered closer, hands clasped behind his back. “You know, my dear Voxxy, I couldn’t help but notice you’re struggling.”

Vox didn’t respond, already shoving in another token.

Alastor leaned down slightly, grin razor-sharp. “Why, if I were playing, I’d have that shark in… Oh—” he checked an imaginary watch, “— Twenty seconds.”

Vox froze. Slowly, he turned his head. “You wouldn’t.”

Lucifer, without missing a beat, nodded. “He would.”

Alastor placed a hand over his heart. “And effortlessly.”

Vox’s screen glitched with visible stress. “…You can’t.”

“Oh, but I can,” Alastor replied cheerfully. “And now that you’ve made your desire so obvious, I find myself terribly tempted.”

Vox looked back at the shark. Then at the claw. Then at Alastor. A horrible realization dawned. “… You’re not allowed to touch this machine,” Vox said.

Alastor laughed again. “Oh? And who, pray tell, is going to stop me?”

Vox jammed another token in with unnecessary force. “Fine. Then I’m not leaving.”

Charlie tilted her head. “Not leaving…?”

“I will stand here,” Vox declared, glaring at the shark, “Until I win it.”

Lucifer raised an eyebrow. “That could take a while.”

“Then it takes a while.”

Alastor clasped his hands, delighted. “Marvelous! A siege!”

Vox played.

And played.

And played.

Token after token vanished into the machine. The claw mocked him relentlessly— Slipping off the shark’s fin, nudging it deeper into the pile.

A small audience gathered.

Some whispered bets.

Alastor offered commentary. “Oh dear, too shallow!”, “Ah! You nearly had it— Tragic.” “My, perhaps try NOT murdering the button?”

Vox’s screen flickered violently. “I do not need your input!”

Minutes passed.

Then more.

Vox’s posture stiffened, shoulders tight, jaw clenched. His movements grew frantic— Less precise, more desperate. The shark remained stubbornly out of reach, as if aware of the drama it was causing.

Alastor leaned in close, voice dropping to a near-whisper. “You know, Vox, the longer this goes on… The more inevitable it becomes that I’ll simply step in.”

Vox slammed the button.

Missed again.

“…I hate you,” Vox hissed.

Alastor beamed. “The feeling is mutual, dearest.”

Lucifer glanced at the plush duck tucked under his arm, then back at the shark, then at Vox. “… You’re never getting that thing,” he said calmly.

Vox didn’t look away from the machine. Didn’t move. “Then neither is HE,” Vox replied.

And so the standoff continued— One shark, one machine, one Radio Demon itching to prove a point, and one TV overlord slowly being destroyed by a claw on a string… Realizing that he’d been standing there too long.

At first, it was just irritation— The kind that buzzed beneath his skin in faint static lines, easily drowned out by spite and determination. Then, it became discomfort. A subtle tightening, an internal system notification that he aggressively ignored.

Then it escalated.

His leg twitched.

He shifted his weight, just slightly, then immediately stilled, eyes locked on the plush shark like it might escape if he blinked.

Alastor noticed, naturally. “Oh?” the Radio Demon crooned, leaning casually against the side of the machine Vox had claimed as his battleground. “You seem… Restless, Voxxy.”

“I’m fine,” Vox snapped, jamming another token in. The claw descended, missed the shark by a humiliating inch, and retracted empty-handed.

The machine chimed cheerfully.

Vox growled.

Charlie glanced between them, then tilted her head. “Um… Vox? Do you maybe want to take a break? You’ve been at this for a while.”

“No.”

Lucifer raised an eyebrow. “You’re sweating.”

“I do not sweat.”

“You’re glitching,” Lucifer corrected.

Indeed, Vox’s screen flickered, just a fraction of a second longer than usual, before stabilizing. He clenched his jaw. “I’m not leaving,” he said through his teeth.

Alastor’s grin sharpened. “Why ever not?”

Vox shot him a look that could have shorted out a lesser demon. “You know exactly why.”

Alastor gasped theatrically. “My word! Are you suggesting I’d steal your prize the moment you stepped away?”

Lucifer answered for him. “Yes.”

Charlie winced. “Alastor…”

“Oh, I wouldn’t steal it,” Alastor said sweetly. “I’d win it. Winning isn’t stealing.”

Vox’s hands tightened on the joystick.

Another attempt.

Another failure.

The shark seemed to mock him now, its stitched grin angled just right, pressed tantalizingly against the glass.

Vox shifted again. His knee bounced once before he forcibly stilled it, shoulders stiff, posture rigid in a way that had nothing to do with confidence anymore.

Alastor leaned closer, voice dropping to a conspiratorial murmur. “You know, Vox, the body has so many limitations. Biological needs. Time constraints. Such a shame.”

Vox hissed, “Stop talking.”

Lucifer watched him for a moment longer, then sighed. “You’re doing this to yourself.”

Another token vanished.

The claw grabbed the shark— FINALLY— Just barely catching its fin.

Vox’s eyes lit up. “Yes—YES—”

The claw slipped.

The shark fell deeper into the pile.

The machine buzzed.

Something inside Vox broke. He slammed his palm against the side of the cabinet, then immediately froze, breathing shallow, posture locking up again. He’d moved too much. And moving too much had been a catastrophic mistake. His bladder cramped and slammed itself around inside him. He tensed his thighs against the angry surge of pressure, heavy warmth burning down his length. “…I hate this,” he muttered.

Charlie stepped closer. “Vox, it’s okay if you need to—”

“No,” Vox snapped instantly, panic sharp beneath the anger. “If I leave, HE wins.”

Alastor placed a hand over his heart, mock sincerity dripping from every syllable. “I wouldn’t even wait for the stick-gizmo to cool.”

Vox’s screen flashed with pure fury. He planted his feet and did not move, even as liquid burned brightly at his opening.

Minutes dragged on.

The arcade music looped twice.

Vox’s composure deteriorated in small, telling ways— Tighter jaw, clipped movements, his antennae flickering erratically. He stopped trash-talking Alastor altogether, focusing every ounce of willpower on standing still and not thinking about how badly he needed a piss.

Lucifer leaned towards Alastor, voice low. “How stubborn is he?”

Alastor whispered back, “Oh, very. We are in for quite a show.”

Vox’s hands trembled. He did not step away. Did not look away. The shark sat unmoved.

Time stopped being measurable in anything useful.

It was no longer measured in tokens spent or attempts failed. It was measured instead in Vox’s increasingly shallow breaths, in the way his shoulders had tightened, in the rigid, unmoving set of his stance as if his body were being held together by sheer will alone.

The need had gone from inconvenient to urgent. Then from urgent to painful.

Vox’s internal systems screamed at him— Warning pings he manually suppressed, error messages he force-closed out of spite. He clenched his jaw so hard it made his screen flicker, knuckles whitening as he gripped the joystick like it was Alastor’s neck.

Another token.

Another try.

The claw descended.

It brushed the shark’s side— SO CLOSE— Before sliding uselessly off the slick fabric and rising empty once more.

The machine chirped happily.

Vox let out a strangled sound that was halfway between a growl and a glitch.

Alastor tilted his head, eyes gleaming. “My, my… Your form is deteriorating.”

“Shut UP,” Vox snapped, though the words came tighter now, strained. He shifted his weight again before freezing, even that small movement had been a mistake. Fluid rolled within him, scorching brutally against his poor bladder’s over-stretched walls.

Charlie had stopped smiling. “Vox,” she said softly, stepping closer, “This really isn’t worth hurting yourself over. It’s just a toy.”

Vox didn’t look at her. Couldn’t. His eyes were locked on the shark like it was the only thing anchoring him to reality. “You don’t understand,” he muttered. “If I leave— If I move— HE wins.”

Lucifer glanced at Vox’s posture, the tension wound so tight it was readily visible, and frowned. “You’re past stubborn. This is stupid.”

Vox laughed sharply, humorless.

Another token vanished.

His hands shook as he guided the claw, movements jerky now, precision gone. He overshot the target entirely, the claw descending into a useless pile of filler plush instead.

Miss.

The machine buzzed.

Vox hissed through his teeth, doubling over before catching himself, straightening abruptly with a sharp inhale that betrayed just how bad he needed a restroom.

Alastor’s smile widened, but there was a flicker of something sharper in his eyes now. Interest, yes. Amusement, absolutely. But also a keen awareness of just how close Vox was to snapping.

“Vox,” Lucifer said lightly, “There comes a point where pride stops being entertaining and starts becoming… Tragic.”

Vox didn’t respond. He couldn’t spare the breath. Every inhale brought the full weight of his bladder down more firmly against his quivering muscles.

Another attempt.

The claw caught the shark again— BARELY— Lifting it just an inch off the pile.

Vox’s entire body went rigid. “Yes—yes—YES—”

The claw loosened.

The shark dropped.

Vox slammed the button too hard, the cabinet rattling. He immediately doubled down on his posture again, breathing shallow, eyes wild, squirming from foot to foot as if instinct were fighting him.

Charlie looked genuinely worried now. “Vox, please. This isn’t funny anymore.”

“Oh?” Alastor asked. “Isn’t it?”

Lucifer crossed his arms, gaze sharp. “…You’re going to seriously regret this.”

“I already do,” Vox snapped weakly.

Time dragged.

The arcade noise blurred into static.

Vox stopped trash-talking entirely. Stopped reacting to Alastor’s comments. All of his focus was consumed by two impossible tasks: Winning the shark and not pissing himself.

Both were failing.

His movements became rushed, sloppy, every attempt worse than the last. The claw missed by inches, then embarrassingly wide margins that made Alastor’s stupid laugh grow louder.

Pain lanced sharply through him, and Vox sucked in a breath, shoulders trembling for just a second before he locked down again.

“No,” he muttered to himself. “Not now. Not—”

Alastor straightened, eyes narrowing just a touch. “This is getting rather undignified.”

Vox shot him a furious look. “You think I don’t know that?!”

Another miss.

Another buzz.

The shark remained untouched, smug in its glass prison.

Vox stood there, shaking, desperate, refusing to yield an inch— Not to his body, not to reason, and certainly not to that stupid fucking, God damned, jackass, smirking, piece of shit, bastard, smug, worthless Alastor!

But, Vox was no longer just uncomfortable, he was operating on sheer panic.

Every system in his body was screaming at him now, red warning icons flashing behind his eyes, alerts he couldn’t fully suppress anymore. He stood ramrod straight, muscles locked so tightly it looked like one wrong movement might shatter him outright.

He had to go SO bad.

No— This was the worst he’d EVER had to go!

His breathing came short and sharp, the glow of his screen pulsing erratically as his concentration fractured. The joystick trembled in his grip, no longer an extension of his will but something he had to actively fight to control.

Another token.

Another try.

The claw lurched wildly to the side.

Missed by a mile.

The machine buzzed.

Vox let out a broken, frustrated sound, cutting himself off immediately, jaw clamping shut as he forced himself still again. He squeezed his eyes shut for half a second, then snapped them open, horrified at the lapse. “Okay— Okay— Focus,” he muttered, voice tight and fast. “Just— Just focus.”

Alastor watched him like a mad scientist observing a particularly interesting mutation. “My, Vox,” he said, “You seem rather… Frantic.”

“Don’t,” Vox snapped, though the word wavered. “Don’t talk.”

Charlie was openly distressed now. “Vox, you’re clearly not okay. Please, step away. It’s just a shark.”

Vox laughed— A sharp, breathless sound that immediately turned into a hiss as he stiffened again. “And it’s MINE.”

Another attempt.

Vox overshot again, hands jerking as he tried to correct at the last second. The claw descended uselessly into an empty corner of the machine.

Miss.

The shark hadn’t moved in ages.

Vox’s vision swam. His screen glitched hard this time, colors bleeding for a split second before snapping back. He swallowed, posture impossibly rigid, as if standing any other way would be catastrophic.

“I can’t leave,” he said, more to himself now than anyone else. “I can’t— I’m too close.”

Alastor hummed. “Mm. You’re not close.”

“I’m CLOSE,” Vox snapped, panic leaking through the anger. “And, if I walk away, you’ll take it immediately.”

“Indeed. Such a tragedy.”

Lucifer sighed. “This is what pride looks like when it stops being impressive.”

Vox didn’t respond. He physically couldn’t spare the attention.

Another token.

Another failure.

His hands shook badly now, making precise movement impossible. The claw brushed the shark again, just enough to shove it even deeper into the pile.

The machine chirped.

Vox made a sound that was dangerously close to a scream and bit it back hard, shoulders trembling as he fought for control.

Charlie reached out instinctively. “Vox—”

“Don’t touch me,” he snapped instantly, fear raw beneath the words. “Please. Don’t.”

She froze, hand dropping.

Time stretched unbearably.

Vox was trapped— By the machine, by Alastor’s smug presence at his shoulder, by his own refusal to lose. Every second made it harder to think, harder to aim, harder to pretend that his bladder wasn’t at its absolute limit.

Vox stood frozen in place, eyes wide, body rigid, fighting a losing battle on two fronts at once— Against a clearly rigged crane machine and against himself.

And Alastor, eyes glinting, leaned just a fraction closer. “Tick, tock, Voxxy,” he whispered. “How much longer do you think you can keep this up?”

Vox didn’t answer.

He slammed in another token.

And missed.

Again.

Worse than that, he LEAKED. His crotch warmed as liquid broke forth and spilled out over him, quickly trickling down one leg. “A—Ah—!”

It felt so sudden!

One moment he was frozen in place, staring at the shark with wild, unfocused eyes, every nerve in his body screaming at him to move— And the next, his bladder was squeezing, contracting, and shoving out its burden. It was slow, dismal, not the waterfall he NEEDED to release at all, but within a few seconds he heard a soft pattering as his choppy flow reached the ground below him.

“No— Nope— No, no, NO—” He spun on his heel so fast his coat flared out behind him, abandoning the crane machine mid-blink.

Alastor’s head snapped up. “Oh?”

Charlie startled. “Vox—?”

“I’M COMING BACK,” Vox barked over his shoulder, already sprinting, voice cracking with urgency. “DON’T—TOUCH—ANYTHING—”

And then he was gone, vanishing to the restrooms with zero dignity left to preserve.

The crane machine sat unattended.

Silent.

The plush shark stared out through the glass.

Alastor slowly turned his head towards it.

Charlie noticed immediately. “Alastor” she said. “Don’t you dare.”

Alastor’s grin spread— Slow, delighted, dangerous. “He LEFT.”

“He had an emergency,” Charlie said weakly.

“And?” Alastor replied, already stepping closer. “Emergencies don’t pause fate, my dear.”

Charlie moved to block him. “Alastor, please— This is already bad enough.”

Lucifer glanced between the machine and the area Vox had disappeared to. “…He IS going to be furious…”

Alastor chuckled. “That’s the idea.”

***

Vox burst into the restroom, slammed the door shut behind him, and braced himself against the wall, breathing hard, screen flickering like a signal on the verge of dropping. “Stupid— Machine—Stupid— Radio freak— FUCK, FUCK, FUCK!”

He didn’t waste time. He couldn’t afford to. He stood at a urinal and tore his still leaking dick out of his pants, the spurts building into a spray almost instantly.

The relief hit like a system reboot, leaving him dizzy, shoulders sagging as he finally— FINALLY—let himself breathe again.

“Ohhhhhh, holy fuck, that’s—” he muttered, cutting himself off and straightening immediately. “Nope. No time. Wrap it up. Go.”

Vox glanced toward the door like Alastor might materialize through it via pure spite. “Just— Just get it done,” he hissed to himself. “Then I’m back. He’s not winning. He’s not—”

The thought alone sent him scrambling to finish up as fast as possible.

Because somewhere out there. There was a crane machine. There was a plush shark. And there was an Alastor-shaped problem standing far too close to both.

Vox came running back as soon as he was done relieving himself, not bothering to wash his hands, coat flapping, breath sharp, screen still faintly glitching from the sheer indignity of having had to retreat at all. He skidded to a stop in front of the crane machines—

— And froze.

Alastor was standing a few steps away, casually twirling a plush shark around by its fin.

Vox’s screen went blank for half a second. “… No,” he said faintly.

Alastor’s grin was incandescent. “Welcome back, Voxxy! You missed a marvelous performance.”

Lucifer, arms crossed, nodded once. “One try.”

Charlie winced. “Alastor…”

Vox stared at the shark. Then at Alastor. Then back at the shark. The sound he made next was undignified. A thin, strained whine dragged out of his throat before he could stop it, equal parts fury and heartbreak.

“You—You—” His voice cracked. “That was mine.”

Alastor clasped the plush to his chest dramatically. “Was it? I don’t see your name on it.”

“I was coming back,” Vox snapped, stomping closer. “You knew I was coming back!”

“Oh, I had faith,” Alastor said brightly. “Just not patience.”

Vox looked like he might actually combust.

Charlie stepped in quickly, hands raised. “Okay—okay, everyone breathe. Alastor, you’ve proven your point. Very thoroughly. You’re better at this game. We all see that. So, maybe now… Maybe you could give Vox the shark?”

The arcade seemed to go quiet.

Alastor slowly turned to look at her. Give it to Vox?! The idea visibly offended him. “My dear,” he said smoothly, “This little fellow represents triumph. Strategy. Skill. A victory snatched at the perfect moment.”

Vox crossed his arms, voice tight and pleading despite himself. “You don’t even LIKE sharks.”

“That’s beside the point.”

Charlie glanced at the plush duck still tucked under Lucifer’s arm, then at Alastor. “You gave Dad one.”

Vox paused and stared at the duck Lucifer held, then at Alastor. “You… You what…?”

Alastor just grinned that stupid grin.

“You… Gave him a gift?” Vox asked. “But… You…”

“Indeed,” Alastor said. “Dear Luci seems rather fond of ducks.”

Vox froze. ‘Dear Luci.’ And, Alastor had given him something… Something he knew he’d love. Alastor… Back when he and Vox were close, Alastor had given HIM little trinkets, tiny objects he could be certain Vox would enjoy, small offerings of their bond.

He scowled at Lucifer. He couldn’t help it. A million thoughts fired through him at once, all the things he’d like to DO to Lucifer if he had the chance.

Alastor rejecting him in the way he had was already bad enough, but now he was REPLACING him!?

“Yeah! That was really nice, Alastor,” Charlie continued. “And this would be nice too. Right now Vox is… Uh… Clearly having a moment.”

Vox bristled. “I am not—”

“You whined,” Lucifer said flatly.

Vox’s screen flashed red. “I did not—”

“You did,” Charlie said gently. “A little.”

Alastor looked between them, shark dangling from his fingers. As Charlie kept speaking to Vox, Alastor couldn’t help noticing the glare the Television Demon was sending to both Lucifer and the duck cradled in his arms.

“Hm…” Alastor hummed to himself, a thought forming. He didn’t… Adore the idea, but so long as no tongue was involved… “Lucifer,” he whispered. “Kiss me.”

Lucifer stumbled. “The fuck?! Why?!”

“Because,” Alastor said. “It would be exceptionally funny.”

“What are you two whispering about?” Vox demanded. “Are you giving me the shark, or not?”

Alastor’s grin softened, but not kindly. Thoughtfully. Like a man considering whether mercy would be more entertaining than cruelty.“Well…” he drawled. “I COULD give it to you.”

Vox leaned forward instantly. “Yes?”

Alastor held the shark closer. “…But, then again,” he continued, “Watching you spiral over a stuffed fish has been one of the highlights of my day.”

Vox let out another frustrated sound, hands balling into fists. “You won. You humiliated me. Congratulations. Isn’t that enough?”

For a moment, Alastor just watched him. Really watched him. "Are you about to start begging?”

"If I do, will I get it?" Vox huffed.

"That's very unlikely."

"Then, what's the point?!"

Alastor's grin widened. "My amusement."

Then Charlie stepped closer, voice soft but firm. “Alastor. Please. This isn’t about winning anymore.”

The Radio Demon’s eyes flicked to her— And something there shifted. Not gone. Just… adjusted. He sighed dramatically. “Oh, I’ll think about it…”

Vox stared. “You’ll think about it? What does that mean?” He gripped Alastor’s shoulders. “C’mon, Al, we—“

Alastor bristled and a squeal of radio feedback exploded as he jerked away, like Vox’s hands were made of acid that burned him. “Alright. Thought about it. It’s not happening.”

Charlie sighed. “Alastor… What’s so hard about—“

“I said no,” Alastor interrupted, eyes not leaving Vox’s. “And, when I say ‘no’, I mean it.”

Charlie glanced back and forth between them, uncertain. Maybe… Maybe Alastor liked sharks more than he let on…? Was that it? Except, he wasn’t gloating anymore… His entire demeanor had changed in the span of a few seconds, and she couldn’t think of any reason why.

“Well…” Charlie said. “If it’s really important to you, Alastor… It is still your choice… And I’m proud of you for sharing with Dad!”

Vox scowled. “Proud of him!? He’s being a jackass!” He seethed, unable to keep his eyes off Alastor, Lucifer, and the duck Lucifer held. Why would Alastor give him that?! And… Were they standing close together on purpose? Did it MEAN anything?! What did it mean?!

It MEANT that Vox was going to have to make them BOTH suffer!

***

The food court was mercifully less chaotic than the arcade.

It still buzzed— Voices overlapping, trays clattering, neon menus glowing overhead— But, the tension had loosened, like the mall itself was finally letting out a breath. Charlie led the way with renewed enthusiasm, weaving between tables and chairs like this was all part of a carefully planned itinerary.

“Okay! Food court time!” she announced. “Everyone needs a snack after all that… Uh… Emotional intensity.”

Lucifer followed, the plush duck tucked under his arm as if it had always belonged there. Alastor strolled alongside him, cane tapping lightly against the tile, holding the shark, his static stabilized.

They settled at a circular table near the center, Charlie bouncing into her seat, Alastor lounging back with ease, Lucifer sitting with careful dignity that did not quite hide his curiosity as he surveyed the food options.

“…Why are there so many choices,” Lucifer muttered. “Nothing should have forty-seven variations.”

“That’s the fun part!” Charlie said. “You can try things you’d never expect to like.”

Lucifer hummed skeptically.

There was a pause.

Then— Quietly, measured—Lucifer spoke again. “Alastor,” he said, eyes forward, voice casual in a way that absolutely was not, “You gave me the duck so easily, but then with Vox…”

Alastor’s grin flicked toward him instantly. “Oh?” he said lightly. “Did you feel slighted on his behalf?”

Lucifer scoffed. “Hardly. I’m just observing.”

Alastor turned his head fully, eyes meeting Lucifer’s. His tone was casual— Offhand, even— As if he were commenting on the weather. “Well,” he said, “I like you better than Vox.”

Silence.

Charlie blinked. “…Oh?”

Lucifer froze. Just for a moment. It was subtle, the slightest pause in his breathing, his grip on the plush duck tightening almost imperceptibly. His brows knit together, clearly searching for sarcasm, for some hidden knife in the words. “…You do?” He said finally.

Alastor shrugged. “Of course. You’re honest. Self-aware. Delightfully entertaining. Vox is loud, insecure, and constantly trying to sell me something.”

Charlie smiled, eyes soft. “That’s… Actually kind of sweet?”

Lucifer didn’t respond right away.

He looked down at the duck. At its lopsided bowtie. Then back at the table.

Charlie stood abruptly. “Okay! Drinks!” she said brightly, clearly sensing the moment but not wanting to smother it. “I’ll be right back!”

She disappeared into the crowd before either of them could object.

Left alone, Alastor leaned back in his chair, grin faint but genuine. “You seem surprised.”

Lucifer exhaled slowly. “You hate me.”

Alastor chuckled. “Untrue. You amuse me.”

“That’s not the same thing as liking me.”

“No,” Alastor agreed. “It isn’t. But neither is it the same as hatred.”

Lucifer didn’t press further. He didn’t need to.

Charlie returned a minute later, balancing three oversized cups filled with electric-blue slush, condensation dripping down the sides.

“Okay!” she said, setting them down. “Blue raspberry slushies! They’re my favorite.”

Lucifer eyed his cup suspiciously. “…Why is it glowing.”

“Flavor,” Charlie said confidently.

Alastor lifted his straw. “If this poisons me, I shall haunt you forever.” He took a sip. His eyes widened a fraction. “…Oh.”

Lucifer noticed immediately. He took a cautious sip of his own. Then another. “…Oh,” he echoed.

Charlie beamed. “Right?!”

“It’s cold,” Lucifer said, stunned. “And sweet. And— Refreshing?”

Alastor hummed approvingly, taking a longer drink. “Remarkable. It’s like drinking happiness.”

They drank.

And drank.

Minutes later, Charlie returned from the counter again, laughing as she set down another round of slushies.

“I thought you might want refills,” she said.

Lucifer cleared his throat. “This one… tastes slightly different.”

“That’s because I added sprinkles,” Charlie said.

Alastor raised his cup in a mock toast. “To unexpected pleasures.”

Lucifer hesitated— Then lifted his own. “…To unexpected pleasures,” he agreed.

And as they sat there— Blue tongues, melting ice, plush toys tucked close— It was, improbably, peaceful.

But only for a while.

Then, there was a subtle shift in the air, a quiet, unspoken tension that settled between Alastor and Lucifer as they both took another long pull from their slushies at the exact same time.

They paused.

Lowered their cups.

And looked at each other.

Lucifer raised an eyebrow. “You’re drinking that rather quickly.”

Alastor’s grin widened. “Am I? I was just thinking the same of you.”

Charlie’s eyes lit up instantly. ‘Oh no…’

Lucifer glanced at the rapidly lowering level of blue ice in Alastor’s cup. “…You trying to prove something?”

Alastor tilted his head. “Perish the thought.“

Charlie leaned forward. “Wait— Are you guys… Competing?”

Alastor took a long sip, eyes never leaving Lucifer’s. “He started it.”

Lucifer laughed. “I’m more than capable of finishing it.”

Charlie practically vibrated in her seat. “This is happening?”

Alastor straightened in his chair. “Very well. First one to finish wins.”

Charlie clapped. “Okay! No powers, no cheating, and if one of you gets brain freeze, you have to say it.”

They began.

Slurp after slurp, the sound obnoxiously loud. The icy blue levels dropped steadily, condensation pooling on the table. Alastor drank with unsettling composure, eyes half-lidded, like this was a pleasant afternoon tea. Lucifer, meanwhile, went at it with fierce determination, posture rigid, refusing to break eye contact.

Charlie watched like she was courtside at the greatest sporting event of her life.

Halfway through, Lucifer paused for just a fraction of a second.

Alastor noticed immediately. “My, my,” he teased. “Slowing down already, Your Majesty?”

Lucifer growled and took an even longer sip, emptying nearly a quarter of the cup in one go.

Charlie gasped. “Dad!”

Lucifer set the cup down sharply, eyes narrowing. “…You feel that?”

Alastor blinked. “…Ah.”

Charlie’s grin went feral. “Brain freeze?”

Lucifer clenched his jaw, absolutely refusing to acknowledge it. “Nonsense.” His hand twitched.

Alastor, unfortunately, was not spared. A second later, his ears flicked sharply, grin faltering for the briefest instant. “…Nnnh.”

Charlie pointed. “Oh my gosh, you both got it!”

“I did not,” Lucifer snapped.

Alastor laughed through the pain, static crackling faintly. “A fascinating sensation! Like an ice pick directly to the skull!”

They powered through.

Slurp. Slurp. Slurp.

They set their cups down.

Charlie blinked. “Wait— Was that—”

Alastor smiled sweetly. “A tie.”

Lucifer stared at him. Then, unexpectedly, he laughed. A short, incredulous sound, quickly smothered but unmistakably real. “…Of course it was.”

Charlie burst into applause. “That was AMAZING.”

Alastor dabbed his mouth with a napkin he conjured. “I must admit, Lucifer, you performed admirably.”

Lucifer nodded once, composed again. “You weren’t terrible yourself.”

They sat back in their chairs, identical blue stains on their tongues, melting cups discarded like trophies.

Charlie beamed at them, heart full. This— THIS—was what she’d hoped for. Her father and Alastor were bonding!

***

Charlie’s energy didn’t slow down.

She bounced through the mall like a spark of sunshine with legs, dragging Alastor and Lucifer behind her. The slushies had been a hit, and she was determined to keep the day going.

“Okay! Next stop—” she paused, scanning the directory, “— The novelty store! They have the cutest little things, and I think you guys would love them.”

Lucifer glanced down at his feet, which had begun to fidget. “Uh… Yes, sounds great! No problem!”

Alastor smiled faintly, though his expression had shifted subtly into something more tense. “Indeed! I could stay here for hours!”

They walked.

They browsed.

They tried on silly hats.

They laughed.

Lucifer and Alastor started moving more carefully, shifting their weight and looking everywhere except at each other.

Lucifer cleared his throat, eyes flicking to a row of stores that included a fancy jewelry kiosk, a perfume stand, and a small, discreet boutique that looked like it sold high-end coats.

“Charlie,” he said quietly, “Do you think we could… Maybe slow down a little? I’m feeling a bit… fatigued.”

Charlie tilted her head. “Oh! Sure! We can take a break!”

Alastor’s smile tightened, a faint shimmer of worry behind his eyes. “Yes, perhaps a slower pace would be wise.”

Lucifer didn’t look at him, but Alastor could feel the tension in the air— Could feel the way Lucifer was trying to manage his own discomfort without drawing attention, because HE was trying to do the very same thing.

Especially since he knew Vox was still somewhere in this mall… And, if they ran into each other again now, if Alastor’s present condition was noticeable…

Alastor swallowed, forcing his expression into calm. “I think I’d like to browse the antiques shop,” he said casually. “A bit of quiet. Some… Class.”

Charlie brightened. “Oh! That sounds nice!”

Lucifer nodded, relief hiding behind his composure. “Yes. Quiet is good.”

Alastor followed her to the shop, his posture stiff, mind racing. How had he ever thought drinking so much would be a good idea?! He knew perfectly well where all that liquid would go, how it would move through him extra quickly if he drank a ton of it all at once. And, he knew how HE was, he knew that the mall’s facilities would not offer him relief from his inevitable consequences!

For that matter, how had LUCIFER thought it would be a good idea? He suffered from the same plight as Alastor, and as Alastor understood it, the earliest beings that had been created were cursed with small bladders. It was a consequence of the design details not being smoothed out yet. There was no way Lucifer could hold as much as Alastor could, yet he’d drank the same amount.

And, Alastor was already getting very, very uncomfortable. There was a constant, pulsing feeling in his bladder that just got worse with every step. Like waves of liquid were being added to it each time he moved.

It must have been even worse for Lucifer.

To his surprise, that certainty didn’t bring Alastor any satisfaction. Of course, if he discovered that Vox shared his issue of stage-fright, and had an absolutely meager bladder compared to his own, he would be delighted. He would revel in it. It would be a just punishment for the crime of being Vox.

But, really, all Lucifer had done was annoy him sometimes. And this thing could get quite painful. Annoyance deserved annoyance in return, not torture.

Alastor felt… Something. He wasn’t sure, exactly. And, whatever it was, it wasn’t strong enough for him to do anything RIDICULOUS, like ask Lucifer how he was doing. Instead, he just kept walking.

Lucifer walked beside him.

Charlie’s cheerful stride slowed as she watched the two of them exchange glances that weren’t quite normal.

Lucifer’s eyes flicked sideways towards Alastor, his expression strained in a way that wasn’t just boredom or irritation. Alastor’s smile was still there, of course but it was too tight, too careful. His posture was stiff.

Charlie paused, turning to face them. “Hey— Are you guys okay? You both look… Weird.”

Lucifer’s mouth opened, then closed again.

Alastor shifted slightly, adjusting his collar.

Lucifer was struggling not to double over. His bladder felt beyond full, thighs straining and knees rubbing as he walked. Ohhh, why had he thought all those slushies were a good idea? He couldn’t even hold THAT much before the pressure got dire, and he’d definitely gone far beyond that limit. It was obvious when he looked down, his middle was visibly distended.

Ah, this was bad! This was very, very bad! No way would the bathrooms be usable for him here. And, by the time he got home, he knew he’d be in mind-breaking agony. It had happened plenty of times over his many, many years. He’d held it and held it, unable to release, and then his whole lower body exploded with intense pain, the urgent pangs rippling through every nerve he had.

Then, if he was really unlucky, a nasty infection would follow.

Stupid, delicious slushies! How the fuck was he supposed to avoid that fate now?! He needed somewhere private!

Maybe, if he just told Charlie, she’d FIND him a place he could actually let go? That would be so embarrassing! She was his daughter, and it wasn’t her job to take care of him, it was meant to be the other way around. Besides, if he notified Charlie, then the Radio Demon would probably overhear.

The… Radio Demon that had the exact same problem Lucifer did. And had drank just as much as him, too.

Lucifer paused and looked Alastor over as best as he could with his limited concentration. Alastor was a bit tenser than he’d been before. He still held the shark, and his grip on the thing was tight, hands flexing. The static that always accompanied him was louder than usual as well.

Shocking himself, Lucifer leaned closer to Alastor, speaking in a low whisper. “I need to—”

Alastor cut in, equally quiet. “I know.” His eyes flicked towards the crowd. “I do as well.”

Charlie blinked. “What are you guys whispering about?”

Alastor’s voice dropped even lower. “Something personal.”

Charlie frowned, looking between them. “Oh. Like… feelings?”

Lucifer nearly choked. “No.”

Alastor let out a soft sigh, the kind that meant he was trying to be polite but failing. “It’s… Delicate.”

Charlie tilted her head. “Okay… But, you’re whispering. That usually means it’s important.”

Lucifer’s expression tightened— Then softened, just slightly. He looked at Alastor again, like he was searching for reassurance.

Alastor met his gaze.

Then, with a subtle nod, he whispered, barely audible. “Alastor and I… Uh… Had too many slushies.”

Charlie’s eyes widened. She blinked. Then, slowly, her face lit up with the kind of realization that comes with sudden, embarrassing understanding. “Oh!” she said, a little too brightly. “Ohhhh.”

Alastor stiffened. Too many slushies felt like an understatement. He was regretting every drink he’d had this week!

Lucifer’s face flushed a shade darker. And he flinched when he realized they were approaching the massive fountain that served as the mall’s centerpiece; One more reminder of how badly he needed to get some fluid out of his body.

Charlie clapped her hands once, excited. “Okay! Okay, I get it!” She stepped back and gave them a reassuring smile, as if this were the most normal thing in the world. “Don’t worry. I can find you a private restroom.”

Alastor’s eyes narrowed slightly. “Private?”

Charlie nodded enthusiastically. “Yeah! Like, there’s gotta be a staff bathroom somewhere, right?”

Lucifer blinked. “A staff bathroom.”

Charlie pointed dramatically. “Exactly! We can ask someone! I mean, it’s no big deal. Everyone needs a bathroom sometimes.”

Alastor’s jaw tightened. “I don’t—”

Charlie was already moving, determination shining in her eyes. “Okay! Follow me! We’re going to find a private one, and you guys can… You know. Stop feeling uncomfortable.”

Lucifer opened his mouth to protest.

Alastor didn’t. He didn’t have time. He simply followed, moving quickly, eyes scanning his surroundings, heart pounding, because the last thing he wanted was for Vox to be nearby.

If Vox ever found out…

Alastor didn’t even want to imagine.

Charlie practically sprinted ahead, her enthusiasm driving her forward like a rocket. She waved her hand over the directory as if it were a magic map, scanning for any hint of “Staff Only” or “Employees.”

“Okay! Staff bathrooms are usually near the back,” she said, talking to herself as much as them. “Or sometimes by the food court… Or near the service halls!”

Lucifer and Alastor followed behind, both trying very hard to look calm.

Which, naturally, made them not look calm at all.

Lucifer walked with a stiff dignity, eyes forward, jaw set. But his stride was too rigid, like he was trying to keep every inch of his body under control. His hands were clasped tightly behind his back as if he were trying to lock away the urge in a metaphorical cage.

But, the need was so intense! His legs crossed when Charlie stopped at the directory. Then, when that wasn’t enough, he shifted his weight back and forth between his feet. Then, when THAT wasn’t enough, he shoved his hands into his pockets, straining to reach his crotch through them, because he REFUSED to hold himself in public.

Alastor, meanwhile, was doing what he always did when he was stressed: Smiling too much and laughing at nothing. “Ahahaha, yes,” he said brightly, voice overly cheerful. “Nothing like a mall’s charming architecture to soothe the soul!”

Internally, he was screaming. He bobbed on his heels a few times, his static amplifying with each little hop. He cupped a hand over his abdomen, as if he could somehow soothe the raging tides warring within him, and was shocked by how firm the area felt. His middle was as hard as a rock, the muscles stretched like taut rubber bands as they fought to contain all he’d had to drink.

Charlie glanced back at them. “You guys okay?”

Alastor blinked. “Absolutely. I’m simply… Appreciating the mall’s exquisite tilework.”

Lucifer winced, tearing his hands from his pockets and shoving them between his trembling thighs. Well, so much for refusing to grab himself in public… But, he couldn’t help it! He had to go so bad! “You’re talking like a brochure, Bambi.”

Charlie nodded. “Good. That means He’s not— Um— In pain.”

Alastor’s grin tightened. “Pain is such an overstatement.” His bladder spasmed again. Hard. He rocked back and forth on his feet. “Ahahaha, onwards now!”

They reached a hallway that led away from the main floor, past a few closed storefronts and into the quieter part of the mall. The lighting changed— Less bright, less public, more utilitarian.

Alastor’s posture stiffened even further. He wished he could go back in time and smack himself for even THINKING about having so many slushies. Oh, just the thought of all that liquid, so much at once and he hadn’t let ANY of it back out! The pain in his midsection was bright and sharp now, a brilliant point of urgency that stabbed his trembling opening with each step.

Lucifer’s breathing became shallow. His bladder was cramping. Tears pricked at the backs of his eyes, and he still hadn’t really been able to let go of himself, hands only releasing when they passed by someone else, and his aching abdomen searing with rage every time the badly needed reinforcement was stolen away.

Charlie kept walking, determined. “Okay!” she said. “There’s a maintenance door. It says ‘Employees Only.’ That’s a good sign!”

Lucifer’s eyes widened. “Charlie—”

Alastor whispered sharply, “Do not open that.”

Charlie paused, confused. “Why not?”

Lucifer leaned in, voice low. “Because it’s employees only.”

Charlie frowned. “Yeah? So?”

Alastor’s voice lowered into a tense whisper. “Because we are not employees, so it may cause a SCENE.”

Charlie blinked, then nodded slowly, like the gears in her head were turning. “Oh,” she said, realization dawning. “Right. Sorry.” She backed up a step. “Okay, okay. I’ll find another way.”

Alastor exhaled softly. “Thank you.”

Lucifer didn’t say anything. He just looked ahead, eyes scanning the corridor.

Charlie led them down another hallway, passing a closed bookstore, a shuttered electronics kiosk, and a janitor’s closet that looked promising. She stopped. “Oh!” she said, pointing. “That looks like it might lead to a staff area!”

Alastor squinted. “It’s labeled ‘Authorized Personnel Only.’”

Charlie nodded. “There’s probably a restroom.”

Lucifer’s expression tightened. “We cannot just—”

Charlie cut him off. “I’ll ask. It’ll be fine!”

Alastor’s eyes widened slightly. “Ask who?”

Charlie looked around. There was nobody. No staff. No security. No one at all. She took a breath, then knocked on the door.

A moment passed.

Then, a muffled voice answered from the other side. “Who is it?”

Charlie’s smile faltered. “Uh… Hi! We’re… Uh… customers. We were just wondering if there’s a private restroom we could use?”

The voice on the other side sighed. “There’s a staff restroom. But it’s for employees.”

Charlie’s eyes widened. “Oh! I totally get it. Sorry!”

Lucifer tensed, still grasping his crotch firmly. A spurt gushed forth and trickled along his inner thigh. “Ah— Ffffuck,” he choked out, chancing a look down. Ugh, WHY did his suit have to be white?! Even that tiny dribble had produced a small, visible dark spot… And plugging up the leak was painful enough to make him whimper.

Alastor’s smile became strained. The agony was worsening, and sweat started to roll down his back. He shook, eyes darting in all directions, as if some private facilities would pop out of thin air if he just wished hard enough. Ah… He’d never conjured anything THAT large before, but perhaps— No. His powers didn’t like to cooperate when his body was hurting this badly, when his mind was this distracted.

Charlie swallowed. “Wait— Maybe… Maybe if we ask nicely?”

Lucifer whispered sharply, “Charlie, don’t.”

“But they might say yes!”

Alastor’s eyes flashed. “They will not.”

Charlie, undeterred, called out again: “Please? It’s… A small emergency.”

The voice on the other side cleared its throat. “No. I’m sorry. But no.”

Charlie’s shoulders slumped. “Okay,” she said quietly. “Thanks anyway.”

Charlie turned back to them, looking defeated.

Alastor and Lucifer were both fidgeting restlessly, like they were about to come apart.

Charlie swallowed, then looked between them. “…Okay,” she said slowly. “We need another plan. We’ll find a spot. I promise.”

Lucifer nodded once, shoulders still stiff. “Good.”

Alastor forced his smile to stay on. “Yes. Thank you.”

Charlie’s face softened. “Don’t worry. I’ll figure it out.” She marched down the hallway like a tiny tornado of optimism, her mind racing for a solution that wouldn’t embarrass Alastor or Lucifer— Especially not in front of a staff member who might spread the story across Hell.

She turned a corner and saw a security desk tucked near a service entrance, the kind of place most customers never noticed unless they needed help.

A bored-looking security guard sat behind it, sipping a coffee and scrolling through a tablet.

Charlie took a deep breath. “Hi!” she chirped, approaching the desk with her brightest smile. “Sorry to bother you, but— Um— Do you know if there’s a staff restroom I could use?”

The guard glanced up, unimpressed. “Staff restroom? That’s for employees.”

Charlie nodded quickly. “Yeah, I know. But— Um— There’s a small… Issue.”

The guard raised an eyebrow. “Issue?”

Charlie glanced back toward Alastor and Lucifer, who stood a few paces behind her. They were constantly shifting. Lucifer had let go of himself, but his hands shook with the clear desire to move back to their previous location. Alastor’s static danced off of him in anxious bursts. Their eyes were clearly pleading. Charlie couldn’t just come right out and say that King of Hell and the Radio Demon were bursting, and she certainly couldn’t say they were bladder-shy.

She swallowed. Then she leaned in slightly, lowering her voice. “I’m… A little shy,” she said, voice quiet. “I don’t like public restrooms. I get really nervous.”

The guard blinked. “…Okay?”

Charlie nodded. “So if I could just use a staff restroom— Just for a second— Like, just to… Be in a more private space, that’d be great.”

The guard’s expression shifted. The coffee lowered.

Behind her, Lucifer’s eyes widened, and Alastor’s grin twitched.

The guard leaned back in his chair, considering. “You’re not an employee,” he said slowly.

“Nope!” Charlie chirped.

He hesitated. “Why not just use the public restroom?”

Charlie smiled tightly. “Because… I’m too nervous. I can’t go…”

The guard stared at her for a moment longer, then sighed. “Fine,” he said finally. “But just this once. And don’t make a habit of it.”

Charlie’s relief was immediate. She practically glowed. “Thank you! Thank you so much!”

The guard stood, unlocking a door behind the desk and pointing down a narrow hallway. “This is the staff corridor. There’s a restroom at the end. Don’t get lost.”

Charlie nodded vigorously. “Okay! Thank you!”She turned back to Alastor and Lucifer, motioning them forward with a quick hand gesture. “Come on!” she whispered. “We can use it. Just… Follow me.”

Lucifer and Alastor exchanged a look.

“You told him it was you?” Lucifer asked.

Charlie whispered back, “I had to!”

Alastor’s voice was a low, amused hiss. “But, why? Wasn’t it embarrassing?”

Charlie blinked. “Kind of. But, I mean, I don’t have this ‘big, scary overlord’ reputation to worry about.”

Alastor’s lips pressed together. “I see…”

Charlie led them down the corridor, past a series of staff doors and storage closets, trying her best to move quickly without looking frantic. Alastor’s posture remained stiff, and Lucifer walked with a careful, controlled stride, but their relief was obvious.

They reached the restroom door.

Charlie pushed it open.

The room was clean, quiet, and— Thankfully—empty.

Alastor let out a breath.

Lucifer looked around, eyes darting as if expecting someone to appear from the shadows.

Charlie stepped aside, giving them space.

“Okay,” she said softly, “You guys take turns, I’ll guard the door.”

Lucifer’s voice softened. “Thank you.”

Alastor nodded once. “Yes. Thank you.” He looked to Lucifer. “After you, my dear.”

Lucifer’s eyes widened, the grip on the duck, still under his arm, intensified. “You’re sure?”

“Yes,” Alastor said. “I’m a gentleman.”

“… Sure you are,” Lucifer said, but he stepped inside, closing the door.

Charlie stood with Alastor in the hallway, heart pounding, hoping like crazy that this would work out for both of them.

Behind the closed door, Lucifer breathed a sigh of relief. He set the duck down on the counter and hurried for the toilet. Another slosh of damp warmth spilled as he got his zipper down, and he cringed. But, the mortification was quickly overshadowed by pleasure when he finally let himself unclench.

The stream poured away, his muscles loosening with bliss. A quiet murmur of relief fell from his lips as he drained himself of his torment. He was vaguely aware of how Alastor was still outside the door, possibly able to hear Lucifer’s urgent flow, but somehow that didn’t bother him as much as it would if anyone else were in that position. Alastor knew what this felt like, what Lucifer had gone through.

When it slowed to a stop, he zipped himself up and inspected the damage from his leaks. There were damp spots, but he could hide them if he kept his legs close enough together… Or held the duck in front of himself.

He emerged moments later, doing exactly that. Charlie didn’t seem to suspect why he was keeping the duck in that position, and he didn’t have time to gauge what Alastor’s reaction was. The Radio Demon pushed straight past him and into the vacant restroom right away.

Well… Lucifer could understand that.

Inside, Alastor yanked down his zipper, all his restraint gone now that he had his much-needed privacy. Charlie was right outside, ensuring no one would interrupt this delicate process, or overhear it, so Alastor faced little resistance from his muscles when he told himself to give in.

Ohhh, that felt amazing… His middle seemed to be slowly deflating, the immense strain fading away trickle by trickle as he just allowed nature to take its course— Albeit aiming very carefully to keep it as quiet as possible

A full minute passed before the last of it had finally made it out, heaving a heavy exhale as he zipped himself back up. “Ahhh… Well now, that’s an improvement.”

He plucked the shark back up from the counter where he’d left it in his haste, pushing the door open.

He looked… Better.

Not just physically. He carried himself like a weight had been lifted, his shoulders relaxing, his smile returning to its normal, confident shape. His eyes, however, were still sharp— Alert, scanning the hallway, like he was still worried someone else had overheard him. Charlie nodded enthusiastically. “Yeah! You were so brave! You handled it so well!”

Charlie shrugged. “Yeah! You didn’t freak out. You didn’t make a scene. You stayed calm. And you both managed to go!”

Lucifer’s lips pressed together, but there was a hint of something like warmth there.

Alastor’s gaze flicked to Lucifer for a moment, then back to Charlie. “I’d like to add, for the record, that you have a rather… Unusual talent for making even the most awkward situations manageable.”

Charlie laughed. “Thanks! That’s what I’m here for!”

Lucifer’s expression softened slightly. “It’s… Appreciated.”

Alastor nodded. “Yes. Back to the fun.”

The elevator screamed like it was dying.

Then THUNK.

Silence followed, thick and suffocating, broken only by the low electrical hum of Vox’s screen-face struggling to recalibrate.

Valentino was sprawled half against the brass railing, cigarette dangling loosely from his fingers, pink smoke curling lazily upward. “Relax, doll,” he purred, unfazed. “We’ve been through worse than a little hiccup. Remember that time the studio caught fire and we just—”

The elevator lights flickered again, dimming to a sickly red emergency glow.

Valentino paused mid-sentence.

“…Okay. Maybe worse than that.”

Vox finally found his voice, the screen of his face glitching briefly into static before snapping back into a sharp, irritated grin. “Alright. Nobody panic. This is my building.” He jabbed a finger at the control panel, which sparked indignantly in response. “My systems don’t just fail.”

He pressed the button for the executive floor again.

Nothing.

He pressed it harder.

Still nothing.

A thin curl of black smoke slithered out from behind the buttons.

Vox’s grin twitched. “That’s… New.”

Valentino laughed, low and amused, flicking ash onto the floor without looking. “Oh, this is adorable. The tech king trapped in a tin box...”

“Don’t start,” Vox snapped, sparks crackling faintly along the edges of his screen. “This is probably just a minor power reroute. I’ll override it manually.” He slapped his palm against the panel.

Calculated.

Vox straightened, static dancing across his face as he forced control back into his voice. “Now we wait. The system will reset. It always does.”

None of them moved.

Valentino’s grin slowly, wickedly returned. “Well,” he said, eyes gleaming in the red light. “Looks like Hell’s decided to lock the three most powerful Overlords in a box.”

Vox didn’t wait for them to open any further.

He bolted.

A glorious, unassuming door with a small, glowing restroom symbol.

Valentino skidded to a stop just behind him. “You got it. You’re there.”

The lock clicked.

That was all Vox managed before his strength finally gave out.

“I— I made it,” he whispered, more disbelief than triumph as he tore down his zipper.

The moment he let go, the pressure that had been crushing him for what felt like hours finally released— All at once, overwhelming and unstoppable. Vox sagged forward with a sound halfway between a laugh and a sob, knees threatening to buckle as relief crashed through him in a dizzying wave.

His screen flickered wildly—then steadied, colors smoothing out, static melting away into blessed, quiet clarity.

The pain vanished almost instantly, replaced by a deep, full-body looseness that made him feel ten pounds lighter and infinitely more alive. His shoulders slumped, tension draining from every joint, every wire, every clenched thought that had been screaming at him to hold on.

Vox straightened slowly once he’d finished, one hand braced on the wall as he caught his breath, the hum of the building suddenly just… Background noise. Normal. Manageable. His screen-face glowed faintly, steady and clear for the first time since the elevator stopped.

Inside, Vox took one last slow breath, savoring the calm like it might evaporate if he moved too fast. He took a moment longer than strictly necessary before opening the door.

When he did, he stepped back into the hallway with his posture fully restored— Back straight, chin up, screen-face polished into a smug, perfectly calibrated grin. If anyone hadn’t witnessed the past half hour, they’d think he’d just left a board meeting, not narrowly escaped the most undignified fate imaginable.

Valentino looked him up and down slowly. “…Wow,” he said. “Look at that. Rebooted. Radiating ‘I absolutely did not almost piss myself in an elevator’ energy.”

Vox didn’t miss a beat. “Choose your next words very carefully.”

Velvette leaned off the wall, arms crossed, eyes sparkling with wicked delight. “Oh please, don’t be shy. We were bonding. Trauma does that.”

“I was under extreme circumstances,” Vox snapped. “Any one of you would’ve—”

Velvette laughed outright. “You ran, Vox. Like full-on, dignity-abandoned, ‘clear the hallway’ ran.”

Vox’s screen flickered, a faint blue blush bleeding through the pixels. “I was moving with purpose.”

“With purpose,” Valentino echoed. “Sure. Is that what we’re calling it now?”

Velvette tapped her chin thoughtfully. “You know, I think this might actually be good for your brand.”

Vox whirled on her. “Don’t you dare.”

“I will delete every social media account you own,” Vox hissed.

Valentino slung an arm around Vox’s shoulders before he could retreat, giving him a friendly— But absolutely infuriating— Squeeze. “C’mon, lighten up. You made it. That’s what counts. And, let me say… That performance? I’m gonna be thinking about it for a LONG time.”

Vox stiffened, then— Against his will— Let out a long sigh. “…I hate both of you.”

Velvette smirked. “Aww. He’s grateful.”

Valentino laughed, steering them back towards the elevator. “So what do we say, Voxxy?”

Vox glared at the doors, then at them. “…We take the stairs.”

*009*2026-05-04 13:59

The red “ON AIR” sign was still glowing.

Vox’s smile, all sharp angles and static-polished charm, stayed perfectly in place as the broadcast rolled on. Cameras floated in their lazy orbits. Screens across Pentagram City flickered with his face, larger than life, confidence distilled into pixels.

But, underneath all of that was a growing, deeply inconvenient problem.

It had started small. A background sensation, easy to dismiss. Vox had dismissed far worse things during live television: Riots, blackouts, co-host meltdowns, even the occasional attempted assassination. Compared to that, a mild need to use the restroom was nothing.

So he ignored it.

He launched into the segment with his usual gusto, voice smooth and electric as he leaned against the sleek desk. “Now, folks, you’re not gonna believe what our competitors are failing at today—”

A twinge cut through his bladder— And his focus.

Vox’s screen-face flickered for half a frame before stabilizing. He didn’t miss a beat vocally, but internally, he paused. That wasn’t nothing anymore. That was… Noticeable.

He adjusted his stance, shifting his weight as if it were a natural part of his performance. His internal processors—Usually reserved for ratings analysis and brand domination— Rerouted briefly to problem-solving.

‘Okay. Fine. Minor issue,’ he told himself. ‘You’ve done plenty of hour-long specials. You can do this.’

The teleprompter scrolled on. Vox kept reading, kept gesturing, kept smiling. He punctuated jokes with perfectly timed finger-guns and leaned closer to the camera for emphasis. The audience at home saw a flawless media mogul in control of every second.

Behind the scenes, the seconds were starting to feel longer.

Another wave of urgency rolled in, stronger this time. Vox’s fingers curled against the edge of the desk, the metal cool under his grip. He laughed— Just a touch sharper than usual— And straightened again.

“Ha! Moving on,” he said, a fraction too quickly.

Somewhere off-camera, one of the studio imps gave a thumbs-up, clearly pleased with how smoothly things were going. Vox returned the gesture with a confident nod while mentally screaming.

‘Why did I skip my last break?’ he thought. He remembered it now: Velvette had been ranting, the ratings had spiked, and Vox had waved off the scheduled intermission like the professional he was. Breaks were for people who didn’t run an empire.

Regret, it turned out, was also for people who ran empires.

He crossed one leg over the other beneath the desk, a move subtle enough that no one would question it. His voice dipped into a more controlled cadence, buying himself time. “Now, stay tuned, because coming up next—”

The teleprompter froze.

Vox blinked once. Twice. His smile tightened, then broadened, turning the glitch into a bit. “Ah, you gotta love live TV, folks!”

Laughter track. Good. He leaned back, casually, but the movement made his core tense. The need had graduated from inconvenient to distracting. His internal monologue was no longer polite.

‘You are a television god,’ he scolded himself. ‘You will not be defeated by basic biology.’

The studio lights felt hotter. He became acutely aware of every second passing, every line of script between him and freedom. He sped up just slightly, trimming pauses, condensing commentary. To the untrained eye, he was just energetic.

To Vox, it was a race against the ticking clock in his midsection.

Velvette’s voice crackled in his earpiece. “Two more minutes, Vox.”

Two minutes might as well have been two hours.

He nodded, grin unwavering. “You heard it here first!” he announced, leaning forward again, elbows on the desk. The posture helped. A little. He focused on the camera lens like it was an opponent to be conquered, channeling all his attention outwards.

‘Just hold it together,’ he thought. ‘You know ALASTOR would be able to…’

At the thought of his friend-turned-nemesis, Vox clenched his thighs together. Alastor, he knew, had some kind of iron bladder. How in the FUCK he’d managed to handle night after night of drinking back when they’d been on good terms, without EVER taking a piss-break, was beyond Vox.

Alastor’s waist was so narrow that Vox could put one hand around it and have room to spare (Not that Alastor had ever LET him do that…) How much space could he HAVE in there for a bladder? Apparently, quite a bit. Because, in all the time he and Alastor had known each other, he’d barely gotten any indication at all that Alastor EVER needed to go.

Occasionally, if Alastor actually got himself drunk, and Vox needed to walk him home, he’d… Say things along the way. Muffled, half-slurred statements. Things like; “Oh… Mmmf… Do… Do hurry, Voxshhh… Even… Even the Radio Demon musssht… Mmmh… I think I needsh t’pee…”

And Vox rolled his eyes, not actually THAT annoyed because an intoxicated, stumbling Alastor was an Alastor that was willing to lean on him— Willing to touch him… “WHY didn’t you go back at the bar?! I asked if you needed to, remember?”

Alastor then started to laugh. “Ohhhh, Vox. You don’t knowwwww!”

Vox huffed. “Yes, Alastor. There’s a lot of shit I don’t know about you. I wish you’d talk to me.”

Alastor just kept laughing, broken up by hiccups and flinches when his need for the restroom apparently spiked. “You only know what I CHOOSE t’tell… I choose to… Tell… Mmmf… That I… I need’t’pee…”

“We’ve established that. How about you tell me why you didn’t go at the bar?”

More laughter. “It’s a seeeecret!”

“Oh, I’m sure. Let me guess, you ‘didn’t have to go then’! You’re like a child when you’re this wasted.”

“Nooope! I did have to go then…”

“Then, what the fuck? Are you just INTO this?” Vox wouldn’t have minded if Alastor was… He’d been trying for so long to find ANYTHING Alastor was into. He’d tried plenty of sexy outfits to get the Radio Demon’s attention, but at BEST Alastor would have no reaction at all. At worst, he’d ask Vox why he’d put on something so ridiculous.

If piss-holding was what got Alastor off, Vox had no qualms with indulging him. He’d do ANYTHING for—

“Into this… Huh?”

“Is it your kink? Fetish? Whatever they called it when you were alive? Does holding your piss turn you on?”

Alastor stopped laughing. “Nooo,” he said. “Why’s… Why’s everything gotta be about sex stuff here… ‘S gross…”

“What IS it about, then? You’re squirming. And you drank, like, half your own weight back at the bar. Do you want me to find you an alleyway, or something?”

Alastor shook his head vehemently, which to Vox, still holding him upright, felt like a nuzzle. His screen flickered. His heart pounded. “You can make it home?” He finally asked after a moment.

“Mm-hm…”

And, he DID make it home. He was so inebriated that Vox had to unlock the door for him, and he didn’t invite Vox inside. He rarely did, yet Vox still felt disappointed every time. He simply nodded, gripping the doorframe to remain standing, legs crossing back and forth. “Thankshyou, Vox. Now I won’t ’splode.” Then, he slammed the door closed.

He’d held what seemed like an emergency-level piss for several blocks while drunk off his— Very adorable— Ass. Vox wasn’t sure WHY (Maybe he’d just been so trashed that he hadn’t registered the urge until it was intense?), but it obviously meant he had a strong bladder.

Alastor never paused his broadcasts to use the restroom, either. Vox would KNOW if anything that embarrassing had ever happened to him.

Vox could do the same.

The countdown began in the corner of his vision. Ten seconds. Vox delivered his sign-off with extra flair, voice ringing with triumphant finality. “And that’s the truth, whether they like it or not!”

Five. Four. Three.

He didn’t blink.

Two. One.

The red light clicked off.

The moment it did, Vox straightened abruptly, the performance mask cracking just enough for him to exhale through clenched teeth. “Cut,” he snapped, already stepping away from the desk.

The crew barely had time to react before he was striding toward the exit, dignity held together by sheer force of will. “Excellent show, everyone!” he called over his shoulder, voice still smooth, pace just a bit faster than usual.

The studio door swung shut behind him.

Vox disappeared down the hallway, ratings intact, reputation flawless, and lesson learned— At least for today; Next broadcast, he would be taking the break.

The hallway lights blurred slightly as Vox picked up speed.

Not running, but moving with purpose, long strides clicking sharply against the polished floor. His composure was back in place, but only just. Every step was careful, every breath measured, as he rounded the corner towards the executive restroom reserved for the Vees.

‘Almost there,’ he thought, relief already beginning to edge in.

He reached the door.

And stopped.

Bright yellow tape stretched across it, fluttering cheerfully, like it hadn’t just ruined his entire afterlife. A hand-lettered sign hung crookedly from the handle: CLOSED FOR CLEANING

Vox stared at it.

For a full, silent second, his screen-face went completely blank.

Then static rippled across it in a sharp, irritated buzz.

“You have got to be kidding me,” he muttered, voice dropping out of broadcast polish and into pure disbelief.

As if summoned by the complaint, the door cracked open just enough for a cloud of aggressively floral cleaning fumes to escape. An imp janitor stood inside, goggles on, holding a mop like a weapon. “Can’t come in, sir,” the imp said quickly. “Biohazard protocol.”

Vox pinched the bridge of his nose. “Biohazard?” he repeated, each syllable vibrating. “What kind of disaster happened in there?!”

The imp winced. “Uh. Mr. Valentino.”

Of course.

Vox’s eye twitched. “What,” he said very calmly, “Did Valentino do?”

The imp glanced back into the bathroom, where the sounds of industrial ventilation hummed ominously. “There was a photoshoot,” he said. “I don’t ask questions.”

And, Vox absolutely did not want answers. He gestured sharply. “How long.”

The imp checked a clipboard. “Thirty minutes. Minimum.”

Thirty minutes.

Vox’s processors screamed.

He straightened, hands clasped behind his back, posture rigid enough to look statuesque instead of desperate.

“Sir,” the imp said, “The one in the lobby is still—“

Vox was already gone.

He pivoted on his heel and headed back down the corridor. His stride lengthened again, just a fraction, as he ran calculations in his head.

‘Lobby. Lobby. Fuck, I’m gonna explode. Elevator or stairs? Elevator might get stuck. Stairs will hurt…’

He ducked into the stairwell, the door slamming shut behind him with more force than strictly necessary. The echo rang up and down the concrete shaft. Vox paused for half a second, one hand braced against the wall, jaw tight.

“This is fine,” he told himself firmly. “This is manageable. You have handled worse.”

He resumed moving, descending with stiff precision, each step measured to avoid any sudden movements that might betray him. His mind tried— Unsuccessfully—to distract itself by replaying ratings numbers, ad revenue projections, anything but the problem at hand.

Halfway down, his earpiece crackled again.

“Vox?” Velvette’s voice chimed in, sharp and curious. “Why’d you bolt like that? You okay?”

Vox did not slow. “Everything is under control,” he snapped automatically.

There was a pause. “…You sound weird.”

“I sound busy,” he shot back. “If Valentino asks where I am, tell him he owes me a clean bathroom.”

Velvette laughed. “Oh. Oh. Wow. How bad do you need to go?”

“Do not,” Vox warned.
