The stairs finally ended. He shoved the door open and scanned the lobby like a man searching for an oasis.

The restroom sign gleamed at the far end.

Vox locked onto it and marched forwards, focus narrowing, pride hanging on by a thread. He could hear muffled sounds from behind the door—Someone inside, apparently taking their time.

Vox stopped short.

His smile reappeared, thin and dangerous. “Of course,” he said softly.

He folded his arms, tapping one foot against the floor in a rapid, irritated rhythm, eyes fixed on the door as if sheer authority might force it open faster.

He stood rigid, shoulders squared, arms crossed so tightly they might as well have been welded there. His screen-face cycled through muted static patterns, the visual equivalent of gritting one’s teeth.

‘Any second now,’ he told himself. ‘Any second they’ll come out.’

Vox inhaled sharply and looked up at the ceiling, as if appealing to the universe. “Unbelievable,” he muttered. “I run the largest media network in Hell, and this is how I go out.”

Another surge of urgency made him stiffen completely. He froze, recalibrating, every ounce of focus turned inward. This was no longer about dignity; This was about survival.

No. No, this was not happening.

The restroom door remained shut.

That decided it.

Vox turned on his heel and strode back through the lobby, posture tight but purposeful. ‘Think,’ he ordered himself. ‘Think.’

The tower had other restrooms, yes, but reaching them would require climbing more stairs. And the clock was very much not on his side.

Which left one option; The city.

The main doors of V Tower slid open as Vox emerged into the perpetual neon dusk. The noise hit him immediately: Car horns, shouting demons, distant music thumping through brick and bone. Normally, this was his element. His domain.

Right now, it was an obstacle course.

Vox paused on the steps for half a second, scanning the streets. Bars, clubs, shops— Plenty of places, theoretically. The problem was getting into one quickly without drawing attention or being stopped.

He straightened his jacket, forcibly smoothing it down. “You can do this,” he told himself under his breath. “Just walk. Like a normal, unbothered overlord.”

He stepped onto the sidewalk.

Every step ached now, his pace brisk but controlled. He passed by a bar with a line out the door. Not a chance. He shot a glance at a café—Closed. Typical.

His internal commentary grew increasingly sharp. ‘Why is everything either locked or crowded?!’

A couple of lesser demons pointed as he passed. “Hey, that’s Vox—”

He did not slow. He did not acknowledge them. His attention was locked forward, eyes scanning for anything with a restroom sign.

Another wave hit him, and he stopped short, just long enough to steady himself, one hand braced against a streetlamp. His screen-face dimmed for a fraction of a second, then snapped back to full brightness.

“Okay,” he hissed. “Okay. New plan.”

Across the street, a rundown but blessedly quiet video rental store flickered to life as its sign buzzed on. No crowd. No line. And, crucially, a glowing PUBLIC RESTROOMS sign barely visible through the grime on the window.

Vox’s eyes locked onto it like it was a miracle.

He crossed the street with renewed determination, every remaining ounce of composure channeled into forward motion. The bell over the shop door jingled as he entered, and cool, stale air washed over him.

A bored demon clerk looked up from behind the counter. “Uh. Welcome.”

Vox didn’t bother with pleasantries. “Restroom,” he said.

“Back left,” the clerk replied, blinking.

Vox was already halfway there.

He reached the door, hand closing around the handle, and paused.

He inhaled slowly, deliberately, forcing his posture straight again. Whatever happened next, he would face it as Vox: overlord, icon, and master of his own image.

Then he opened the door and stepped inside, finally, blessedly out of the chaos of the city.

The door creaked shut behind him.

Vox took exactly one step forward— And stopped.

The restroom was… A disaster. Cracked tiles, flickering light, something dripping that absolutely should not have been dripping, and a smell that hit like a physical wall. Vox’s screen-face reacted before his pride could stop it, glitching violently as every internal alarm screamed NO.

He stared.

Then he slowly, carefully stepped back.

“Nope,” he said flatly.

Another surge of urgency made the decision for him. Vox pivoted and was out the door in an instant, dignity shedding like loose pixels as he stormed past the confused clerk.

“Restroom’s out of order,” Vox snapped without stopping. “Hire a fucking cleaning crew.”

The bell jingled wildly behind him as he burst back onto the street.

The city suddenly felt much larger.

Vox moved faster now, restraint replaced by necessity. He weaved between demons, ignored shouted greetings and insults alike, and scanned every building with mounting intensity. His internal narration had devolved into blunt commands. ‘Find somewhere. Now.’

Bars and clubs were packed. Shops were either locked or looked just as bad as the last one. His posture had gone rigid again, every muscle engaged in the effort of holding it together, both literally and figuratively.

Then he saw it.

The sign was unmistakable, even through the neon clutter of the street: HAZBIN HOTEL

Vox skidded to a halt.

He stared at the building like it might bite him. Of all places…

His processors immediately supplied the problem, complete with a flashing mental warning label: ‘Alastor.’

The last person Vox wanted to encounter on a day that was already rapidly becoming a personal humiliation reel.

Vox clenched his jaw. “Absolutely not,” he muttered.

Another wave hit— Stronger, sharper, leaving absolutely no room for debate.

He froze, then exhaled through his teeth. “…Fine,” he growled.

He straightened his jacket one last time, tugged his composure back into something passable, and marched up the steps. Each one felt like a personal betrayal, but survival outweighed pride.

The front door swung open.

Warm light spilled out, along with noise— Voices, laughter, the general chaos that followed the hotel like a curse. Vox stepped inside and immediately felt every eye turn toward him.

The lobby went quiet.

Charlie blinked. “Oh! Uh—Welcome?”

Vox did not stop walking. “Restroom,” he said briskly, not looking at anyone in particular.

From somewhere behind the front desk came a familiar, cheerful voice, smooth as velvet and twice as dangerous. “Well now,” Alastor drawled. “What an unexpected pleasure!”

Vox’s shoulders stiffened. He stopped just short of the hallway, turned slowly, and fixed Alastor with a tight, brittle smile. “Radio Demon,” he said. “Lovely as always. I’m just passing through.”

Alastor’s grin widened, eyes glinting with unmistakable amusement. “I’m sure…”

Vox resisted the urge to snarl. “Point me to a restroom,” he snapped, “And, I’ll be gone before you can start a monologue.”

Charlie glanced between them, sensing the tension. “Uh—Yeah! There’s one just down the hall, second door on the right.”

Vox did not thank her. He simply turned and marched off, pace brisk and focus absolute, every step fueled by sheer willpower.

He made it three steps down the hallway.

Then the lights flickered.

The temperature dropped just enough to be noticeable, the air filling with a faint hum of radio static. Vox stopped mid-stride, shoulders tensing as a shadow stretched across the carpet in front of him— Long, antlered, unmistakable.

“Ah-ah,” Alastor said pleasantly, his voice suddenly right beside Vox’s ear. “I’m afraid I can’t allow that.”

Vox froze.

Slowly, he turned his head. Alastor stood there with his hands clasped behind his back, smile wide and eyes sharp, blocking the hallway.

Vox’s screen-face glitched, then reassembled into a tight, forced grin. “Out of my way,” he said, clipped and controlled. “I don’t have time for your games.”

Alastor tilted his head. “Oh, I disagree! You see, when YOU show up unannounced at MY establishment, I feel the need to keep an eye on things.”

Vox shifted his weight, immediately regretting it when the pressure flared against the base of his member. He straightened again, hands curling into fists at his sides. “I told you. I’m here for the restroom. That’s it.”

Alastor hummed. “Mmm. Funny thing about that.” He leaned in just a bit, eyes gleaming. “I don’t believe you.”

Vox let out a short, humorless laugh. “You think I came all this way to snoop around your sad little redemption project?”

“I think,” Alastor said cheerfully, “That you’re a schemer, a spy, and a walking surveillance network with legs. And I think you’d LOVE to poke your nose where it doesn’t belong.”

Behind them, Charlie hovered anxiously at the edge of the lobby. “Alastor, maybe he really just needs to—”

Alastor raised a finger without looking away from Vox. “Now, now, Charlie. One can never be too careful.”

Vox clenched his jaw. The delay was doing him no favors. His posture had gone rigid again, every muscle locked as he fought to maintain control. “Listen to me very carefully,” he said through his teeth. “I am not interested in your hotel, your guests, or your nonsense. I am experiencing a… Time-sensitive situation.”

Alastor’s smile twitched. “Oh?” he said lightly. “Is that what they’re calling espionage these days?”

Vox’s screen-face flickered hard, static hissing across it for half a second before he forcibly stabilized it. “You are insufferable.”

“And you,” Alastor replied, voice sweet as poison, “Are not wandering my halls without supervision.” He stepped fully into Vox’s path now, blocking the hallway completely. Vox had to stop short, biting back a sharp inhale. The proximity was unbearable— Alastor’s presence alone was irritating on a good day, and today was very much not that.

Vox crossed his arms tightly, more to keep himself together than for attitude. “Then escort me,” he snapped. “Stand outside the door if it makes you feel powerful.”

Alastor studied him. Really studied him. His eyes flicked just briefly over Vox’s stiff posture, the tension in his stance, the way he wasn’t pacing or posturing the way he normally would. The grin never left Alastor’s face, but something thoughtful sparked behind it.

“Well,” Alastor said slowly, drawing the word out, “You do seem… Unusually motivated.”

Vox glared. “Your point?”

Alastor chuckled, straightening. “My point is that I don’t trust you as far as I can throw you.” He stepped aside just enough to gesture down the hall. “But I’m very curious to see what you do next.”

Vox didn’t hesitate. He moved forward immediately, pace quick and controlled, not daring to look back. “Fine,” he said stiffly. “Follow me. Supervise. Whatever satisfies your paranoia.”

“Oh, gladly,” Alastor replied, falling into step beside him, voice low and delighted. “After all— If you ARE lying…” His grin widened. “I wouldn’t want to miss the moment you’re caught.”

Vox said nothing. He couldn’t afford to. Every second counted now, and with Alastor at his side, the pressure— Literal and otherwise— Had never felt higher.

Alastor walked alongside Vox with an easy, almost jaunty stride, hands clasped behind his back as if this were nothing more than a pleasant tour. Inside, however, his thoughts churned.

‘No. Absolutely not.’

He watched Vox from the corner of his eye— The rigid posture, the clipped movements, the way his attention was laser-focused straight ahead. Vox was always observing, always cataloging. That was what made him dangerous.

That meeting… The way Vox’s gaze had lingered on him for far too long. The questions. And then the nightmares… Nightmares where Vox’s grin filled every screen in Hell, broadcasting a scandal with relish. Headlines screaming about the Radio Demon’s little secret. Laugh tracks. Static applause. Alastor standing helplessly as his carefully curated mystique unraveled over something so absurdly… Personal.

It was unacceptable.

So now here Vox was, claiming he “just needed the restroom,” marching around the hotel’s halls like he belonged there.

Alastor did not believe that for a second.

“Well,” Alastor said lightly, breaking the silence as they walked, “You’ll forgive me if I find your sudden visit suspicious.”

Vox didn’t look at him. “I don’t owe you forgiveness.”

They reached the hallway intersection. The restroom was just beyond it— Close enough that Vox could practically feel relief within reach. He slowed despite himself, fists clenched so tightly his knuckles buzzed with static.

“I gain nothing from this,” Vox said stiffly. “Unless you count not humiliating myself in public.”

Alastor stopped walking.

That made Vox stop too.

Alastor turned to face him fully, grin unwavering but eyes sharp, searching. “Humiliating yourself?” he echoed. “My, that’s an unusual concern for you.”

Vox bristled. “Move.”

Instead, Alastor stepped directly in front of the hallway again, blocking it completely. The radio static around him intensified, a low, possessive hum.

“I think,” Alastor said carefully, “That you’re trying to bait me. Poke around. Find something you can twist into entertainment.”

Vox stared at him in disbelief. “You think this is about you?”

“Most things you DO are about me,” Alastor leaned down slightly, smile tight. “I think you’ve been itching to uncover something sensitive. And I think you’ve convinced yourself that this”—he gestured vaguely—“Is your opportunity.”

Vox’s screen-face flickered violently, irritation and desperation bleeding through the pixels. “You are not that interesting,” he snapped. “I don’t care about your habits, your fears, or whatever bizarre hang-up you’re projecting onto me.”

Alastor stiffened. Projecting. That hit closer than Vox could possibly know. For just a fraction of a second, Alastor’s smile wavered. Then it snapped back into place, brighter than ever. “Mm. That’s exactly what someone lying would say.”

Behind them, Charlie shifted awkwardly. “Alastor… Maybe we should just let him—”

“No,” Alastor said sweetly, without turning. “I think we’ll do this my way.”

Vox’s breathing had gone shallow. He stood unnaturally still now, every bit of him screaming to move, to hurry, to not be standing here while Alastor played mind games.

“Last warning,” Vox said, voice low and buzzing. “Get out of my way.”

Alastor chuckled, though there was tension beneath it. “I’m afraid I can’t do that, old sport.” His eyes gleamed.

And with that, the two overlords stood locked in place— One driven by urgent necessity, the other by spiraling paranoia— Neither willing to back down, and the restroom just a few agonizing steps away.

Vox’s composure finally began to fracture in small, undeniable ways that were impossible to smooth over. His foot shifted. Then shifted again. His shoulders tensed and released in sharp, uneven intervals, like he was fighting his own body for control. The glow of his screen-face jittered, static popping faintly at the edges. “Okay,” Vox said, voice tight and far less polished than usual, “this is no longer a discussion.”

He took a step forward.

Then stopped short, sharply inhaling, the movement had been a mistake. He planted his feet again, posture rigid, arms pulled in close to his sides like he was holding himself together by force alone. “O—Ohhh…”

Alastor noticed.

Of course he did.

The Radio Demon’s smile sharpened, but his eyes narrowed, studying Vox with intense scrutiny. He circled half a step, like a predator deciding whether the prey’s limp was real.

“Oh, you’re QUITE the performer,” Alastor said lightly. “Trembling, pacing, urgency in the voice—Very convincing.”

Vox shot him a glare so fierce the hallway lights flickered. “I am NOT performing.”

Another wave of urgency hit him hard enough that he actually hissed through his teeth. He doubled over for a second before catching himself, straightening with visible effort. His hands clenched and unclenched, fingers buzzing with static.

“I swear,” Vox snapped, “if you do not move right now, I am going to—”

“Going to what?” Alastor interrupted cheerfully. “Storm off and sulk? Broadcast a smear campaign? Or perhaps”—his grin widened—“This is the part where you dramatically reveal it was all an act?”

Vox couldn’t stand still anymore. He paced two short steps, stopped, then paced back, movements tight and frantic rather than calculated. The usual confidence in his stride was gone, replaced with raw impatience and barely restrained panic.

“This is real,” Vox said, through clenched teeth. “I skipped a break. Valentino contaminated my restroom. The city failed me. And now YOU are in my way.”

Alastor’s smile did not falter, but something uncertain flickered behind his eyes. Still, his nerves won. “Mmm,” he hummed. “Or perhaps you’ve decided that embarrassing me is worth a little method acting.”

Vox let out a sharp, frustrated sound that was half-laugh, half-growl. “You think I would put myself through THIS,” he gestured vaguely at his own visibly strained state, “For gossip?”

He shifted again, more noticeably this time, and froze, jaw clenched, screen-face dimming as he focused entirely on not losing control. The hallway was silent except for the low crackle of Alastor’s radio static.

Charlie winced. “Alastor… He really doesn’t look okay.”

Alastor didn’t answer right away.

He watched Vox carefully— The stiff posture, the lack of bravado, the complete absence of showmanship. Vox wasn’t posturing for an audience. He wasn’t angling a camera. He looked… Genuinely overwhelmed.

And yet…

The nightmares lingered in Alastor’s mind. Vox’s grin. Broadcast screens. Laughter.

Alastor folded his arms. “You’ll forgive me if I’m not convinced,” he said, though his tone was less playful now. “You’re very good at selling a narrative.”

Vox snapped his head up. “I don’t have time to sell you anything!” His voice echoed down the hall, sharp and unfiltered. He immediately froze again, breathing shallow, even raising his voice had shaken the liquid inside of him too much.

“I am at my limit,” Vox said quietly, every word vibrating with strain. “This is not about you. This is not sabotage. This is a biological emergency.” For once, there was no joke. No branding. No smug confidence. Just pure, raw desperation.

Alastor hesitated— Just a beat too long.

The hallway seemed to shrink around them, the restroom still painfully close, Vox barely holding together, and Alastor caught between suspicion and the growing realization that if Vox WAS acting… He was committing to it far more than anyone reasonably should.

Alastor watched Vox for another long second.Not as an adversary this time, but as someone clearly, genuinely unraveling.

The pacing wasn’t theatrical. The tension wasn’t stylized. Vox wasn’t angling his body towards an imaginary camera or shaping the moment into something clever. He was rigid, then restless, then rigid again, like a system overheating and cycling through failsafes that were no longer working.

Slowly, reluctantly, a realization crept in.

‘…Oh.’ Alastor’s smile softened by a fraction. Not kind, but less sharp. He straightened, radio static lowering to a faint hum. “Well,” he said, tone lighter but more cautious now, “You certainly are committed to this little charade.”

Vox shot him a look that could’ve shattered glass. “If this is a charade,” he snapped, then stopped himself abruptly, inhaling through clenched teeth. “Then I deserve an award.”

Charlie stepped closer, voice gentle. “Alastor… I really think he needs to—”

“Yes, yes,” Alastor said quickly, waving a hand. “I’m getting there.” Because he was. The pieces clicked together in a way he didn’t like. Vox had no leverage here. No audience. No advantage. And yet he was still pushing forward, still barely holding himself together. That wasn’t Vox’s style— Unless the motivation was real.

Which meant…

Alastor glanced, just briefly, towards the hallway. Towards the restroom. Towards the door that hid something he very much did not want Vox to see.

His smile tightened again, not with amusement this time, but anxiety.

Because Charlie, in her well-meaning, relentlessly thoughtful way, had put a white-noise machine in there.

A small one. Discreet. Tucked on a shelf near the sink, humming softly whenever the light was on. She’d said it helped make the bathroom feel “less stressful,” and Alastor— After some protesting—Had reluctantly allowed it. Allowed it because it had HELPED. It distracted him. It covered up his own… Noises. It made it easier for him to let go when he desperately needed to.

And Vox… Vox noticed things. Vox cataloged things. Vox would see the machine, hear it, understand it in an instant. And then—

Alastor’s mind supplied the rest with brutal clarity: Vox’s knowing smile. The implication. The broadcast that would follow.

Alastor’s paranoia flared back to life, sharp and insistent. He stepped in front of the hallway again, though with less confidence this time.

Vox let out a strained sound somewhere between a groan and a growl. “Don’t,” he warned quietly. “Do not do this.”

Alastor hesitated. “I believe you,” he said at last, a little stiffly.

Vox stared at him. “That is the least reassuring tone I’ve ever heard.”

“But,” Alastor continued, raising a finger, “That doesn’t mean I’m eager to let you poke around.”

“I am not poking around!” Vox snapped— And immediately regretted the force of it, freezing again, posture locked as he waited for the wave to pass. His voice dropped to something raw. “I just need the bathroom. Two minutes. I won’t look at your… I don’t know, ginormous vibrator, or whatever you’re so afraid of me seeing.”

Alastor’s eyes flicked to the hallway again.

Two minutes.

Two minutes was plenty of time for Vox to notice the faint hum. To connect dots. To file the information away like ammunition.

Alastor’s smile strained, radio static buzzing faintly with his unease. “You see,” he said slowly, “The thing is… I value my privacy.”

Vox let out a shaky, incredulous laugh. “You are currently standing between me and disaster, and THAT’S your concern?”

“Yes,” Alastor said promptly. “It is.”

Charlie looked between them, realization dawning. “Oh. Ohhh. Is this about the—”

Alastor shot her a look.

She clamped her mouth shut immediately.

Vox noticed that too. His eyes narrowed. “What,” he said carefully, “Is in that bathroom?”

“Nothing of interest to you,” Alastor replied quickly.

Vox shifted again, clearly at the absolute end of his patience and control. “Alastor,” he said, voice tight but deadly serious, “I promise you— On my brand, my network, and every cursed screen in Hell— That I just need to use the bathroom.”

Alastor studied him, then he exhaled, long and slow, smile still fixed but no longer playful. “…Very well,” he said at last. He stepped aside— Just enough to let Vox pass— Radio static crackling with nervous energy.

“Touch nothing,” Alastor added quickly. “Look at nothing. And if I so much as suspect you’ve learned something you shouldn’t—”

Vox didn’t wait to hear the rest.

He surged down the hallway, control hanging by a thread, relief and dread tangled together, while Alastor followed close behind, every step filled with the terrifying knowledge that Vox was about to be far closer to one of his most carefully guarded vulnerabilities than he had ever intended.

Vox practically lunged for the door.

Whatever last scraps of composure he had left were abandoned the moment his hand hit the handle. He yanked it open and slipped inside, the door slamming shut behind him with a sharp click that echoed far louder than it should have.

For half a second, he just stood there, breathing hard, every muscle locked, every nerve screaming.

“Oh thank—” he muttered, cutting himself off as another surge hit, sharper than all the rest.

He staggered forwards, movements rushed and uncoordinated, fingers trembling as they went to his fly, fumbling in a way that would’ve been unthinkable under any other circumstances. His screen-face flickered wildly, static rippling as his focus narrowed to a single desperate goal.

“Come on, come on—” he hissed under his breath. “Have to piss NOW, have to—“

The zipper caught.

“FUCK!”

Vox froze, panic flaring white-hot for a split second. He yanked again— Too hard— Then, forced himself to slow just enough to fix it, hands shaking as precious seconds ticked by.

Finally.

The moment he was able to actually relax and open his floodgates, the relief hit him all at once.

Vox let out a long, unsteady breath, shoulders sagging as the tension drained out of him in a wave so intense it almost left him dizzy. His knees threatened to buckle, and he had to brace himself with one hand against the wall, screen-face dimming as the constant internal alarms finally went silent.

“Ohhhh,” he breathed, voice low and shaky. “…Oh, that’s—”

Relief didn’t begin to cover it.

It was overwhelming, full-body, like surviving a near catastrophe by the narrowest margin imaginable. The rigid tightness in his posture melted away, replaced by something looser, almost boneless, as he stood there recovering, breathing evening out inch by inch.

He became dimly aware of a soft, steady sound beneath it all— A gentle hiss, like static tuned to the exact right frequency. White noise. Constant. Comforting.

Normally, Vox would have been curious. Right now, he was far too busy trying not to collapse from sheer relief to care.

Vox closed his eyes and let himself exist in the rare, blissful absence of urgency, completely unaware of how close he was to uncovering a secret that could start an entirely different kind of war.

Outside the door, Alastor stood perfectly still, rigidly upright, hands folded behind his back, smile fixed so firmly it might have cracked porcelain.

The faint hum from inside made his radio static twitch in sympathetic, anxious bursts.

Charlie hovered a few steps away, watching him with careful concern. She waited until the hallway settled into an uneasy quiet before speaking.“Alastor,” she said softly, “you okay?”

Alastor chuckled immediately. Too quickly. “Why, my dear, I’m perfectly fine.”

Charlie did not buy it for a second. She stepped closer, lowering her voice. “I get it,” she said gently. “You don’t like people knowing personal stuff. Especially Vox. Especially THIS.”

Alastor’s smile tightened, but he didn’t interrupt.

Charlie clasped her hands together. “I promise—I won’t let him find anything you don’t want him to know.“

Alastor glanced at her, gaze flicking just slightly towards the bathroom door before snapping back. “…You understand more than I’d like,” he said quietly.

Charlie smiled, warm and reassuring. “I do. And you deserve to feel safe here. This is your home too.”

For a moment Alastor’s static softened, the constant hum dipping into something calmer. He straightened his coat, regaining a bit of his usual theatrical composure. “Hmph,” he said. “Very well. I suppose I’ll take you at your word.”

She beamed. “Good! Because honestly, Vox looks like he went through something traumatic, and I don’t think he has the energy to start a scandal right now.”

Alastor snorted despite himself. “No,” he admitted. “If nothing else, desperation does tend to humble even the most insufferable personalities.”

They fell quiet again.

Inside the bathroom, the white noise continued its steady, unobtrusive hum, doing exactly what it was meant to do— Masking sounds, keeping things private.

A few moments later, the bathroom door opened. Vox stepped out, posture dramatically less rigid, shoulders finally dropping as though he’d shrugged off a hundred pounds of tension. His screen-face flickered with subtle relief, static pulsing gently as he blinked around the hallway, a little more composed than before.

“I have to say,” he muttered, voice calmer now, “Whatever is in there—It’s oddly… Soothing.” He gestured vaguely to the restroom. “The sound. That… Noise thing.”

Charlie perked up, eyes bright. “Oh! That? It’s nothing fancy,” she said with a reassuring smile. “It’s mostly to keep cockroaches away. They really hate that noise for some reason.”

Vox raised an eyebrow, glancing at her for a fraction of a second, then seemed satisfied. “Ah. Cockroaches,” he repeated, as though that explained everything. “Makes sense.” He straightened, tucked his hands behind him, and adjusted his posture.

Without another word, he strode past Charlie, moving with his usual brisk efficiency, though noticeably lighter on his feet.

Charlie let out a quiet breath, smiling as she watched him leave. “Well, that worked out,” she murmured.

Alastor, standing nearby, let the tension in his frame slowly ebb, static lowering to a calm hum. He straightened, fixing his grin back into its usual confident, composed shape. “Yes,” he said softly, almost to himself. “That went… Alright.”

Charlie glanced up at him. “See? Sometimes, all it takes is a little harmless story. Nobody needs to know your secrets, not today.”

Alastor’s gaze flicked once more toward the bathroom door, then back at Charlie, eyes glinting faintly. “Precisely. And may it remain that way.”

The hallway settled into a peaceful quiet. Alastor sighed. Vox had left, and the Radio Demon’s most carefully guarded vulnerability stayed exactly where it belonged; Hidden.

*010*2026-05-11 13:00

Every Overlord had arrived on time, and now they sat in varying degrees of disinterest, hostility, or smug patience.

Vox sat rigidly in his chair.

Not because of Zestial’s obnoxiously close presence, or because Velvette kept tapping her claws against the table in an arrhythmic, headache-inducing beat.

No.

It was because Vox really, really needed to pee.

At first, it had been ignorable. A minor inconvenience. The sort of background signal easily drowned out by the constant hum of electricity that lived beneath his synthetic skin. He’d noticed it right as the meeting began— An irritating alert ping at the back of his awareness— But, he’d dismissed it. Overlords didn’t excuse themselves. Overlords didn’t fidget. Overlords certainly didn’t admit to something as embarrassingly mundane as a biological necessity.

Unfortunately, time in Hell had a way of stretching itself out when most didn’t want it to.

Alastor was talking. He’d been talking for a while.

Vox’s screen flickered faintly as he adjusted his posture, crossing one leg over the other with calculated casualness. The movement sent a sharp reminder through him, like a warning spike in his internal systems. His jaw tightened, digital smile never wavering, but his hand curled slightly against the armrest.

‘Fantastic,’ he thought bitterly. ‘Of all the meetings. Of all the days.’

Alastor had JUST seen him desperate less than a week ago. And he’d been so fucking weird and obnoxious about it that Vox had no desire to repeat the experience. It would be one thing if it had been Alastor’s usual brand of irritation, taunting, teasing… Maybe fucking around with his auditory powers to blast running water noises at a decibel level high enough to explode both ear drums and bladders… Assuming that was an ability Alastor possessed.

But, no. The torment hadn’t been silly tricks and insults. It had been… Stubborn, strangely panicked refusal.

Vox only had two theories. The first was the one he’d already proposed to Alastor; That the Radio Demon stored a weird sex toy in that bathroom and would be humiliated if Vox found it. And, the second was that Alastor was concerned Vox would make a mess of the bathroom just to spite him and give him something to clean.

Which… Okay, that wouldn’t have been completely OFF the table, if Vox hadn’t been having an emergency…

Other than that? Vox had NO clue what Alastor’s fucking problem was.

Whatever it was, Vox didn’t think he wanted to see any MORE of it. At least, not when his bladder was burning with need.

He glanced, just briefly, towards the door and tried not to groan. This wasn’t a “step out for a moment” kind of gathering. This was a sit, listen, and assert dominance kind of gathering.

Vox subtly shifted again, adjusting the angle of his hips under the table. He hoped that no one was paying close enough attention to notice, especially not Alastor. His screen flashed to a different expression for half a second, a glitch of irritation quickly smoothed back into his usual confident grin.

Valentino leaned closer, elbow bumping Vox’s side. “You look tense, babe,” he muttered, smoke curling from his lips. “Something crawled up your—”

Vox shot him a sharp look, static crackling audibly. Valentino laughed and leaned back, apparently satisfied.

The pressure built, slow but relentless. Vox tried to distract himself by pulling up internal displays—News feeds, viewer metrics, anything— But, even his favorite numbers failed to fully drown out the sensation. His knee bounced once before he forcibly stilled it, fingers tightening again on the armrest.

Across the table, Velvette smirked. “You okay?” she asked, eyes sharp. “You’re glitching.”

“I’m fine,” Vox replied instantly, voice smooth and amplified just enough to command attention. His screen brightened, broadcasting confidence in high definition. “Just bored.”

A few of the Overlords chuckled. Alastor’s smile widened, eyes gleaming with something that might have been amusement.

The meeting dragged on.

Plans. Territory disputes. Veiled threats wrapped in polite language. Vox nodded when appropriate, contributed when necessary, all while carefully, desperately maintaining control over both his image and his body. The need had become impossible to ignore now, a constant, pressing presence that demanded resolution.

‘End the meeting,’ Vox willed silently. ‘Wrap it up. Blow something up. I don’t care.’

But Hell was nothing if not cruel.

Alastor did not stop talking.

If anything, he seemed to settle in.

He leaned back in his chair, cane resting against his shoulder, voice lilting and theatrical as he elaborated on his point for the third time—Embroidering it with anecdotes that absolutely no one had asked for. His smile never wavered, but Vox noticed something odd: The way Alastor’s fingers tightened around the handle of his cane, knuckles whitening beneath the red gloves. Vox remembered how Alastor always squeezed the fuck out of that thing during walks home from the bar, but only if he needed to—

Vox’s screen dimmed for half a second.

‘Well, now,’ he thought. ‘You too?’

This might get a little awkward… Vox predicted that both he and Alastor would be hurrying out of here, straight to the closest bathroom, meaning they’d have to piss side by side because whoever designed the bathroom down the hall had decided TWO urinals would be sufficient.

Urinating right next to his nemesis was going to feel strange. Of course, back when he and Alastor were FRIENDS, it was a different story. They’d—

Huh.

Vox searched his memory banks for every moment he could recall with Alastor and realized, for the first time, that a certain mundane, everyday activity was missing.

He and Alastor had been friends for YEARS. DECADES. One would think that, in all that time, Alastor would have relieved himself in his vicinity at least once.

But, no. The main memories he had involving Alastor and urination were those drunk, stumbling walks home in which Alastor occasionally complained about NEEDING to go, but never did it.

Any time Vox went to the restroom, Alastor had waited outside for him… His smile occasionally twitching, likely annoyed at Vox for needing a break…

The only time Alastor had decided to use the restroom with him, he hadn’t actually gone. Vox had finished, then waited outside the stall, listening to Alastor huff and fidget, feeling a touch of concern well up inside him. Finally, Alastor had stumbled out of the stall, informing Vox that his zipper was completely jammed and that he needed to hurry home right away so that he could free himself with a pair of tweezers.

How miserable… Poor Alastor had looked like he was going to burst, it was the worst possible time for a wardrobe malfunction.

Other than that one time, Alastor had never, ever accompanied him. Alastor never ducked into an alleyway when they were out together. Alastor had never watered a bush during those rare times they spent a significant number of hours away from the city.

Alastor had only ever actually excused himself to the restroom a few times while in Vox’s presence; Once during an incident in which he’d tickled Alastor too hard and the Radio Demon had needed to rush off, once when Vox had accidentally gotten them both tangled up in his wires, and again when the walk home had been too long and Alastor had wanted to stop at his friend Rosie’s house.

Aside from that, there was just that one incident where Alastor got so sick… Vox had gotten scared that time, admittedly. He’d never seen Alastor like that before. He’d been running such a high fever, hallucinating, literally crying for his mom… And he couldn’t keep anything down.

Vox ultimately decided he’d stay by Alastor’s side until he was sure he was able to retain fluid in his body— Meaning, until Alastor needed to use the restroom. But, even THEN, even in his weakened, barely-there state, when that time came, he insisted he could walk to his bathroom by himself and needed no assistance.

Vox had assumed Alastor just felt ashamed of himself for being so bogged down by a flu, but now… It was sort of strange, wasn’t it?

Vox had never, he realized dimly, seen Alastor relieve himself.

It shouldn’t have meant much. Vox hadn’t even NOTICED until this moment, after all. And, Alastor clearly had a strong bladder, so infrequent bathroom breaks were to be expected. But, it was… Weird. Decades of close companionship and Alastor had never performed this one vital, common action where Vox would notice. x

It was just another sign that, contrary to what Vox may have believed— To what Vox WANTED to believe— Alastor HADN’T let his walls down for him. Not all the way. Alastor had still kept his guard up, even when they were so close.

Or, had they ever been truly close? Was ALL of it just in Vox’s head?

After all, Valentino was completely comfortable around Vox, and he’d pissed in front of him so many times that Vox was a tad disturbed by it…

Vox shifted again, this time sliding his foot back beneath his chair, posture growing increasingly rigid. His internal systems were now less a background hum and more a persistent alert he couldn’t mute. He kept his grin sharp and broadcast-ready, but his processors were dedicating an embarrassing amount of power to not squirming.

Across the table, Alastor gestured broadly as he spoke, then— Just briefly— Paused. The movement stilled, his cane tapping once against the floor before he resumed as if nothing had happened.

Vox caught it.

Alastor’s eyes flicked towards the door for a fraction of a second.

Then he laughed— Too loud, too sudden— And launched right back into his monologue.

Vox let out a slow, controlled breath through clenched teeth.

‘Unbelievable.’

Alastor, the Radio Demon himself, was clearly uncomfortable. And, rather than wrapping things up like a rational tyrant, he was doing the opposite. Talking more. Drawing it out. Asserting dominance through sheer refusal to acknowledge a problem.

Which meant Vox was trapped along with him.

Valentino had noticed Vox’s tension again. “You’re really not selling the ‘bored’ thing,” he muttered. “You look like you’re buffering.”

Vox elbowed him sharply under the table, earning an annoyed hiss. The movement was a mistake. He froze afterward, hands flattening against his thighs, jaw locking as he recalibrated his stance.

Velvette was absolutely watching now. Her grin widened, eyes flicking between Vox and Alastor with growing interest. “Wow,” she drawled. “Is this a new kind of power struggle? ‘Who can sit still the longest?’ Riveting.”

Alastor’s smile twitched.

Just barely.

He continued speaking, voice smooth and melodic, but Vox could hear the strain now. The slightly quicker cadence, the way Alastor’s words were stacking on top of each other as if he were afraid of silence. Afraid that if he stopped, even for a moment, something would be acknowledged.

The Radio Demon did not excuse himself. Did not show weakness. And certainly did not admit to something as undignified as needing a restroom.

Vox, meanwhile, was running out of options.

He adjusted his seating again, slower this time, deliberate enough to look intentional. His knee bounced once, twice— He forced it still, but the effort made his screen flicker with static at the edges.

Alastor finally paused.

Silence dropped over the table like a guillotine.

Every Overlord looked to him.

Alastor stood.

The movement was sharp, sudden enough that his chair scraped loudly against the floor. He planted his cane with exaggerated confidence, grin stretched wide, eyes blazing.

“Well!” he said brightly. “I believe that covers my thoughts on the matter. Unless anyone has pressing concerns?”

Vox seized the opportunity. “Meeting adjourned,” he snapped, standing almost immediately— Too quickly, if anyone were paying close attention. “We’ve covered enough ground for one day.”

For a tense moment, no one argued.

Then Valentino shrugged. Carmilla scoffed but rose anyway. One by one, the Overlords stood, filing toward the doors with varying degrees of annoyance.

The second the doors opened, Vox did not run… But, he came very close.

Alastor followed at a measured pace, posture impeccable, smile unwavering… Until the doors closed behind them.

Then, just for an instant, both demons halted in the corridor.

Their eyes met.

No words were exchanged.

Vox straightened his jacket, screen flickering as he turned sharply down the hall. Alastor cleared his throat, adjusted his grip on his cane, and walked with rigid dignity.

Neither looked back.

Neither acknowledged anything.

But Hell had rarely seen two Overlords move so urgently, and so carefully, at the same time.

Vox did not bother pretending anymore.

The moment he turned the corner, his stride lengthened into something just shy of a run—Heels clicking sharply against the polished floor as his internal alerts flared in frantic, overlapping warnings. His screen glitched with static at the edges, grin tightening into something strained and brittle.

‘Almost there. Almost there.’

The restroom entrance came into view like a mirage: Tall doors marked with glowing sigils, mercifully unguarded, mercifully close. Vox let out a breath he hadn’t realized he was holding and veered toward it, one hand already lifting—

—And then he stopped.

Because he noticed Alastor walking right past it…

The Radio Demon didn’t even glance at the doors. He strode by with that same measured pace, cane tapping rhythmically against the floor, posture perfect, smile wide and untroubled.

Vox stared.

His processors lagged for half a second as the absurdity of it hit him.

“—Hey,” Vox blurted, turning sharply. “What are you doing?”

Alastor froze. Just for a fraction of a second, so brief it would have been invisible to anyone else, but Vox saw it. The cane hesitated mid-tap. Alastor’s shoulders stiffened before he resumed walking, laughter bubbling up immediately afterward. “Why, whatever do you mean, my dear Vox?” Alastor said cheerfully, not turning around.

Vox gestured sharply at the restroom doors, then immediately regretted the movement as another urgent wave hit him. He clenched his hands into fists at his sides. “The bathroom,” he said, voice tight. “You— You looked like you needed to go.”

Alastor finally turned. His smile was immaculate. Broadcast-ready. Completely, utterly composed. “I assure you,” he said lightly, “I have no such need.”

Vox’s screen flickered with disbelief. “Oh, bullshit.”

“Not at all!”

The Radio Demon twirled his cane once, the motion crisp, but Vox noticed the way his grip never loosened. Not even a little.

“You squirmed for forty-five straight minutes while you were talking,” Vox said. “You cut the meeting short like a bomb was about to go off.”

“You’re seeing things!”

“You kept looking at the door.”

Alastor laughed, a bright, ringing sound that echoed down the hall a bit too loudly. “My dear fellow, you couldn’t possibly believe I was thinking about anything so… Mundane.”

Inside, panic clawed its way up Alastor’s spine. ‘Think. Think. Do not let him linger. Do not let him ask questions. Do not—‘

He could feel it now, the same pressure Vox was fighting, except Alastor refused— Utterly refused— To even let the thought finish forming. The idea of stepping into that restroom, of standing there WITH Vox, trying to go, hearing Vox’s stream pour easily from him while his own stayed locked away, waiting for the inevitable moment when Vox realized he was malfunctioning, and decided to—

No. No, no, no.

Better to walk straight into a raging fire.

Vox narrowed his eyes, studying him. “So you’re just… Leaving?”

“Indeed!” Alastor replied, already pivoting his body away from the restroom again. “A stroll does wonders for the mind.”

“It looks like you need something else,” Vox snapped.

Another beat of silence stretched between them.

Vox shifted his weight, clearly reaching the edge of his patience— And his restraint. His screen flashed briefly, static crawling across the corners.“Look, if this is some kind of pride thing, I really don’t have time—”

Alastor’s smile sharpened. “I do not need to go,” he said, voice still pleasant but edged with something final. “And I would appreciate it if you did not project your… Current discomforts onto me.”

The words landed cleanly.

Vox flinched, then scowled. “I am not projecting.”

“Mm-hmm.”

Alastor tipped his head, eyes gleaming. “Now, if you’ll excuse me, Vox—” He took a step away.

Vox hesitated.

Every instinct screamed at him to forget this conversation and bolt through the doors before it was too late. He was this close to losing the battle entirely. But the sheer strangeness of Alastor walking away, of denying something Vox knew was happening—

“Alastor,” Vox said again, lower this time.

The Radio Demon stopped, shoulders tightening despite himself. “Yes?”

Vox looked between him and the restroom, jaw clenched, frustration and urgency warring on his screen. “You’re telling me you don’t need to use it. At all.”

Alastor did not turn around. “I am telling you,” he said carefully, “That I am perfectly fine.”

The lie held.

Barely.

“Now, if you’re through with this… Odd fascination, I must be on my way,” Alastor said. His grin never wavered, but inside, chaos raged. Every step he took away from the restroom only made it worse. His carefully cultivated façade of composure was beginning to crack, little static sparks of panic buzzing along his nerves. He could feel it— Every fiber of his being screaming for relief— But the thought of trying to use that restroom, especially with VOX, made his heart pound.

‘The hotel. I need to get back to the hotel,’ he thought frantically, vision flickering ever so slightly as he imagined the privacy of his own space. ‘I can go as soon as I’m there!’

Vox, on the other hand, was teetering on the edge of disaster. His steps were faster as he trailed after Alastor, a harsh click-click against the floor. Every second brought the kind of internal warnings that made his processors whine. His bladder screamed for release, and yet… Curiosity gnawed at him.

Alastor was acting so fucking weird. Vox couldn’t believe it. He had seen the signs. The subtle tension, the stiff movements, the twitch in the cane grip. Why wouldn’t he go? Vox’s screen flickered with static edges, almost like a nervous tic. “You’re hiding something,” he said flatly, striding to fall into pace beside the Radio Demon. “Come on, Alastor, what’s going on?”

Alastor’s smile widened, tighter than before. “Oh, my dear Vox, nothing at all! I am entirely fine. Absolutely. Perfectly.”

Vox narrowed his eyes, posture rigid. “Entirely fine?” His tone carried the weight of someone on the verge of catastrophe themselves. “You walked past the bathroom.”

Alastor’s heart hammered violently, the panic threatening to override his usual cheerfulness. ‘Don’t admit anything. Not ever.’ He forced his grin wider, voice light, controlled. “Indeed. I simply… Did not feel the need at that moment. Do I need to prove that to you?”

Before Vox could respond, Alastor conjured an enormous pitcher of water, and downed it in several long, dramatic gulps. His cheeks flushed slightly— Not from thirst, oh no, but from sheer dread. The walk back to the hotel was now guaranteed to be a lot more painful. He vanished the pitcher with a snap of his fingers, forcing a laugh.

“See?” he said brightly, voice a little higher than usual. “Would I do such a thing if I truly needed to… Expel anything? No! Absolutely not!”

Vox froze mid-step, blinking. “You’re… Drinking more water?” His tone was incredulous. “Right now?”

“Indeed!” Alastor replied, smiling wider, though the strain in his posture betrayed him. “Hydration is vital, Vox! You should try it sometime. Keeps the mind sharp, the smile bright, the— Well, all sorts of things!”

Vox’s legs twitched. His internal alarms were screaming, but his curiosity— Dangerous, unrelenting curiosity— Pushed him on. “So, just to be clear,” he said, stepping closer, voice low and serious, “You don’t need to piss. At all. Not even a little?”

Alastor’s grin stiffened, spine straightening, cane tapping once sharply on the floor. “Absolutely… Not. I assure you. No need whatsoever. Why, I couldn’t get a drop out if I tried!”

Well. At least THAT part was true…

‘Every lie should have a kernel of truth to it!’ he thought, panic making his mind race. Every step away from the restroom was agony. The water pooling in his stomach was a test of nerves. ‘Just make it to the hotel. Just make it to the hotel.’

Vox, meanwhile, clenched his fists, swaying slightly as his own bladder screamed. He was so close to collapse he could hardly think straight— But, something about Alastor’s insistence, the way he was trying so hard to convince him he wasn’t desperate, kept Vox’s focus hooked.

“You know,” Vox said, voice almost a growl, “If you really weren’t about to explode, you wouldn’t be trying this hard.”

Alastor froze mid-step. A static buzz shot across his body, ‘Oh no. No no no no no,’ he thought frantically.

He couldn’t let Vox see. Not now. Not ever. He swallowed hard, clenched his fists, and forced his face into a dazzling grin. “Trying so hard, you say? Why, Vox, you flatter me! I simply… Enjoy the theater of walking and talking at the same time. That’s all. Nothing more.”

But inside, every fiber of his being was screaming: ‘Hotel. Bathroom. Now.’

Vox tilted his head, suspicious. “Uh-huh,” he said slowly. “Right. Sure. You’re fine. Totally fine.”

Both demons walked on, one trying to survive with dignity, the other teetering on the brink of explosion, their own curiosities and instincts set on a collision course…

Vox’s steps were shorter now, almost stiff, each one accompanied by a faint flicker of static along his screen edges. His hands were clenched at his sides, jaw tight, and every nerve screamed at him that he was seconds away from disaster. And yet… He couldn’t stop. That dangerous little spark kept him glued to Alastor’s side, even as his own body threatened mutiny.

Alastor noticed immediately. He had felt it in the slight stagger in Vox’s stride, the subtle twitch of his fingers. Vox was on the edge.

Alastor slowed his pace just enough to look back, cane tapping lightly against the floor. His smile, polished and bright as ever, didn’t hide the tension in his posture. “Vox,” he said in that velvety, exaggeratedly cheerful tone, “Why ever do you persist in trailing me? It is rather obvious you are in urgent need of… Relief.”

Vox froze mid-step. He blinked at the Radio Demon, eyes wide, static crackling sharply across the edges of his screen. “I will be fine!” he protested, though his voice was cracking slightly, betraying his desperation. “I can still wait!”

Alastor raised a perfectly arched eyebrow. “Oh? You seem positively frantic,” he said, a note of amusement— Or was it mockery?— In his voice. “The angle of your stance, the slight wobble in your stride… You are telling me, then, that you can still endure?”

Vox swallowed hard, pressing a hand against his crotch instinctively. “It… It’s bad,” he admitted in a strangled voice. “I… just…” He trailed off, shoving his other hand against his thigh as another urgent wave hit him. His static flickered violently, almost like sparks flying off an overworked circuit.

Alastor’s smile widened, though his fingers tightened around his cane, knuckles whitening.“Well,” he said carefully, voice light but clipped, “if that is the case… I must insist, my dear Vox, that you head back to the restroom immediately. It would be… Entirely improper for you to soil yourself in my presence.”

Vox froze again. “Ah?” His voice cracked. He looked down at himself, then back at Alastor, panic and incredulity battling in his expression. “I would never!”

Alastor’s grin never faltered, though his own internal panic had reached new heights. Each step was agony, every blink a reminder that his bladder was screaming at him, begging for release. ‘Just make it back to the hotel. Just survive,’ he told himself, heart hammering.

“Go back then,” Alastor said smoothly, voice calm but with a sharp edge. “If you do not, I fear that a… Catastrophe may occur. And I should not like to witness such an event myself.”

Vox’s screen flickered violently now, almost like a warning siren. “Catastrophe?!” he gasped, shifting his weight frantically, the urgency clawing at him so sharply he almost staggered. “I—You—Ugh! I’m about to explode! I—”

Alastor’s grin widened a fraction too fast, a glitter of panic flashing behind the eyes. “Precisely why you should proceed to the restroom, my dear Vox,” he said. “It would be… Prudent.“

Vox blinked, torn between urgency and obsession. His curiosity about Alastor was eating him alive, even as his body screamed for release.

Alastor adjusted his grip on the cane, swallowing hard. Every passing second was agony. ‘Please, just… The hotel… Then safety… Then— Oh, heavens…’

Vox took a halting step forward, nearly tripping, then steadied himself, the edge of panic and curiosity merging into one combustible mixture. “Why… Why aren’t you going?” he demanded, voice tight. “If you’re not—If you’re really fine—Why does it look like you’re suffering too?”

Alastor froze mid-stride, the cane tapping halting abruptly. Inside, the Radio Demon was screaming. ‘Do not admit it! Do not admit it!’ His grin stretched, sharper than ever, hands gripping the cane tightly. “Because,” he said smoothly, “I… Do not need to go. Perfectly fine. Nothing whatsoever.”

Vox squinted. “Really?” His voice was incredulous. “Even with all that water?”

Alastor’s fingers twitched. He could already feel that some of the water had reached his bladder. “Even so,” he said evenly, voice silky, grin impeccable. “I am entirely fine.”

Inside, every second felt like a lifetime. The streets stretched endlessly. Vox was teetering, curiosity sharpening, bladder screaming. And Alastor… Alastor had never wanted the hotel more in his entire existence.

Alastor’s smile remained perfect. His cane tapped rhythmically against the cracked, glowing sidewalks as they moved, but inside, every step was agony.

“Oh!” Alastor said suddenly, voice light. “It must be these infernal streets! The uneven cobblestones… My legs are simply cramping! Yes, yes, nothing more.”

Vox blinked, eyes narrowing suspiciously. Static flickered wildly around the edges of his screen. “Cramping?” he repeated, voice sharp. “Right. And that’s why you’re walking so… Carefully, and why your posture keeps shifting, and why—”

Alastor twirled his cane with a flourish, intercepting Vox’s gaze with an immaculate grin. “Oh, my dear Vox, you flatter me with your keen observation!” With Vox’s eyes— So sharp, so obsessive— He felt like he was walking on the edge of a razor.

Vox’s step faltered slightly, one hand pressing against his side. He was dangerously close to the point of no return. “Huh,” he muttered, voice tight. “Cramping. Yeah… Sure. That explains it. Totally.”

Alastor flickered with faint static, an unintentional shiver betraying his panic. He had to keep moving, had to keep up appearances. But, the farther they walked, the more impossible it became to maintain the act.

‘Just make it to the hotel,’ he thought, heart hammering. ‘Just survive, and no one finds out. Vox must not follow all the way.’

The streets of Hell stretched interminably, jagged and alive with firelight, the air thick with smoke and the faint odor of brimstone. Each step echoed around them, counting down the seconds of torment for both demons.

Vox’s pace slowed as his desperation reached critical levels. He was wobbling now, one hand clutching at his crotch, static sparking nervously across his screen. But the pull of curiosity— The need to see what the reason behind Alastor’s behavior could possibly be— Was stronger than the urge to retreat.

“You know,” Vox said through gritted teeth, voice tight with tension, “If your legs are cramping THIS badly, you should probably… I don’t know, sit down? Or rest somewhere?”

‘I don’t have time to rest! I need to get to the hotel before I combust!’

Alastor’s grin stretched too wide. “Rest? Oh, my dear fellow, I assure you, a true Overlord does not succumb to something as trivial as leg cramps! One simply… Marches forward, regardless of discomfort. See?” He tapped the cane against the street again, a little harder than usual.

‘Do not stop walking. Do not stop moving. And for the love of everything, do not let him follow you home.’

Vox blinked at him. “Right… Marching forward,” he muttered. “And yet you’re shifting constantly, and your… Posture… And—”

Alastor tilted his head, bright grin in place, but fingers tightening around the cane. “Merely leg cramps, Vox! Nothing more. Absolutely ordinary! Perhaps… You’re imagining things?”

Vox stopped dead in the middle of the street, one hand pressed to his crotch, eyes narrowing sharply. “Imagining things?” he said, voice strained, static buzzing dangerously across the edges of his screen. “Alastor, I know you’re not just cramping. You’re hiding something.”

Alastor forced an elegant chuckle. “Hiding? Moi? Oh, no, Vox! Merely enjoying the thrill of our little… Promenade. Yes, yes! Observe the bustling streets of Hell as we march onward!”

Inside, panic boiled like lava. ‘Keep moving. Do not let him corner you. Hotel first… Then everything else.’

Vox, however, was dangerously close to losing focus entirely. Each step made his urgency more extreme. He was teetering on collapse, yet he couldn’t stop following. His eyes never left Alastor, desperate to understand how the Radio Demon could possibly seem so composed while walking like this.

Alastor’s smile remained flawless, but the internal screaming intensified. ‘Please, please, do not follow me all the way home. Please!’

Curiosity clawed at Vox as sharply as his own bladder. His steps had slowed to a stagger, each one a battle between sheer urgency and obsession. His hands clenched against his sides, static crackling violently across the edges of his screen like a warning alarm, but he couldn’t stop. He couldn’t— Not when Alastor’s composure, that flawless, maddening grin, seemed so impossible to reconcile with the signs Vox had already noticed.

Every step pressed painfully against his limits. Every breath was a shallow, jagged gasp. His processors were screaming at him to prioritize himself, to bolt for a restroom before disaster struck. But curiosity— Alastor—Had him trapped in a strange, irresistible gravitational pull.

Alastor, meanwhile, was bristling. Vox trailing him, so obviously on the verge, was… Unnerving. And yet, terrifyingly, it also made him hyper-aware of his own crisis. The closer they got to the hotel, the more his bladder screamed, the more each step tightened the coil of pressure in his abdomen.

‘Just a few more blocks,’ he told himself desperately. ‘Then… The hotel. Privacy. Dignity. Relief.’

Vox was swaying slightly, one hand clutching his abdomen, the other pressing against the edge of his coat to steady himself. His curiosity and obsession screamed at him: he HAD to know. He had to see what Alastor’s deal was!

Vox’s steps faltered. He grabbed the nearest lamppost for support, swaying dangerously.

Alastor’s eyes flickered, briefly, at the hand clinging to the lamppost, the strained posture, the sparks of static surrounding Vox like a storm warning. ‘Oh, dear gods,’ he thought. He’s willing to push himself to the brink. He WILL follow me all the way home if I don’t— If I don’t do something.’

If that happened, if he couldn’t slip into the hotel and sprint to his room in privacy… The humiliation, the agony— He shivered at the thought.

Vox’s steps wobbled again, dangerously close to collapse, but his fixation on Alastor, his need to see, to understand, overrode every internal warning.

Alastor’s cane tapped against the street with measured rhythm, but every tap felt like torture, the pressure building unbearably. Each step was a silent countdown to private sanctuary— The hotel where he could finally relieve himself, alone, without anyone knowing. And yet Vox’s insistence on following, on watching, made every second more torturous than the last.

‘Just a few more blocks… Just a few more blocks,’ Alastor repeated internally, panic coiling tight, gnawing at his carefully maintained poise. His fingers twitched around the cane. ‘Do not stop. Do not trip. Do not let him notice. Do not— Oh, goodness me, I cannot hold it much longer!’

Vox had reached critical mass. Every breath was agony, every movement a desperate battle against the screaming alarms of his own body. Static flickered violently across the edges of his screen as he staggered alongside Alastor, one hand clutching his crotch, the other pressed to the edge of his coat in a vain attempt at control.

And then they passed it.

A nondescript building with a flickering neon sign: PUBLIC RESTROOM – OPEN 24 HOURS.

Vox’s eyes widened. Static crackled sharply. He froze mid-step for a fraction of a second, then staggered forwards, barely able to think through the pain and pressure building inside him. “Oh—Oh no, oh no, oh no…” he muttered, voice ragged and trembling. “I can’t… I can’t hold it… I—”

Alastor, glancing back, caught the sudden halt and the wild, desperate look in Vox’s eyes. His cane tapped once, nervously. “Vox?” he said carefully, voice sugar-coated but tinged with alarm. “Do you think it might be wise of you to—“

But Vox was already gone.

He lunged towards the building like a starved creature, stumbling over the cracked sidewalk in his desperation. One hand slammed the door open, the other clutching the handle as he practically threw himself inside. His screen flickered with static like sparks flying off an overworked circuit, every motion punctuated by the agonizing urgency that he could no longer deny.

Alastor froze on the sidewalk, cane tapping lightly against the stone, lips pressed into a tight, smile. “Well,” he murmured under his breath, a flicker of genuine panic in his eyes. “I suppose he won’t be following me home now.”

His own bladder screamed in envy, wishing he could take care of his own ever-tightening crisis.

Alastor tapped his cane again, a slow, careful rhythm, forcing himself to stride forward. “Just a few more blocks,” he murmured to himself, teeth clenched. “Just the hotel… Then safety… Privacy… then— Oh, dear gods, the relief…”

Alastor forced a shaky, gleaming grin as he continued walking, heart hammering, fingers tightening on his cane.

‘Not long now…’ he thought, willing every agonizing step to be the last before the sanctuary of his hotel.

For the first time in what felt like hours, the streets felt… Quieter. Just enough for him to steel himself for the final stretch.

Inside the small, flickering restroom, Vox had at last torn down his zipper, legs trembling. His screen edges were still crackling violently, static popping with every heartbeat as the agonizing pressure finally… Finally released. Relief surged through him like liquid fire washing away hours of tension.

“Ohhh… Yes,” he muttered, voice trembling with exhaustion and bliss. His knees bent slightly. “Fucking FINALLY!”

The world outside the restroom ceased to exist. Every step, every wobble, every agonizing second of chasing Alastor through the streets evaporated in the warm glow of sweet, sweet relief. Vox sagged further against the wall, nearly floating on the sensation. His body felt lighter, freer, and yet… His uncertainty about Alastor, remained. Even now, between shaky breaths, his thoughts were circling the Radio Demon and his baffling behavior.

Once he finished, Vox continued to tremble from relief, eyes half-lidded, body still humming with the aftershock of desperation finally satisfied. He leaned back against the wall, static fading slowly, still replaying Alastor’s every twitch, every step, every strained smile.

Meanwhile, Alastor was NOT having it so easy.

The streets of Hell stretched ahead, cruelly long and jagged, feeling a brutal, throbbing reminder of the water he’d guzzled earlier. His bladder tightened like a vice around him, every movement sending sharp scolds that his attempt to prove himself to Vox had backfired spectacularly. Cane tapping against the cracked stones, posture still impeccable— Or at least as impeccable as it could be under the circumstances— He pushed forward towards the hotel.

‘Just… A few more blocks…’ he repeated internally, teeth clenched behind his perfect grin. ‘You’ll relieve yourself soon, Alastor. Very, very soon.’ If Vox had followed me all the way… I would have— Oh, heavens, I cannot even imagine!’

Alastor’s steps were measured, careful, each tap of the cane both a countdown and an indication of how painfully urgent it had become. The hotel loomed in the distance like a promised land, each window glowing faintly, each sign a beacon of hope. But the closer he got, the more the pressure in his bladder threatened to undo every shred of his composure.

‘Hold it, Alastor!’

He adjusted his grip on the cane again, forcing a cheerful tilt to his head as though everything were perfectly ordinary. ‘Keep smiling. Keep walking. Nobody must see.’

A small, unintentional shiver ran through him as another sharp pang hit. ‘Please… Just a few more steps… Just the lobby… The stairs… Your room…’

Alastor’s pace quickened, eyes darting to the hotel doors. His polished grin was perfect, forced, practiced. But, his internal monologue screamed: ‘Not long now… Just a few more steps… Do not trip… Do not falter… Do not let anyone see…’

The final stretch awaited. The lobby doors gleamed under the light. The sanctuary of his private room— And the relief it promised— Was just beyond them.

Alastor burst through the doors of the hotel like a man possessed, cane tapping against the polished floor with frantic urgency. The few people in the lobby looked up in surprise as the Radio Demon practically flew past them. He didn’t pause to greet anyone. He didn’t flash his usual wide, cheerful grin. There was only one thing on his mind: RELIEF. NOW.

“Excuse me, pardon me!” he called over his shoulder in a strained, forced cheerfulness, though the words sounded hollow even to him. “Important… Errre… Overlord tasks… Very important!”

He charged up the stairs, his normally precise stride reduced to a desperate, almost clumsy sprint. Each tap of the cane was like a pulse of agony, every step a reminder that his bladder was seconds from bursting wide open.

‘Just the room… Just the room…’ he thought, eyes wide behind that polished grin. ‘Do not trip… Do not faint… Do not—Oh, heavens, I cannot last another second!’

Finally, he reached the door to his suite, practically throwing it open with one hand while the other gripped the cane for balance. He stumbled inside, shutting the door behind him with a force that rattled the frame.

He made it. He had made it.

Alastor dashed to his bathroom like he was escaping infernal chains, kicking the door open and nearly collapsing over the threshold. Fingers trembling, he fumbled at the zipper on his pants, heart hammering, legs shaking, every nerve screaming in protest.

‘Yes… Yes, finally…’ he thought, teeth clenched, voice whispering under his breath. ‘Relief… Privacy… Salvation…’

And then… The moment arrived. He managed to unzip himself JUST in time, and the sensation of release was instantaneous, overwhelming, and almost absurdly satisfying. His body sagged, cane clattering to the floor as he closed his eyes, letting out a shaky, relieved sigh.

“Ohhhhh… Sweet mercy…” he murmured, voice trembling. The ordeal— The streets, the water, Vox, the panic— It was all over. Finally, at last, he could breathe without desperation clawing at him.

Alastor blinked slowly as he let himself savor the sheer, unadulterated bliss. The grin broadened—Slightly crooked, a mixture of pride, exhaustion, and lingering panic— But, it was his grin, unforced.

Behind the closed bathroom door, the Radio Demon could finally allow himself a moment of vulnerability: A few shaky breaths, a muttered curse about his own foolish choice to guzzle that water, and the quiet, private acknowledgment that chasing composure while on the verge of absolute disaster was… Far more exhausting than anyone would ever know.

Alastor shuddered as the last drops seeped out. He straightened, brushing his hands over his suit, picking up his cane. His grin— Fully, perfectly back— Was flawless once more. “Yes… Yes, everything is entirely under control,” he whispered to himself. “Nothing to see here… Merely the Radio Demon… Performing a little maintenance!”

Minutes later, Alastor sat in his room, hands clasped tightly over his knees, cane resting against the arm of his chair, and his grin… Well, it was there, but it didn’t quite reach his eyes. Every tick of the clock, every faint creak of the hotel settling, sent a shiver through him. His private ordeal was over, yes— But, the stress? The dread that Vox might somehow figure out what was wrong with him?

That was very much alive.

‘He can’t know. Ever,’ Alastor told himself. ‘If he even suspects… Oh, the horror, the humiliation, the eternal teasing— No, no, no!’

He downed a glass of water, forcing a calm sip, but each gulp only reminded him of the precarious balance of his bladder. ‘I must appear normal. Utterly normal. Controlled, cheerful, unshakable… As always.’

“I cannot allow him to discover it. No. Absolutely not. He cannot, will not, must not know!”

The paranoia gnawed at him relentlessly. He straightened his jacket, brushed invisible dust from his shoulders, and smiled— A perfect mask of composure. “Yes… perfectly normal. Utterly unshakable. Not a hint of— Oh, heavens…”

Meanwhile, somewhere across Hell, Vox had finished his own recovery. He leaned back against a wall outside the public restroom, screen edges slowly stabilizing as static faded to a soft, lingering buzz. Relief lingered, but the curiosity remained sharp and relentless.

‘Alastor,’ Vox thought, eyes narrowing. ‘He’s hiding something. I can feel it.‘ His mind replayed every twitch, every carefully practiced smile. ‘He’s hiding something. Something… Personal. Something he doesn’t want me to see.’

Even in the bliss of release, Vox’s attention was fixated. He could feel the pull of it, the magnetic frustration of wanting to KNOW. His mind was buzzing with scenarios, deductions, calculations.

Vox wandered back into the streets, thoughts buzzing through him like electricity. His obsession burned hotter now.

‘He’s hiding something,’ Vox repeated to himself. ‘And I WILL find out what it is. Somehow.’

This was far from over.
