The walk back to the hotel was, naturally, a lot quieter than usual.

Hell seemed to stretch longer without Alastor’s constant commentary, without the radio hum that normally bent reality around him. Even his footsteps sounded wrong, too soft for someone who commanded fear with a laugh.

She glanced at him. “Okay,” she said, trying to sound confident. “So, Dad didn’t do it. Which means…”

Alastor stopped.

Slowly, he turned his head— Not towards her, but toward the lamppost across the street.

Charlie followed his gaze.

At first, she saw nothing unusual. Just another crooked streetlight, buzzing faintly. Then the lens caught the glow just right.

A small, black camera blinked red.

“…Oh,” Charlie breathed. “That’s not good.”

Alastor’s smile widened, sharp and knowing. He lifted his cane and tapped it once against the pavement.

The camera rotated smoothly, whirring as it focused on them.

Static crackled— Not radio static, but digital. The air shimmered, and a familiar, irritatingly smug voice oozed from hidden speakers.

“Ohhhh,” Vox purred. “Look what wandered into frame.”

The screen of the camera flickered, projecting a hologram of Vox’s face above it—grinning, eyes glowing, pixels dancing along the edges of his sharp smile.

Charlie groaned. “Vox. Of course it’s Vox.”

Alastor stared up at the projection, utterly still. His shadow writhed behind him, stretching towards the camera like a living thing straining at a leash.

Vox’s grin widened. “Aw, what’s the matter, Al? Cat got your tongue?”

He waited.

No reply.

Vox laughed, delighted. “Oh, this is beautiful.”

Charlie stepped forward. “Vox, if you did this, undo it. This isn’t funny.”

Vox feigned offense. “Charlie Morningstar! I’m wounded. You think I’d hurt your precious Radio Demon for fun?”

Alastor tilted his head slowly, eyes narrowing.

Vox leaned closer to the camera, his face filling the projection. “Okay, fine. Maybe a little for fun.”

Charlie crossed her arms. “Why? What do you even want?”

Vox’s eyes flicked back to Alastor. “I want a confession.”

The word seemed to hang in the air.

Alastor’s posture stiffened— Just a fraction. Barely noticeable. But Vox noticed.

“Oh yes,” Vox crooned. “See, Al, you’ve been so careful. So private. All that charm, all that control… and yet.” His smile turned razor-sharp. “Everyone’s got dead air somewhere.”

Alastor’s fingers tightened around his cane.

Charlie frowned. “Confession of what?”

“I’m not sure. All I know is that he’s hiding SOMETHING.“

Alastor knew; The humiliating, infuriating condition that locked his body against itself under observation. The loss of control. The vulnerability. The sheer indignity of it. A weakness so mundane it felt obscene compared to the horrors of Hell.

The camera zoomed in slightly, as if savoring the moment. “All you have to do,” Vox said smoothly, “Is tell me what you’ve been keeping from me.”

Charlie’s eyes widened. “Vox, that’s— No. That’s not okay.”

Vox waved her off. “Relax. I’m not asking for anything illegal. Just honesty. Authenticity. The audience loves that.”

Alastor’s grin trembled. His shadow recoiled, curling defensively.

“How can he even tell you if you’ve stolen his voice?” Charlie asked.

Vox rolled his eyes. “He can write it down, genius.” His voice softened, mockingly sympathetic. “How about it, Al? You get your voice back. Everything goes back to normal. All you have to do is let me know what you’re so adamant about hiding.”

Alastor shook his head once— Sharp, final. ‘No.’

Vox sighed. “Really? You’d rather stay mute forever than open up to me?”

Alastor stepped closer to the camera. He lifted one hand and drew his thumb across his throat in a clean, decisive motion. His eyes burned.

Vox froze for half a second. Then laughed, bright and electric. “There he is. Still scary, even without the sound.”

Charlie looked between them, anger rising. “This is blackmail, Vox.”

“Correction,” Vox replied. “This is content.”

Alastor straightened, smoothing his coat. His grin settled back into place— Sharp, unyielding, defiant. He would not confess. Not this. Not ever. Not to Vox.

Vox’s smile thinned. “Suit yourself, Al. Enjoy the silence.”

The hologram flickered.

The camera powered down.

And Alastor stood there, voiceless.

Charlie stepped closer to him, placing a gentle hand on his arm. “We’ll find another way,” she said firmly. “I promise.”

Alastor did not look at her right away. When he did, his eyes were steady. The message was clear. Vox had crossed a line.

And when Alastor finally got his voice back— One way or another—Hell would hear about it.

Charlie’s hand lingered on Alastor’s sleeve, grounding herself as much as him. She didn’t look at him right away. She already knew— Had known the moment Vox said confession and the way Alastor’s entire presence had tightened, like a trap snapping shut.

She exhaled slowly. “Okay,” she said at last. “We’re not doing that.”

Alastor glanced at her.

She met his gaze, steady and sincere. “This… Thing. It’s yours. And you don’t owe it to him. Or to anyone.”

Alastor’s smile softened just a fraction, the edge less vicious, more… Tired. He inclined his head once. A silent thank-you.

Charlie continued, her tone warming with resolve. “Besides, even if you did tell him? He wouldn’t stop there. He’d replay it. Remix it. Turn it into a joke, or worse.” She shuddered. “Nope. Not happening.”

Alastor’s shadow uncoiled slightly, easing out of its defensive knot.

Charlie began pacing again, thinking out loud. “So. Vox tied your voice to a condition. A confession, specifically. That means the curse is probably structured around consent or voluntary disclosure. Which is super gross, by the way.” She stopped and snapped her fingers. “But, there must be a loophole. We just have to find it.”

Alastor tapped his cane thoughtfully against the pavement, eyes narrowed in concentration.

Charlie stepped closer. “And Alastor? Just so you know.” She hesitated, then spoke gently. “I DO know what he’s trying to make you say.”

His grin froze.

She raised her hands quickly. “I’m not judging. At all. It’s just… Vox uses people’s vulnerabilities like weapons. And I wanted you to know you’re safe here. I won’t tell anyone. Ever.”

The silence stretched, but it was different now. Less oppressive. More intentional.

Alastor slowly placed a hand over his chest and bowed— Not the theatrical flourish he used on enemies, but something smaller. Real.

Charlie smiled back, eyes shining. “We’ll outsmart him. Okay? We always do.”

Alastor straightened. His eyes glowed faintly red. Vox wanted a confession, and he wasn’t getting one.

***

The effects of Alastor’s silence had rippled outwards.

Down the hall, the clack-clack-clack of obsessive scrubbing echoed as Niffty darted from surface to surface, rag in hand, eye wild with focus. She was humming to herself— Fast, frantic, off-key— As she polished the same already-spotless section of banister for the fourth time in ten minutes.

“Gotta keep clean!” she chirped, scrubbing harder. “Mr. Alastor likes it sparkly!”

The banister gleamed like a mirror.

Niffty didn’t stop.

Her contract burned faintly at the back of her mind, the familiar invisible pressure that commanded her forward. She was supposed to stop when Alastor said so. That was how it always worked.

But, he wasn’t here.

“Okay, okay, Niffty, calm down,” she muttered to herself, though her voice came out squeaky and high-pitched from frustration. “I can do this. Clean… clean… clean…”

But he hadn’t said anything before he and Charlie had left earlier.

The contract was… Unforgiving. It didn’t say “until Alastor grants a break, IF he’s able.” It said “until Alastor grants a break.” And unbeknownst to her, the overlord couldn’t grant anything right now. His voice was gone.

Niffty paused mid-scrub, blinking rapidly. “…Huh.”

She kept glancing towards the lobby, expecting to hear the usual radio-laced drawl calling out a new task, or at least a dismissive “That’ll be quite enough!” Instead, there was only the distant hum of the hotel and the scratch of her own rag. Her smile wobbled.

“…Mr. Alastor? Aren’t you back yet?”

No answer.

Her shoulders hunched, and she scrubbed again—faster now. “Okay! That’s fine! I can keep going! I don’t get tired! I’m great at this!”

The rag began to fray.

At the bar, Husk was having a much worse time. He slammed a glass down onto the counter with more force than necessary.

The bar was empty. No patrons. No noise. No Alastor leaning over the counter with that smug grin, making commentary Husk pretended not to listen to.

Husk took a long drink, ears flicking back irritably.

He glanced toward the doorway again. “You know, normally by now he’d be tellin’ me to take a break,” Husk said to the empty room. “Or at least insultin’ my posture.”

Silence.

His grip tightened on the glass. “Boss?”

Nothing.

Husk exhaled slowly, smoke curling from his mouth. He set the glass down and rubbed at his face with one clawed hand. “…Damn it.”

The contract was clear. ‘No breaks unless explicitly granted.’ Alastor’s voice was the key—spoken permission, sealed by intent.

No Alastor, no permission.

Which meant Husk was stuck behind the bar, until the Radio Demon got back.

“Great,” he growled. “Just great.”

Niffty screeched suddenly, almost toppling over a bucket of soapy water. “Husk! He… He isn’t even… Where is he?! I can’t— Ugh! I need a break! My arms are… My arms are literally noodles!” She flailed, smearing a streak of polish across the floor.

Husk snorted bitterly. “Noodles, huh? You think I’M any better?” He waved a paw at a stack of half-washed glasses teetering dangerously on the bar edge. “I’ve been cleaning these damn glasses since sunrise. And now Alastor’s just fucked off, so we’re… Trapped.“

The truth of the situation hit them both at once. Alastor’s orders had become a prison. Without his presence, they could not be released.

“Why didn’t he give us a break before he left?” Niffty asked. “He always does!”

“Maybe he forgot,” Husk said.

Niffty stopped mid-scrub, her tiny hands shaking. “What if… What if he doesn’t come back? We’ll… We’ll be working forever!” Her voice cracked at the thought, and Husk let out a bark of laughter mixed with despair.

“Forever,” Husk echoed, shaking his head. “Damn. That’d be… Uh… Fun?” He drained his glass in one frustrated gulp. “Nope. Not fun. Not fun at all. I… I think I actually hate this place now.”

Niffty clutched her duster. “Where’d he go? He’s being a bad boy…”

Husk grunted. “How should I know? I try not to keep tabs on the guy unless I have to.”

Every clink of Niffty’s duster against the marble, every scratch of Husk’s claw against the bar, was a reminder of their helplessness.

It didn’t take long for the situation to descend into a new kind of torture. Niffty darted around, duster in hand, cheeks flushed— Not just from exertion, but from an ever-growing pressure in her tiny bladder. She paused mid-swipe, froze, and blinked rapidly.

“Oh… no,” she squeaked, wobbling slightly. “Ohhh no, oh no, oh no!”

Husk, perched on a bar stool with a growing grimace, wasn’t faring any better. He shifted in his seat, crossed his legs, uncrossed them, shifted again. Each movement only made the pressure worse.

“Uh… Niffty,” Husk growled, his voice low and rough, “You feelin’… Y’know… Uncomfortable, too?”

Niffty’s duster nearly flew from her hands. “Hush! Yes! Oh yes! I… I think I might explode into lots of ribbons covered in piss!” She danced in place, scrubbing the floor with one hand while the other pressed between her legs. “I… I can’t… I can’t hold it much longer!”

Husk groaned, tail curling in frustration. “Tell me about it! I’ve been sittin’ here for… I dunno… hours?” He squirmed again, practically vibrating in place. “And the more I move, the more… Arghhh!“

They exchanged a glance of mutual suffering. There was no hope in each other’s eyes— Only shared torment.

Minutes stretched like hours. Niffty tried to clean with exaggerated small steps, balancing on the balls of her feet, while Husk kept rearranging bottles behind the bar— Anything to distract from the unbearable pressure in his lower half.

“Ohhhhhh… I can’t—Husk, I—uh—agh!” Niffty’s squeal broke briefly, muffled by a fist over her mouth. “You’re gonna have to get someone new to clean up my urine-flecked remains after I burst!”

Husk winced, crossing his legs tightly. “Don’t… Don’t look at me like that! You’re not the only one dyin’ here!”

Every tick of the clock seemed amplified, echoing through the lobby with cruel precision. Each second brought them closer to the edge of agony.

Finally, Husk muttered darkly, “I swear… If he doesn’t get here soon… I’m… I’m gonna… something…” His voice trailed off.

Niffty was wobbling precariously on a polished floor, duster forgotten. “I… I can’t… I can’t anymore!” Her eye welled up with tears. “I’M gonna make a mess! ME!”

Time slowed, and the hotel seemed to pulse with tension. Husk and Niffty were caught in a nightmare of their own bodies, slaves to a contract enforced by a Radio Demon who had vanished.

Niffty bounced on the balls of her feet, duster clenched tightly in one hand, the other pressed desperately against herself. Her wide eye darted from the gleaming floors to the bar. Her tiny body trembled from the searing, gnawing pressure that threatened to take over her entirely.

“Ohhhhhh… Ohhhhhh noooo… I… I can’t— Gah!” she shrieked, spinning in a circle like a frenzied ballerina, duster slamming against a vase that teetered dangerously on the edge of a table. It clattered to the ground with a crash, shattering a flower pot. She froze mid-scream, realizing the mess she’d made, then flailed again. “I… I… I’m—”

“Don’t— Don’t even start with me!” Husk barked from behind the bar, trying to pour a drink with shaking hands. Every bottle in his reach seemed like it might topple; Every movement made the pressure in his bladder throb painfully. His tail lashed back and forth like an angry whip. “I… I can’t even! I can’t! This is—ugh! This is—Fuck!”

Niffty’s squeal split the air again, high-pitched and frantic. She spun and tried to clean up the shattered vase, but her movements were erratic, uncoordinated. “I… I… I’m losing it! I… I can’t… I ca—Ugh!” She yelped and clutched between her legs, hopping up and down as she tried to keep cleaning.

Husk slammed his paw onto the counter, rattling glasses. “Yeah! Me too! I’m losing it! I—” He pressed one paw to his crotch, jaw tight, muttering through gritted teeth. “Where… Where IS that bastard?!”

Niffty staggered, arms flailing. “I… I can’t… I can’t hold it… I can’t!” Her voice cracked on the last word. She spun around the lobby, duster forgotten, bumping into a chair and sending it sliding across the floor. She fell to her knees, pressing her hands to her forehead in despair. “I’m… I’m going crazy! I… I— This isn’t GOOD pain at all!”

Husk growled and pressed his legs tightly together, shifting in his chair. “I… I can’t… I can’t even… Think straight!” He reached for a bottle of something— ANYTHING— To distract himself, but his shaking paws betrayed him, sending the bottle clattering to the floor.

Niffty’s tiny frame shuddered violently as tears pricked her eye. “It… It hurts… It… It really hurts!And NOT in the fun way!” Her high-pitched squeals ricocheted off the walls. She tried to clean the remains of Husk’s bottle, missed entirely, and ended up slipping slightly, arms flailing in a desperate, almost manic attempt to stay upright.

Husk groaned, throwing a towel over the counter in frustration. “Great… Yeah… Yeah… now we’re both going insane. Thanks, Alastor… Thanks a lot!” He pressed one paw to his forehead, tail thrashing violently, teeth gritted.

Minutes stretched unbearably. Niffty began muttering to herself, words incoherent and rapid, squeaks and squeals intermingling with frantic apologies to the floor and the furniture. Husk muttered in irritation and growing panic, pacing behind the bar, trying not to focus on the unbearable pressure threatening to overwhelm him.

Neither of them knew how long they could last. Niffty couldn’t hold still at all. She darted from one end of the lobby to the other, scrubbing, polishing, bouncing, hopping, and squeaking in a near-constant stream of manic energy. Her tiny arms waved wildly, duster clattering against the floor with alarming frequency, and every few seconds she would pause, clutching herself and spinning in circles, trying to escape the pressure building inside her.

“I… I… I can’t! I… I can’t hold it!” she squealed, hopping from one foot to the other so fast she almost blurred. Her voice cracked with each desperate word, high-pitched and frantic.

Husk was in the exact opposite state: Barely moving at all, his tail twitching angrily, legs crossed and pressed together in a pitiful attempt at restraint. His face was drawn, his jaw tight, and every minute that passed without Alastor returning made him grind his teeth harder. He muttered under his breath, his voice low and ragged.

“Fuck… This… This is… Painful,” he groaned, shifting in place and pressing one paw desperately to his crotch.

From the corner, Angel Dust slinked into the lobby, flicking his cigarette ash onto the floor. He noticed Husk’s misery instantly. “Oooohhh, kitty-cat… You look like you’re about to pop a fuse,” he drawled, smirking as he leaned closer. “You know… Reminds me of this one time with Valentino.”

Husk shot him a glare so sharp it could slice through steel. “Don’t… Don’t start,” he muttered through gritted teeth, trying not to move.

Angel tilted his head, smirk widening. “Nah, no startin’. Just… Uh… Comfortin’. Yeah. So, ol’ Val? Big shot, thinks he owns the world. Once… Wanted to do a special kinda shoot… Wouldn’t let me hit the bathroom for, like… Hours. Hours! And lemme tell ya, kitten, it was PAINFUL. Miserable. I was squirming, twitching, dancin’ on the spot like a… Like a… Well, like Niffty right now!” He gestured vaguely toward Niffty, who shrieked and twirled mid-clean.

Husk’s tail flicked angrily. “Yeah, GREAT. Real funny,” he growled, though a small shiver betrayed him. “I… I don’t… This… I can’t…”

Angel scooted closer, tail curling around Husk’s chair, voice taking on a slightly more soothing tone. “Hey, hey, listen. I know the feelin’. It’s… Hell. Absolute Hell. And lemme tell ya, we survive these things, yeah? Painful as it is, we survive. And you? You LOOK miserable, kitten. Poor thing. Ugh, I feel you.”

Husk pressed his paw to his forehead, muttering to himself. “Survive? Survive… Barely, Angel. Barely survive… I… I’m gonna— Ugh—”

Meanwhile, Niffty bounced past them, squealing and spinning like a top. “I… I… I… can’t! I… I—Ohhhh!” She clutched at the floor as if she could somehow glue herself to it. Her cleaning was becoming entirely nonsensical: She scrubbed the same spot repeatedly, then jumped to a completely clean section, then spun in circles, all while muttering frantic apologies to the furniture and floor.

Angel rested a hand on Husk’s shoulder, giving him a gentle squeeze. “Look… You and me? We’ve been through worse. Not THIS, sure, but… Hell’s… Got a way of testin’ you, kitten. And right now? Yeah, you’re miserable, but, hey, we’ll get through it. Probably.”

Husk let out a long, low groan, tail thrashing behind him. “I… I hope so, Angel. I really… I really hope so…”

Niffty let out another ear-piercing squeal and leaped onto the couch, bouncing like a deranged jack-in-the-box. Her tiny fists banged against her cheeks. “I… I… I can’t! I… I… I… Aghhhhhh!”

***

Charlie realized something was wrong as soon as she and Alastor got back to the hotel. She found Niffty still cleaning the already immaculate floor, jiggling like mad.

“Niffty?” Charlie asked gently. “You can stop now.”

Niffty looked up, eye glassy. “No, I can’t! Mr. Alastor didn’t say I could!”

Charlie’s stomach dropped.

She hurried to the bar next… And found Husk wriggling painfully in his seat.

“Let me guess,” Charlie said quietly. “You’re stuck.”

Husk growled. “Bingo. Where is that bastard?”

She swallowed hard and turned back towards where Alastor stood frozen in place, clearly listening— Clearly understanding.

Charlie approached him slowly. “Alastor… Niffty and Husk can’t take a break unless you say so. And you can’t do that without your voice.”

Something like genuine distress flickered across Alastor’s face. His silence wasn’t just hurting him, it was hurting NIFFTY!

…

…

… And Husk.

Alastor’s shadow writhed, stretching, as if trying to reach them through sheer will alone.

Charlie reached out and took his hand. “Hey. This isn’t your fault. Vox did this.”

Alastor looked at her, eyes dark and burning. He raised his free hand and mimed speaking, then pointed towards Husk and Niffty. He shook his head sharply.

“I know,” Charlie said.

***

Niffty pressed her legs together. Her rag hung limp in her hand, forgotten. She bounced once. Then again. “Okay!” she chirped a little too brightly. “Everything’s fine! Just fine! Totally fine! He’ll come tell me any second now!”

She glanced to where Alastor stood in quiet conversation with Charlie, communicating in gestures and sharp looks. Her smile wavered.

“…What’s he doing?” she muttered to herself.

The pressure in her mind still pulled her towards her assigned task. Clean. Wait. Obey. The contract didn’t care about discomfort. It only cared about permission.

And permission had always come in the same form; Spoken.

Niffty bounced again, this time letting out a small squeak. She slapped both hands over her mouth, eye wide.

“Nope! Not squeakin’. Very unprofessional,” she scolded herself, then whispered, “But wow I really, really have to go.”

She darted a look towards the nearest bathroom door. It was right there. So close.

Her foot lifted… And immediately snapped back to the floor as the contract tugged at her like an invisible leash.

Niffty whimpered softly.

***

Alastor noticed the full extent of the situation. He took in Husk’s posture, the rigid tension. He saw Niffty’s bouncing and clutching. Recognition hit him instantly.

With as… Sensitive as the topic of urination was for him, he wasn’t at all like certain OTHER overlords who would force their contracts to hold their natural needs to the point of pain, if only because seeing them squirm would be too suggestive to his OWN bladder. No, he gave his pets breaks at regular intervals.

But, he couldn’t now…

His shadow twisted violently at his feet, agitated, furious.

Charlie followed his line of sight. Her expression fell when she saw how much worse off Niffty was getting. “Oh no. Niffty…”

Alastor stepped forward at once, raising his hands, gesturing frantically; ‘Go relieve yourselves,’ but the motions were nonsensical without words to accompany them. He mimed speaking, pressed a hand to his throat, then pointed desperately.

“The fuck is wrong with you?!” Husk asked.

“Vox took his voice,” Charlie explained. “He can’t talk…”

Husk’s eyes widened as the news sank in. Alastor wasn’t making him wait to torture him, Alastor just literally COULDN’T give him a break.

“WHAT?!” Niffty shrieked.

Charlie hurried, kneeling in front of Niffty to bring herself to her level. “Hey. Sweetie. You okay?”

Niffty shook her head. “Nope! Holding something very important!”

Charlie winced sympathetically. “Right. Bathroom important.”

Niffty’s eye watered just a little. “Mr. Alastor always lets me have breaks for this,” she whispered. “He always does.”

Alastor stood frozen behind Charlie, hands trembling at his sides. This was his failure. His poor Niffty!

…

…

… And Husk.

Alastor slammed his cane against the floor. The crack echoed like a gunshot.

Everyone jumped.

The hunt began. Alastor floated from room to room, searching for something—A tool, a means, a method to communicate without his radio-laced tones. He rifled through drawers, overturned chairs, and flicked through stacks of papers with a manic intensity, desperate for a blank sheet and something to write with.

It wasn’t until he entered Angel Dust’s room— A chaotic mess of sequins, empty liquor bottles, and stray feathers— That he saw it: A scrap of paper and a pencil tucked into the corner of a cluttered nightstand. His crimson eyes gleamed. A simple thing, really, but effective. With that, he could write. He could command. He could— FINALLY— Grant the break.

‘Ah… Perfect,’ he thought silently to himself,

He scribbled quickly on the paper, each stroke precise, almost artistic. “Break. Now.” The words themselves seemed to hum with authority, the jagged edges of the letters vibrating faintly with static. He held the paper aloft, rushing back into the lobby with a flourish, eyes gleaming with satisfaction.

He shoved the paper at Niffty.

Niffty froze mid-spin, her wide eye locking onto the paper. Her cheeks flushed and her tiny hands twitched. “…He… He…?” she squeaked, unsure if it was real.

Alastor held the paper up to Husk.

Husk squinted at the letters, muttering under his breath. “Huh… He’s… Actually…?” His tail twitched less violently, the weight of near-catastrophic bladder pressure slightly mitigated by the promise of imminent release.

Niffty gasped, clapping her tiny hands over her mouth. “Yay!” She didn’t even wait for Husk, bolting towards the nearest restroom, squealing with relief and manic energy all at once. Husk followed, tail flicking, muttering curses of equal parts frustration and relief as he shuffled after her.

“I… I… I can’t… Wait a second longer!” Niffty squeaked. Her legs moved so fast she seemed almost to blur.

Husk, tail lashing, let out a low, guttural groan. “Yeah… Yeah, me too… Can’t… Can’t hold it…!”His paw fumbled with his zipper, every step sending jolts of anticipation— Relief or disaster, he didn’t know which— Through his already frazzled nerves.

The two of them barreled down the hallway, a chaotic, desperate blur of motion and whimpers. Niffty’s tiny squeals echoed off the walls, Husk’s grumbling curses barely audible over her frantic cries. Both were on the verge of complete panic—But finally, finally, they reached the bathroom.

Niffty yanked the door open. She practically flung herself into a stall, tearing her skirt up and ripping down her panties. She barely made contact with the seat before her body gave in to the extreme relief it had been denying all day. She moaned with delight and pure bliss, tiny fists gripping the edge of the seat, her face a mix of red, sweat, and unimaginable satisfaction.

Meanwhile, Husk fumbled with the door to another stall, paw at his crotch, tail twitching uncontrollably. He muttered a string of low curses under his breath, not out of anger but from sheer desperation. “Come on… Come on…!” He finally got the door shut behind him, fumbling with the zipper just in time. The moment he let go, the tension in his body dissolved into pure, utter relief, a long, low sigh escaping him as if he’d been holding every drop of water in the universe inside of his abdomen for hours.

For both of them, the world seemed to slow. Their frantic, bouncing, trembling, near-mad energy collapsed into a blissful, shivering calm. Niffty’s squeaks softened into small, happy whimpers. Husk leaned against the wall, eyes closed, tail curling loosely around his legs. The torment of the past hours— The unbearable desperation, the unyielding contract, Alastor’s absence— Slipped just enough to let them catch their breath.

***

The hotel settled into an uneasy calm once Niffty and Husk emerged from the bathroom, looking visibly lighter.

Charlie watched them leave, then turned back to Alastor. “Okay,” she said, clapping her hands softly, decision made. “We need someone who understands curses AND social leverage magic. Someone old-school. Someone who hates Vox almost as much as you do.”

Alastor already knew where this was going.

She smiled. “Rosie.”

His eyes flickered with reluctant agreement. Rosie knew contracts. She knew etiquette-bound magic, favors wrapped in politeness, secrets hidden behind smiles. If anyone could untangle a curse built on confession and humiliation, it would be her.

Charlie gestured toward the door. “Let’s go.”

Alastor stepped forward… and immediately stiffened.

The movement sent him an unpleasant reminder of the coffee he’d had that morning, sharp and insistent. He halted for half a second, posture locking as if bracing against an internal jolt.

His bladder protested, the sensation made worse by stress, by silence, by the length of the morning. Normally, he would have excused himself with effortless charm, vanished for a moment, returned unbothered and pristine.

Normally, he could say something to Charlie. ‘I just need to… Freshen up a moment before we leave.’

Now… He straightened abruptly, smoothing his coat with more force than necessary. His smile widened, rigid and flawless, and he gestured grandly for Charlie to lead the way.

Charlie didn’t notice. “Great! Rosie’s gonna love this,” she said as they headed out. “Well—‘Love’ isn’t right. But she’ll help.”

Alastor followed, cane tapping against the pavement in a precise rhythm that masked the tension in his steps. Every instinct screamed at him to stop, to signal, to do something— But, the very idea of trying to explain THAT with gestures alone made his stomach twist with mortification.

No.

Absolutely not.

He would endure.

The walk was longer than he remembered.

Or perhaps every step simply made him more aware of his body in a way he despised. He adjusted his stride subtly, posture impeccable, hands folded behind his back to keep from betraying discomfort. His shadow, traitorous thing, flickered once as if sensing his unease— But, he forced it still with a sharp glare.

Charlie chatted as they went, filling the silence. “Rosie’s dealt with curses tied to etiquette before. Like, rules-based magic. Confession magic is very… Her wheelhouse.”

Alastor nodded politely, jaw tight.

The pressure grew, dull but persistent. He clenched his teeth behind his grin and focused on anything else— The cracks in the sidewalk, the smell of brimstone and roses in the air, the distant sound of traffic.

Anything but the very mortal, very undignified problem he was carrying with him.

They reached Rosie’s place at last, the familiar warmth of Cannibal Town curling around them. The building loomed ahead, welcoming and ominous all at once.

Charlie turned toward him, cheerful. “See? Not too bad of a walk!”

Alastor inclined his head. ‘Liar,’ his body replied.

Rosie’s door swung open before they could knock.

“Well now,” Rosie said, eyes lighting up as she took them in. “To what do I owe the pleasure?”She paused. Her gaze sharpened, locking onto Alastor. “My dear,” she said slowly, “What happened to your static?”

Alastor bowed deeply, cane pressed to his chest, smile razor-bright and perfectly controlled. He did not trust himself to do anything else.

Charlie stepped in quickly. “Hi, Rosie! We need your help. Alastor’s been cursed…”

As they crossed the threshold, Alastor followed with measured grace, every ounce of his will focused on maintaining composure.

He could handle curses.

He could handle Vox.

He could handle silence.

But as the door closed behind them, one thought echoed mercilessly in his mind: ‘Please let this not take long.’

Because he really, REALLY needed to unload his poor bladder.

Rosie ushered them into her parlor with practiced warmth, already pouring tea as if nothing were amiss— Bone-cups, neatly arranged plates, the faint scent of roses barely masking iron and smoke.

“Now,” she said, settling gracefully into her chair, folding her hands, “Tell Auntie Rosie everything.”

Charlie launched into it at once— Vox, the camera, the confession clause, the way Alastor’s voice had been cleanly severed. Rosie listened intently, eyes sharp, nodding at the right moments.

Alastor stood off to the side, cane planted firmly before him. At first, he was fine. Then the minutes stretched. And stretched.

Rosie leaned forward. “Confession-based curses are dreadfully intimate things. They’re fueled by shame. Vox is leveraging humiliation as a power source.”

Charlie groaned. “That’s exactly what I was afraid of.”

Alastor shifted his weight— Subtly, carefully. The movement sent a spike of discomfort through him, sharp enough that his breath hitched before he could stop it. His grip tightened around his cane, knuckles whitening.

Normally— NORMALLY— This would be trivial here.

Rosie’s bathroom was just down the hall. He had been there countless times.

But NOW…

He couldn’t excuse himself. Couldn’t ask. Couldn’t even cough audibly or manipulate his static to politely draw attention.

So, he could not go.

Alastor was nothing if not a creature of rules. Of manners. Of social contracts just as binding as magical ones. To simply leave a conversation in someone else’s home without a word? To wander their halls unannounced?

Unthinkable.

His posture stiffened as another wave of urgency hit, more insistent now. He pressed his thighs together just a fraction, the movement hidden by his long coat, and fixed his gaze on the far wall.

‘Endure.’

Rosie was saying, “If Vox tied the curse to a confession, then redirection might break it.”

Charlie leaned forward eagerly.

“But we must be delicate,” Rosie said. ‘If the curse senses coercion or avoidance, it could retaliate.”

Alastor’s jaw clenched. Retaliation sounded bad. He adjusted his stance again, slower this time, breath shallow. His shadow flickered, betraying his tension, but Rosie and Charlie were too deep in conversation to notice.

Charlie nodded. “So we need a way for Alastor to reclaim his voice without actually— Well— Confessing to Vox.”

“Yes,” Rosie said thoughtfully.

The pressure became impossible to ignore.

Alastor’s fingers twitched. He considered— Briefly— Trying to mime something to Rosie. But the very idea of gesturing THAT need made his face burn beneath his fixed smile. No. Absolutely not. He would rather be damned twice over.

He straightened his back, forcing composure through sheer will. He had endured torture. He had endured binding circles and starvation curses and worse.

This was… This was merely undignified.

Rosie rose suddenly, pacing. “I may have an idea…”

Alastor flinched.

Another jolt of desperation surged, sharp enough that his knee buckled a hair’s breadth before he caught himself. His cane tapped the floor a little harder than intended.

‘Too loud.’

Neither of them looked his way.

Charlie was saying, “If we could trick the curse into thinking the condition was fulfilled—”

Rosie snapped her fingers. “A proxy confession!”

“Yes!” Charlie beamed.

Alastor squeezed his eyes shut for half a second, breathing carefully through his nose. Every instinct screamed at him to flee— To abandon etiquette, pride, everything— But, he remained rooted in place. Because that was who he was. Because silence was already stripping enough from him.

He would not let desperation take the rest.

So he stood there, impeccably dressed, impeccably smiling, legs locked and will burning, while the conversation carried on just a little too long.

And neither Rosie nor Charlie noticed a thing.

Rosie stopped pacing. “The curse might be fulfilled if Alastor confesses. Period. Not necessarily to Vox.”

The room seemed to still.

“If Vox structured this as a confession curse,” Rosie continued, tapping a finger against her chin, “Then the magic may not actually care who hears the truth. Vox only assumes he’s the necessary audience because that’s what would humiliate Alastor most.”

Charlie’s eyes widened. “So… If Alastor tells anyone new about his… ‘Problem’?

“Anyone,” Rosie confirmed, “So long as the confession is sincere and acknowledged.”

Charlie turned toward Alastor, hope lighting her face. “Alastor—that could work!”

Alastor’s breath caught, but the relief he might have felt was drowned beneath a far more immediate, physical agony. The pressure had crossed a threshold now, no longer a dull insistence but a sharp, aching pain that made his vision blur at the edges if he focused on it too long.

He shifted again— Too quickly this time— And had to grip the back of a chair to steady himself. His cane rattled softly against the floor.

Rosie frowned. “My dear, are you quite—”

“I think he’s just overwhelmed,” Charlie said quickly, misreading the situation entirely. “This is a lot.”

Alastor straightened at once, schooling his expression back into careful composure. He forced his shoulders back, forced his grin into place.

Yes. Overwhelmed. That was a far preferable assumption.

Inside his head, thoughts tangled and slipped, each one disrupted by another spike of urgency that made it hard to breathe, let alone think.

Anyone.

Anyone he trusted.

The list was… Painfully short. And everyone ON it ALREADY knew about his… Stage-fright…

His vision swam for a moment as another wave of discomfort hit, stronger than before. He pressed his knees together under his coat, every muscle taut, breath shallow and controlled. The pain was becoming distracting— Debilitating.

Focus.

Think.

Then…

… Zestial.

The name surfaced.

Old. Powerful. Polite to a fault. A creature of etiquette and restraint, bound by ancient courtesy as deeply as Alastor himself. Zestial would not mock him. Would not broadcast it. Would not even react improperly.

More importantly, Zestial would understand the weight of confession. The cost of it.

Alastor swallowed hard. Yes. If there was anyone in Hell he could endure saying it to, it would be Zestial.

His shadow stirred, sensing his decision.

Charlie was watching him now. “Alastor? Do you… Do you know who you’d tell?”

He met her gaze. He lifted his cane and traced a careful Z in the air.

Rosie’s brows rose. “Zestial,” she said approvingly. “An excellent choice. Discreet. Honorable.”

Charlie smiled. “That’s perfect! We can go to him right now.”

Alastor nodded. Right now was good. Right now was very, very good. The sooner he could speak, the sooner he could excuse himself for some RELIEF.

He turned towards the door with perhaps a touch too much urgency, posture still impeccable but movements tighter than before. Every step he took was an act of sheer will, pain flaring brighter with each second.

‘Just a little longer. Just hold on, Alastor.’

***

Rosie led the way with measured steps, her long skirt swishing just above the cracked pavement. Charlie kept pace beside her, chattering softly about magical technicalities and the particular dangers of Vox’s methods, while Alastor trailed behind, cane tapping in rapid, precise rhythm that betrayed far more than his impeccable posture suggested.

By the time they arrived at Zestial’s residence— A stately, almost cathedral-like building perched precariously atop a jagged hill— The pain in Alastor’s bladder had reached an excruciating crescendo. His legs were trembling slightly now with every step, knees pressed tightly together under his coat, hands gripping his cane like it was the only thing holding his existence together. His face remained perfectly composed, but inside, the sensation was unbearable.

Charlie and Rosie were already speaking with Zestial when Alastor finally entered the grand hall. The faint smell of incense and parchment filled the space. Zestial, tall and dignified as ever, gave a small, polite nod of acknowledgment, then leaned forward in interest.

“Alastor,” Charlie began, “Vox has cursed him. His voice is gone, and the curse is tied to a confession. The magical rules are… Unusual, but we think it can be lifted if Alastor confesses to anyone.”

Zestial’s brows furrowed. “Confession? Indeed… A delicate matter. And thou art suggesting he is to speak this truth to me?”

Charlie shook her head. “Not speak. He can’t. He’ll have to write it down. You just need to read it.“

Alastor’s shadow quivered at his feet as he approached the writing desk. The anticipation of relief made him shiver. But, the bladder pain was overwhelming now, radiating through his lower abdomen with every heartbeat. He clenched his thighs so tightly it was almost impossible to take another step.

Rosie glanced at him. “We’ll need paper and quill, of course. Have you ever used a quill before, pet? I can assist…”

Alastor HAD used a quill before, but he was shaking so hard that it probably wasn’t going to look like it. He forced his hand to remain steady as he accepted the quill. His other hand gripped the edge of the desk, knuckles white.

Charlie knelt beside him, trying to offer encouragement. “You’re okay, Alastor. Just… Take it slow. Zestial’s going to read it, and then your voice comes back.“

The thought of relief was too much to dwell on openly. Alastor focused on the paper, forcing himself to ignore the excruciating pressure filling up his lower body. Every movement, every inhalation, was a test of will.

He began writing, each letter deliberate, immaculate, the elegant strokes betraying none of the torment he was enduring. The words were clinical, precise, stripped of dramatics— His confession rendered in black ink across the page.

‘I suffer from a rather embarrassing and strange affliction. A medical condition most commonly referred to as’

He stopped, hand shaking more as he tried to will it to write the next words down. The hesitation lasted another several seconds, before a brutal throb from his midsection spurred him on.

‘Shy-bladder syndrome.’

Meanwhile, Zestial leaned in to examine the writing, tilting his head thoughtfully. “… Is that all, Alastor?”

‘Is that all…’

As if the Radio Demon’s hidden weakness were a trifling, insignificant thing!

Alastor nodded.

“Very well… This should suffice for the magical requirements. It is sincere, and the integrity of the confession is evident.”

Alastor’s shadow quivered violently as he set the quill down, hands trembling. Relief would come any moment now. But, the remaining seconds stretched endlessly.

Charlie’s eyes flicked between Alastor and Zestial. “You may need to read it aloud,” she urged gently.

Zestial’s long fingers traced the lines of the confession, reading it softly enough for only Alastor to hear. As the words left Zestial’s lips, a low hum of energy stirred in the room; The sound of magic shifting, breaking, and realigning.

Alastor’s knees almost buckled with the combined relief of the curse unraveling and the unbearable pressure finally nearing its end. His shadow surged, wrapping around him protectively, as though acknowledging the personal victory he had achieved.

When the last words were read, the hum stopped. Alastor exhaled sharply, and for the first time in hours, he was free.

He barely managed to nod at Zestial, thanking him with a look more eloquent than words could manage. His shadow recoiled slightly, and a warmth spread through him— His static returning at last.

And, it was extremely loud. Fizzing, popping, exploding with his urgent need and the tension knocking around inside his body.

He took a deep breath. He could feel his voice returning— Tiny tremors of it, rasping at the back of his throat— But, nerves and sheer embarrassment made it almost impossible to get the words out.

“…I— I…” he began, his tone cracking, too high-pitched for his liking. He cleared his throat once, twice, trying to force his usual smooth, radio-laced cadence back into place.

Charlie, noticing his struggle, smiled encouragingly. “Yes, Alastor?”

He pressed his hand to his throat, muttering under his breath, the words nearly lost: “…I need to…” Then, with a shaky but audible voice: “May I… Please use the restroom?” The sentence, so simple, felt monumental. Relief surged as he finally admitted it aloud.

Charlie’s smile widened.

“Of course,” Zestial said, stepping aside.

“Go on, hurry!” Charlie said.

Alastor bowed slightly— Stiff, formal, still impeccably mannered— And shuffled towards the door, legs trembling with a mix of relief and lingering desperation.

As soon as he was behind the door with his zipper down, the dam broke. The hours of restraint, of holding himself together for propriety’s sake, vanished instantly. Every ounce of discomfort drained away, leaving him leaning back against the cool wall with a long, shuddering exhale. His voice, finally fully returned, trembled slightly when he muttered to himself.“Never… Again… Shall I endure such… Indignity…”

Even in the privacy of the restroom, the faint trace of pride, formality, and old-world flair lingered in every word. Alastor grinned to himself, shadow flickering in quiet amusement, finally free— Both of Vox’s curse and of the torment that had nearly broken him.

Alastor’s voice, once stolen, now hummed triumphantly through the air; A sound sharp, warm, and perfectly terrifying once more.

And Vox still hadn’t uncovered his secret.

*018*2026-06-01 13:46

Alastor had been in many unpleasant situations in his long, winding existence, but THIS was new. He had been captured before, certainly. But never— Not once— Had he been tied to a reinforced steel chair in the center of Vox’s studio and forced to endure a situation so mortifying it made even him question every decision that had led him here.

They hadn’t exactly been… Good ones. His imprisonment was only temporary, he knew that, just long enough for him to use Vox to break free of Rosie’s leash.

The truth, which he was still struggling to fully come to grips with, was that his plan hadn’t worked out the way he’d been imagining it. The idea was to make ROSIE break her end of their deal, which was to make Alastor the strongest sinner in Hell. If that title went to someone else, then their contract would be over, and Alastor would be free to do as he pleased.

Alastor had honestly believed wholeheartedly that simply losing a FIGHT to Vox would be enough. He’d thought it would be simple! Vox wouldn’t pass up the opportunity to rough Alastor up, and Alastor would put up JUST enough of a fight to make it look like he was really trying. Then, when Vox ‘won’, Alastor and Rosie’s deal would be over.

But, then Niffty and Husk just HAD to show up, and the situation was no longer under Alastor’s control to the extent he needed it to be. Niffty could have been— Well, of course, Alastor didn’t really care if she was injured, but she WAS the only person capable of keeping the cockroaches in the hotel from forming an army…

So, he’d struck a new deal with Vox, which he’d HOPED would have the same effect. Vox could have HIM as his prisoner if he let Niffty and Husk go. Alastor assumed, once again, that this ‘defeat’ would be enough to break Rosie’s deal.

It wasn’t…

One more miscalculation. One more loss of control.

He had, of course, come up with a failsafe in case something like this occurred; If Vox put his hands on Charlie, then Alastor would no longer be his prisoner. And, Vox had always had such a terrible habit of grabbing people, it was only a matter of time. And, while Alastor waited for Vox to slip up, he could keep trying to find a way to earn Vox the label of ‘most powerful sinner’.

But, then there was Alastor’s FINAL miscalculation. He had not correctly predicted HOW Vox would choose to keep him imprisoned. He hadn’t thought he’d be tied down for quite THIS long. He’d assumed that, once they’d gotten to the tower, his bonds would be removed and he’d be able to leave the chair, not permitted to leave the tower, but free to wander it.

But, no. Vox apparently sought to keep him completely restrained indefinitely.

And, that was a problem.

The reason for his torment was not torture, nor interrogation, nor some sinister technological procedure, no.

It was just his bladder.

And it was winning.

He had relieved himself directly before putting this plan into motion, he hadn’t been SO foolish that he’d allowed such a crucial step to slip his mind. But, that had been AGES ago now. And his middle was absolutely throbbing. Even refusing Vox’s offer of a drink had failed to stave off this inevitable crisis for too long.

How in the FUCK was he going to survive this without his nightmares becoming a reality?

The studio lights hummed above him, bright and clinical. The sharp scent of overheated wires, and disinfectant filled the room. Cables snaked across the floor like black vines, and then there was Vox’s grinning face, watching Alastor with relentless amusement.

The bindings were excessive, even by Vox’s melodramatic standards; Thick industrial straps tightened around Alastor’s wrists, crossing his chest, and pinning his ankles to the chair legs. They were enchanted, too. He had already tested them with every trick he could muster without blowing up half the building.

Not too long ago, Vox had electrocuted him. It was that moment, when his body was placed under so much strain, muscles convulsing out of control, that he’d realized just how severe his need for release truly was.

Had he been anyone else, the shocks would have coaxed out a leak, if not a complete accident. But, he wasn’t anyone else. He was Alastor, and Alastor—

“Well, Alastor,” Vox purred. “You’re remarkably quiet for someone who usually won’t shut the fuck up.”

Alastor’s smile remained perfectly carved onto his face, but a faint twitch at its corner betrayed him. “Merely conserving energy,” he replied, voice steady, though he subtly shifted in his seat. The pressure in his lower abdomen throbbed relentlessly, assuring him that he could not avoid his inevitable, awful fate for too much longer. “One never knows when one might require it.”

Vox’s circuitry-bright eyes narrowed. He knew something was off. “Ohhh? And here I thought you were plotting your grand escape. You don’t seem very… Focused.”

Alastor’s eye twitched again, his bladder’s walls contracting. He could feel a sort of fizzing sensation near his opening. If he could press his thighs together, he would. But the bindings stopped him. Each time he tried to adjust himself to a more comfortable position, only to fail, his abdomen cramped.

The cramps were a warning. Worse yet, they were a promise. A promise that, eventually, he was going to have to do something… Very unpleasant. Something painful. Something that would cut through him like the sharpest of blades.

There was no way around it, from the looks of things.

Was he going to be tied down for the entire duration of his imprisonment?! Until Vox inevitably laid a hand on Charlie and broke the deal he had made?! That was going to take at least a few days. No way could Alastor wait that long. Especially if he couldn’t even fidget properly.

No.

He was going to have to notify Vox of his need for a bathroom break. And, as if that weren’t bad enough, he was going to have to HOPE Vox would grant him one. Alastor was uncertain if he even would or not. The chance to watch Alastor wriggle and writhe, to perhaps even hear him beg (Fat chance!) Might be too enticing for him to pass it up.

Then again, Vox did seem to prefer keeping his space clean…

At least for now, Alastor could still hold out. He could contain the liquid sloshing up against his floodgates. Maybe he wouldn’t even actually have to ask… Maybe he wouldn’t have to say those words to Vox, maybe…

Oh, but it would also be terrible for Vox to NOTICE his need on his own, it would mean Alastor had failed to keep his composure, that his attempts to fidget in his seat hadn’t been as subtle as he’d allowed himself to believe.

Alastor gritted his teeth. The best case scenario, the one he desperately hoped for, was that he’d be able to conceal his urgency for the next… Few hours or so. However long it took for Vox to think, on his own ‘It’s been quite a while, I wonder if Alastor might like to relieve himself now?’ And just… Ask.

Then, all Alastor would have to do is say ‘yes’. He wouldn’t have to reveal how serious the urge was. He wouldn’t have to bring it up on his own. He wouldn’t have to convince Vox to let him go. He wouldn’t have to risk Vox discovering… Anything.

He just had to hold himself still until that— Hopefully— Happened. “I assure you, I am perfectly—” He paused as his body gave a small, involuntary jolt. His legs attempted to cross again, the bindings stopping them in their tracks, and his opening burning as the expected reinforcement failed to arrive. “Perfectly fine.”

Vox’s grin widened, pixels crackling with glee. “Ohhhh, this is beautiful,” he laughed, leaning back. “The Radio Demon, squirming in his chair like a toddler on a road trip. Oh no!” He cooed sarcastically. “Did I make you miss your scheduled bathroom break?”

Alastor inhaled sharply through his nose. So much for his plan… It HAD been a bit of a long-shot, he could admit that, but he still wished he’d managed to keep this hidden for a LITTLE while longer. “No,” he said primly. “I simply did not anticipate being tied up long enough for… Nature’s requirements to become inconvenient.”

“‘Inconvenient,’ he says,” Vox snorted. “Just admit it, Al— You can’t wait much longer. You’re about to drench that fancy, little suit of yours. How humiliating. For you. This is the best day of my life.”

Another uncomfortable spike of pressure forced Alastor to clench everything humanly and inhumanly possible. He refused to let his expression crack, but his voice wavered just slightly when he spoke again. “Vox, my dear fellow.”

“Oh, this is already good,” Vox said, wriggling his brows. “That tone? That’s the ‘I’m about to beg’ tone.”

Alastor’s left ear jerked downwards, flattening out against his head. “I am NOT begging,” he said crisply.

“Not begging YET,” Vox chimed. “Let’s see how long it takes… Would you like me to play you some ambient noise? A nice, relaxing rainstorm might loosen those lips of yours, get you groveling…”

Alastor ignored him. It wasn’t easy, moving past Vox’s bait rarely ever was, but he was used to facing difficult tasks with a smooth air of confidence— That always got under Vox’s circuits much faster than any other reaction possibly could. “I am making a reasonable request,” he said. “I require you to untie me. Briefly,” he added hastily.

“For what?” Vox asked, voice syrupy sweet with poisonous mischief. “You need to tell me WHY. Otherwise, how do I know you’re not plotting something. So, what is it? Do you have to change the radio batteries? Tighten a dial? Tune yourself to a different station?”

Alastor closed his eyes. “I believe we’ve established that you are WELL aware of what I need.”

“Mm… No. I don’t think I am. Enlighten me.”

“I need,” Alastor said with painfully measured patience, “To visit the restroom.” His bladder strained at the words, urging him to elaborate, to emphasize that this need was not one that could be brushed aside, to utter a shameful term like ‘Emergency’.

He did his best to ignore IT, just as he had Vox’s earlier taunt.

There was a full second of silence.

Then Vox shrieked with laughter so loud it overloaded two of his screens, causing them to glitch into static. “Oh my GOD,” he howled, doubling over. “This is unbelievable! You want ME to untie you so you can go pee?! HA!”

“I do not ‘want’ anything from you,” Alastor snapped, his composure fracturing for the first time since he’d been tied down. He had to salvage this… Had to turn it around. “I am simply attempting to avoid soiling your rather expensive-looking flooring. Not to mention shorting out all of your ridiculous, little gadgets.”

Vox paused. He looked down at the pristine floor and all of the delicate wires decorating it. He looked back at Alastor. “…You wouldn’t.”

Alastor held his smile and said nothing. It was a bluff, VERY much so. As, regardless of how unavoidable it may have seemed, he doubted his ability to follow through on the threat. Oh, if left in his restraints, his bladder WOULD eventually fill to the point that an involuntary release SHOULD occur. But… ‘Should’ and ‘Would’ were two distinctly different things.

There had been a few instances, back when he was alive, where Alastor found himself so maddeningly desperate that he feared he was on the cusp of an… ‘Accident’. There had even been a couple of similar ordeals here in Hell.

But, regardless of how much it FELT like he was about to soak himself, his stage-fright always managed to both save and doom him. Save him, because his bladder’s inability to empty itself would thwart the ultimate humiliation of an accident. And doom him, because rather than letting out the surplus of fluid that had become too much for his body to contain, he’d be met with a clawing, burning hot volt of agony that careened all through his torso. And, if he was particularly unlucky, a mild infection in the days that followed.

Of course, Vox had no way of knowing that Alastor couldn’t make good on his threat, and that was what the Radio Demon was counting on.

Vox stared.

Alastor stared back.

A bead of sweat rolled down the side of Vox’s screen.

Alastor’s smile widened a fraction, both to better convince Vox that he was serious, and out of relief that this seemed to be working.

“…Okay, nope,” Vox finally muttered. “No way am I explaining to my cleaning crew why the floor smells like deer piss.”

Another pulse from Alastor’s bladder made him jerk again. Not good enough. His voice sharpened, working to keep all uncertainty out of his tone. Vox grinned again, smug as a cat on a warm server tower. “There you go,” he said, arms folded. “Now, let’s run along so you can do your business, and do try not to die of embarrassment on the way.”

Alastor straightened, smoothing his suit and adjusting his bowtie with as much dignity as one could muster while fighting increasingly urgent biological demands in front of their nemesis. “I assure you,” he replied, smiling thinly, “I will manage.”

‘Vox cannot know. He cannot EVER know.’

“And you expect me to let you go wandering around by yourself. Plotting something. Attempting escape.”

“I assure you, my only intentions at the moment involve relief,” Alastor said, static fizzing once again. The more insistent he got about this, the more Vox was going to wonder, the more likely he was to—

THAT’S what you’ve been hiding?! You’re pee-shy?!”

“Or can you not even manage that?”

Alastor inhaled sharply. “That,” he said, voice tight, “Is not the correct clinical term.”

“Oh my GOD,” Vox wheezed, bending over as laughter crackled through the speakers. “This is priceless! THAT’S why you refused to use the bathroom the other week! It’s why I’ve never seen you go! That’s why you hid in a closet to piss when I had my camera following you!”

“Hid in a closet to— What?!” Alastor said, static popping off of him so hard that it burned his skin. “I haven’t the foggiest idea what you’re talking about!”

“Oh, bullshit! The Radio Demon, scourge of the damned, can’t go if someone’s listening! You’ve got stage fright!”

“NO WAY,” Vox cackled, cutting him off. “You’re telling me you could blast half the underworld into static but you can’t handle the sound of someone else existing in the same vicinity as your… Plumbing?!”

“It’s ADORABLE,” Vox corrected.

“It is—” Alastor stopped, lips pressed into a tight line. The urge in his abdomen throbbed again, cruel and demanding.

Vox’s eyes suddenly narrowed. Flashes of their past came to him in rapid fire. Alastor wriggling. Alastor rushing to get home. Alastor looking like he was in terrible pain, as Vox fussed over him and tried to get him to open up and say what was hurting him so bad. “If you would simply be so courteous as to leave me alone,” Alastor tried, “I could—”

“Oh no,” Vox sang. “If I do that, how am I supposed to know you’re not plotting escape?”

Vox suddenly leaned so close he was practically pressed against Alastor.

“Say it.”

Alastor blinked, stepping back, twitching at the sensation of their bodies brushing. “Say what?”

Alastor’s smile quivered with barely concealed pure, homicidal rage. “That will not be happening.”

“Absolutely not.”

“You want to go to the bathroom, don’t you?”

Alastor’s eye gave one violent, traitorous twitch.“Vox.”
